Restrictions, L-parameters, and local coefficients for genuine representations
Fan Gao, Freydoon Shahidi, and Dani Szpruch

Abstract
. We consider the restriction and induction of representations between a covering group and its derived subgroup, both on the representation-theoretic side and the L-parameter side. In particular, restriction of a genuine principal series is analyzed in detail. We also discuss a metaplectic tensor product construction for covers of the symplectic similitudes groups, and remark on the generality of such a construction for other groups. Furthermore, working with an arbitrary irreducible constituent of a unitary unramified principal series, we prove a multiplicity formula for its restriction to the derived subgroup in terms of three associated R-groups. Later in the paper, we study an unramified L-packet on how the parametrization of elements inside such a packet varies along with different choices of hyperspecial maximal compact subgroups and their splittings. We also investigate the genericity of elements inside such an L-packet with respect to varying Whittaker datum. Pertaining to the above two problems, covers of the symplectic similitudes groups are discussed in detail in the last part of the paper.



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 11F70; Secondary 22E50

1.
Introduction

Let
F
be a
p
-adic field. Let
G={\bf G}(F)
be the group of
F
-rational points of a connected split reductive group
{\bf G}
over
F
.
Let
H\subset G
be a closed subgroup, which might be the
F
-rational points of a subgroup
{\bf H}\subset{\bf G}
over
F
but not always so. Let
\pi\in{\rm Irr}(G)
be an irreducible admissible representation. It is an important question to determine the restriction
\pi|_{H}
. As the literature on this problem is vast, we only mention three representative examples. The first one originated from the theory of Gelfand pair
[
GK
,
Gro1
]
, and it includes the spherical theory when
H\subset G
is a maximal compact subgroup, and the Whittaker theory when
H
is the unipotent radical of a Borel subgroup. The second example concerns the Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture, see
[
GP1
,
GGP1
]
and
[
Gan14-2
]
including references therein. The third example arises from the special case where
H
is closely related to the derived subgroup
G_{\rm der}
of
G
.

If
\pi|_{H}
is of finite length, then we have the semisimplification

(\pi|_{H})^{\rm ss}=\sum_{i}m_{i}\pi_{i}\in\mathscr{R}(H),

where
\pi_{i}\in{\rm Irr}(H)
and
\mathscr{R}(H)
denotes the Grothendieck group of
{\rm Irr}(H)
. Part of the problem is then on determining the multiplicity
m_{i}
, which usually manifests in terms of some representation-theoretic and arithmetic invariants associated with the pair
(\pi,\pi_{i})\in{\rm Irr}(G)\times{\rm Irr}(H)
. A special case is the last example mentioned above. Now we elaborate on this, as its covering analogue is the main focus of our paper.

It was shown by Silberger
[
Sil79
]
that for every
\pi\in{\rm Irr}(G)
, the restriction
\pi|_{G_{\rm der}}
is semisimple of finite length and thus

\pi|_{G_{\rm der}}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}m_{i}\cdot\pi_{i}

with
\pi_{i}\in{\rm Irr}(G_{\rm der})
and
m_{i}\geqslant 1
. This is a weaker form of a conjecture by Borel
[
Bor
]
that all the constituents
\pi_{i}
belong to the same L-packet associated with the parameter

f\circ\phi_{\pi}:\text{WD}_{F}\to{}^{L}G\to{}^{L}G_{\rm der},

where
f
is the natural quotient map. In fact, the conjecture of Borel concerns slightly more general group homomorphism
H\to G
where
H
may not be
G_{\rm der}
. A special case of this is when
G_{\rm der}\subset H\subset G
, and for such
H
Adler and Prasad proposed a unified formula for the multiplicity
m_{i}
, see
[
AP1
]
. The original conjecture by Borel was also generalized in another direction using the enchanced L-parameters. By an approach via the Hecke algebras, such generalized version was proved for principal series and unipotent representations of
G
, see the work of Aubert–Baum–Plymen–Solleveld
[
ABPS17
]
and Solleveld
[
Sol20
]
. We note that there are other works on various families of groups and representations for the restriction problem from
G
to
G_{\rm der}
, see
[
Key3
,
Nev15
,
BCG18
,
Cho19
]
.

The starting point of this paper is motivated from the above (third) example in the setting of covering groups. More precisely, assume that
F
contains the full group
\mu_{n}
of
n
-th roots of unity, and consider a central cover

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}

of
G
by
\mu_{n}
arising from the Brylisnki–Deligne framework
[
BD
]
. Denote by

{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})

the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible genuine representations of
\overline{G}
, where
\mu_{n}
always acts by a fixed embedding
\mu_{n}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
. The derived subgroup of
\overline{G}
is equal to the cover
\overline{G}_{\rm der}
of
G_{\rm der}
obtained from the pull-back of
\overline{G}
via the canonical inclusion
G_{\rm der}\subset G
. While the restriction problem for representations from
G
is
G_{\rm der}
is already a nontrivial one involving data from the L-parameter side, this is even more the case for central covers.

Indeed, it is already instructive to consider the restriction of the principal series representations of
G
and
\overline{G}
to
G_{\rm der}
and
\overline{G}_{\rm der}
respectively. Let
I(\chi)
be a principal series of
G
, where
\chi
is a character of the torus
T
. It is well-known that

I(\chi)|_{G_{\rm der}}=I(\chi_{0}),

where
\chi_{0}=\chi|_{\overline{T}_{0}}
with
T_{0}\subset G_{\rm der}
being the split torus. However, if
I(\chi)
is a genuine principal series of
\overline{G}
, where
\chi
is a genuine character of the center
Z(\overline{T})\subset\overline{T}
, then the restriction
I(\chi)|_{G_{\rm der}}
, which is still semisimple, may not be an isotypic sum of genuine principal series of
G_{\rm der}
. In fact, this phenomenon arises from its counterpart for the genuine representations of covering tori. That is, the decomposition of an irreducible genuine representation of
\overline{T}
, when restricted to
\overline{T}_{0}
, is more delicate than the linear case, which is deceptively simple. Such delicacy is a consequence of the fact that

Z(\overline{T}_{0})\nsubseteq Z(\overline{T})

in general.

Thus, we expect multiple layers of subtleties arising from the multiplicity formula for the restriction
\pi|_{\overline{G}_{\rm der}}

for
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})
. This also renders many new phenomena regarding the local coefficients associated with
\pi
and certain intertwining operators. To illustrate, we consider first the linear case. Let
\pi
be a
\psi
-generic representation of
G
, where

\psi:U\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}

is a nondegenerate character of the unipotent radical
U
of a Borel subgroup
B=TU
. We have the canonical identification

{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi)={\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi|_{G_{\rm der}}).

It follows from the multiplicity-one property of Whittaker functionals that one has

{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi)={\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{0})

for a unique constituent
\pi_{0}\subset\pi|_{G_{\rm der}}
. Thus, the arithmetic information encoded in the local coefficients of
\pi
for
G
is completely elucidated from this unique
\pi_{0}
.
The simplest such example is when
\pi=I(\chi)
is a principal series of
G
as above. This is in contrast with the situation for covers. Indeed, although for
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})
we still have

{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi)={\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi|_{\overline{G}_{\rm der}}),

the two sides might be of high dimensions. In fact, it is possible that
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{i})\geqslant 1
for every constituent
\pi_{i}\subset\pi|_{\overline{G}_{\rm der}}
. Hence, it is necessary to study every space
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{i})
, as there may not be any distinguished one. Already as a preliminary step, it is essential to study the multiplicity formula in the decomposition
\pi|_{\overline{G}_{\rm der}}
.

In fact, the relation between the Whittaker spaces and restriction problems can be deepened if one considers the more “restrictive” Whittaker space. More precisely, the
\psi
-Whittaker space
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi)
of
\pi
is naturally a genuine
\overline{Z(G)}
-module, where
\overline{Z(G)}
is a Heisenberg type group and thus its genuine irreducible representations are finite dimensional with the same dimension. If the uniqueness of
\psi
-Whittaker functionals for
\pi
holds, then
\overline{Z(G)}
is necessarily abelian. The converse may not hold. However, assume that
\overline{Z(G)}
is abelian and
\mu
a genuine character of it, and let
\psi\times\mu
be the corresponding representation of
U\times\overline{Z(G)}
, then one can ask for the dimension of the
\mu
-isotypic subspace

{\rm Wh}_{\psi\times\mu}(\pi)\subset{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi).

For the two-fold Kazhdan–Patterson covering
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(2)}
with twisting parameter
c=0
(in the notation of
[
KP
]
), it was shown by Gelbart, Howe, and Piatetski-Shapiro
[
GHPS
]
that
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi\times\mu}(\pi)\leqslant 1
for every
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(2)})
. This fact was used in
[
GPS
]
to study distinguished theta representations. Such uniqueness results for
{\rm Wh}_{\psi\times\mu}(\pi)
do not hold for higher degree cover of
{\rm GL}_{2}
, nor for covers of
{\rm GL}_{r}
in general. On the other hand, similar results were obtained for certain covers of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
(see
[
Szp4-1
, Theorem 5.1]
), which follow from analyzing the restriction of
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{(2)})
to
\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}^{(2)}
and the uniqueness of Whittaker models for the metaplectic group
\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}
as shown in
[
Szp1
]
.

We also mention that some restriction and induction results between the above double cover
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(2)}
and the derived subgroup
\overline{{\rm SL}}_{2}^{(2)}
were explicated in the work
[
GPS83
]
by Gelbart and Piatetski-Shapiro, for both local and global groups.
For untwisted (i.e.,
c=0
as above) Kazhdan–Patterson covers of
{\rm GL}_{r}
, similar results were obtained by Adams
[
Ada03
]
. In a more recent work
[
PatPr1
]
, Patel and Prasad refined the multiplicity formula for the restriction problem for
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(2)}
, by utilizing the Shimura–Waldspurger correspondence.

It is also worthwhile to point out that in the tame setting when
p\nmid n
, even if
I(\chi)
is a
(K,s_{K})
-unramified representation of
\overline{G}
, where
K\subset G
is a hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup of
G
and

s_{K}:K\hookrightarrow\overline{G}

a fixed splitting (assuming its existence), there might exist constituents in
I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{\rm der}}
which are not unramified with respect to

K_{0}:=K\cap G_{\rm der}

and its inherited splitting into
\overline{G}_{\rm der}
from
s_{K}
. This already occurs for even-fold cover of
G={\rm GL}_{2}
and was discussed in some depth in
[
Szp6
,
GSS2
]
.

Our paper is thus partly motivated from all the above and we investigate several aspects of the representation theory of covering groups centered around such restriction problem, mainly on the following themes:

(A1)

the restriction and induction functor between
\overline{G}
and
\overline{G}_{\rm der}
on the representation side,

(A2)

natural speculations and results on the L-parameter side corresponding to the restriction and induction in (A1),

(A3)

the behaviour of the Whittaker spaces and the local coefficients matrices for representations of
G
and
G_{\rm der}
with respect to restriction.

Since at this stage we do not have a full understanding of the
\psi
-Whittaker space (or even its dimension) of an arbitrary genuine representation of a covering group, in order to obtain precise results, at various places we focus only on genuine principal series, or even on the unramified ones. Even with such confinement on the class of representations under investigation, exhibited are many new and interesting results which do not seem to exist for linear algebraic groups.

1.1.
Main results

We give a brief summary on the content and main results proved in this paper. While some notations are standard, we refer the reader to the actual theorems where full elaboration is given not only on the notations and also on their content. The three themes in (A1)–(A3) are not studied in a strictly linear order and are interwoven with the results proved in various sections.

1.1.1.

In §
2
, we study the restriction and induction on the representation side between
\overline{G}
and a certain normal subgroup
\overline{H}
of
\overline{G}
. For this purpose, we first recall the general Clifford–Mackey theory. There are essentially two families of such
\overline{H}
considered in this paper, which are of different nature.

(i)

In the first case,
\overline{H}\subset\overline{G}
is such that every
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})
is
\overline{H}
-concentrated (see Definition
2.1
). This implies that the support of the Harish-Chandra character distribution
\Theta_{\pi}
lies in
\overline{H}
. Such normal subgroup
\overline{H}
will be the focus of discussion in §
3.4
, where we study the metapletic tensor product construction of
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
, as an analogue to that of
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r}
studied in
[
Kab1
,
Mez04
,
Tak3
,
Tak4
,
Cai1
]
.

(ii)

For the second type, we take

\overline{H}=\overline{Z(G)}\cdot\overline{G}_{\rm der},

where
Z(G)\subset G
denotes the center of
G
. Here
\overline{Z(G)}
and
\overline{G}_{\rm der}
are of dual-pair alike inside
\overline{G}
. Every irreducible representation of
\overline{Z(G)}\cdot\overline{G}_{\rm der}
is of the form
\tau\boxtimes\rho
, where
\tau
and
\rho
agree on the intersection of
\overline{Z(G)}
and
\overline{G}_{\rm der}
. Since
\overline{Z(G)}
is a Heisenberg-type group,
\tau\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{Z(G)})
is of finite dimension and determined by its central character. The study of restriction and induction between representations of
\overline{G}_{\rm der}
and
\overline{G}
essentially amounts to those between
\overline{H}
and
\overline{G}
.

In fact, the consideration in (ii) above applies to the Levi subgroups
\overline{M}
of
\overline{G}
, and concerns the remaining discussion in §
2
. For convenience, we write henceforth

M_{0}:=M_{\rm der}

for every Levi subgroup
M\subset G
, and denote

\overline{M}^{\dagger}:=\overline{Z(G)}\cdot\overline{M}_{0}\subset\overline{Z(M)}\cdot\overline{M}_{0}.

Thus,
G_{0}
denotes
G_{\rm der}
, and
\overline{G}^{\dagger}=\overline{Z(G)}\cdot\overline{G}_{0}
.
Note that we use
\overline{Z(G)}
(instead of
\overline{Z(M)}
) in the definition of
\overline{M}^{\dagger}
for all Levi subgroup
M\subset G
.
In §
2.3.1
, we give a sufficient criterion for the induced representation

{\rm Ind}_{\overline{M}^{\dagger}}^{\overline{M}}(\tau\boxtimes\rho)

to be irreducible. The idea is simple as we explain now. The group

\mathfrak{Q}^{\dagger}:=\overline{M}/\overline{M}^{\dagger}

acts naturally on

\mathfrak{Q}_{M}:=Z(\overline{M}^{\dagger})/Z(\overline{M})

by conjugation and thus on its Pontryagin dual
\widehat{\mathfrak{Q}_{M}}
. If the action of
\mathfrak{Q}^{\dagger}
on
\widehat{\mathfrak{Q}_{M}}
is free, then the Mackey’s theory gives the irreducibility of
{\rm Ind}_{\overline{M}^{\dagger}}^{\overline{M}}(\tau\boxtimes\rho)
. Similarly, we can define
\mathfrak{Q}_{Z}
and
\mathfrak{Q}_{M_{0}}
associated with
\overline{Z(G)}
and
\overline{M}_{0}
which gives a
\mathfrak{Q}^{\dagger}
-equivariant embedding

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathfrak{Q}_{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathfrak{Q}_{Z}}\times\widehat{\mathfrak{Q}_{M_{0}}}.}

We have

Theorem 1.1

(Theorem
2.11
)
.

Let
\overline{M}\subset\overline{G}
be a covering Levi subgroup.

(i)

Suppose the action of
\mathfrak{Q}^{\dagger}
on either
\widehat{\mathfrak{Q}_{Z}}
or
\widehat{\mathfrak{Q}_{M_{0}}}
is free. Then
{\rm Ind}_{\overline{M}^{\dagger}}^{\overline{M}}(\tau\boxtimes\rho)
is irreducible for every
\tau\boxtimes\rho\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}^{\dagger})
.

(ii)

Specializing to the case
M=T
, if the action of
\mathfrak{Q}^{\dagger}
on both
\widehat{\mathfrak{Q}_{Z}}
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{Q}_{T_{0}}}
are trivial, then for every
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{T})
we have

\pi|_{\overline{T}^{\dagger}}=(\tau\boxtimes\rho)^{\oplus e}

for some

e\in\mathbf{N}
. On the other hand, if
Z(\overline{T})\subset Z(\overline{Z(G)})\cdot Z(\overline{T}_{0})
, then
{\rm Ind}_{\overline{T}^{\dagger}}^{\overline{T}}(\tau\boxtimes\rho)
is an isotypic sum of an irreducible genuine representation of
\overline{T}
.

In Corollary
2.12
, we give several explicit and equivalent conditions for the restriction
\pi|_{\overline{T}_{0}}
to be an isotypic sum. Following an idea from
[
Szp4-1
]
, we study the irreducibility of parabolic induction for
\overline{G}
and
\overline{G}_{0}
, see Theorem
2.15
.

Several families of covers are worked out in detail in §
2.4
. In particular, we analyze the structure of the restriction and induction of representations for covers of
{\rm GL}_{r},{\rm GSp}_{2r}
and
{\rm GSpin}_{2r+1}
, and apply Theorem
2.11
and Corollary
2.12
to obtain concrete results, see Propositions
2.16
,
2.20
and
2.22
. We note that the example of covers of
{\rm GSpin}_{2r+1}
was already discussed by Kaplan in
[
Kap004
]
. The scheme of analysis in §
2.4
essentially follows
[
Szp4-1
,
Szp5
]
. Indeed, the results in
[
GPS83
]
for
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(2)}
were generalized in
[
Szp4-1
]
for
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{(2)}
, where the latter also includes a discussion on the analogous problem of restriction and induction on covering Levi subgroups of
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{(2)}
. It should be noted that a common feature of all the covers studied in
[
Ada03
,
GPS83
,
Szp4-1
]
is that
\overline{Z(G)}
is abelian. However, in this paper (and also the previous study
[
GSS2
]
), we do not impose such constraints and treat covers in generality.

In the last part of §
2
, we work out explicitly in the tame case (i.e.,
when
p\nmid n
) the decomposition of a genuine principal series
I(\chi)
of
\overline{G}
when restricted to
\overline{G}_{0}
. Recall that in the tame case, if
\overline{G}
splits over the hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup
K\subset G
generated by
{\bf T}(O)
and
e_{\alpha}(O)
, then we could fix such a splitting
s_{K}:K\hookrightarrow\overline{G}
and consider a
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series
I(\chi)
. Such a
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series is parabolically induced from
i(\chi)\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{T})
. We set
K_{0}=K\cap G_{\rm der}
.

Theorem 1.2

(Theorem
2.24
)
.

Let
i(\chi):={\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}}^{\overline{T}}(\tilde{\chi})\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{T})
be with central character
\chi
. Let
I(\chi)
be the associated genuine principal series of
\overline{G}
.

(i)

One has

i(\chi)|_{\overline{T}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}\bigoplus_{\omega_{\gamma,j}\in\mathscr{E}(\chi,{}^{\gamma}\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))}i(\omega_{\gamma,j}),

where every
i(\omega_{\gamma,j})\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{T}_{0})
appears with multiplicity one in the double sum of the right hand side.

(ii)

Consequently,

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}\bigoplus_{\omega_{\gamma,j}\in\mathscr{E}(\chi,{}^{\gamma}\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))}I(\omega_{\gamma,j})

for the restriction of the genuine principal series
I(\chi)
. Here generically, the
I(\omega_{\gamma,j})
’s appear multiplicity-free in the double sum of the right hand side.

(iii)

Assume that
I(\chi)
is an
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series. Then
I(\omega_{\gamma,j})
is
(K_{0},s_{K})
-unramified if and only if
\omega_{\gamma,j}
belongs to the set
\mathscr{E}(\chi,\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))
, i.e., with
\gamma=0\in\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}
being the trivial class.

In the above theorem, the finite abelian group
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}
is a natural quotient of
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}
, the latter of which is the “moduli space” of the Whittaker space
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\chi))
when
I(\chi)
is unramified, as discussed in detail in
[
GSS2
]
. We highlight that from Theorem
1.2
(iii) above, the genuine principal series
I(\omega_{\gamma,j})
of
\overline{G}_{0}
with
\gamma\neq 0
is not
(K_{0},s_{K})
-unramified.
In fact, such
I(\omega_{\gamma,j}),\gamma\neq 0
is unramified with respect to
(K_{0}^{\prime},s_{K^{\prime}})
inherited from a different pair
(K^{\prime},s_{K^{\prime}})
for
\overline{G}
.

It is easy to work out from Theorem
1.2
when the restriction
I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}
is an isotypic sum of a genuine principal series: this is exactly when
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})
is an isotypic pair in the sense of Definition
2.23
, i.e., when

Z(\overline{T}_{0})\subset Z(\overline{T}),

or equivalently by Corollary
2.12
,
Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}=Y_{0,Q,n}
.

1.1.2.

In §
3
, we study the dual side of the restriction and induction discussed on the representation side in §
2
. Some anomalies in the covering setting were already discussed in
[
GG
]
and part of the work here may be considered as a generalization and further elaboration of some speculations there. To motivate the general setup, it is instructive to consider the Kazhdan–Patterson double cover
\overline{G}=\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(2)}
, which by restriction gives the classical metaplectic double cover
\overline{G}_{0}=\overline{{\rm SL}}_{2}^{(2)}
. Regarding the dual groups, one has

\overline{G}^{\vee}={\rm GL}_{2}\text{ and }\overline{G}_{0}^{\vee}={\rm SL}_{2}.

Clearly, there is no natural nontrivial homomorphism of algebraic groups from
\overline{G}^{\vee}
to
\overline{G}_{0}^{\vee}
, which supposedly encodes from the dual side the restriction functor from
\overline{G}
to
\overline{G}_{0}
. On the other hand, both
\overline{G}^{\vee}
and
\overline{G}_{0}^{\vee}
map naturally to
{\rm PGL}_{2}
, which thus serves as the bridge between the two dual groups.

For general
\overline{G}
, there is a natural linear algebraic group
H
and a cover
\overline{H}
whose dual group
\overline{H}^{\vee}
is endowed with two natural maps:

f_{G,H}:\overline{G}^{\vee}\longrightarrow\overline{H}^{\vee}\text{ and }f_{G_{0},H}:\overline{G}^{\vee}_{0}\longrightarrow\overline{H}^{\vee}.

These two maps can be extended to homormophisms of the corresponding L-groups. Thus, we expect a natural commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{Z(G)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}Z(\overline{G})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{WD}_{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{G}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{c,z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\rho}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{G,z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{G,H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{G_{o},H}}

indicating some natural speculations on the pertinent parameters for representations involved in the restriction and induction between
\overline{G}
and
\overline{G}_{0}
. This we explain below.

Denote by

\phi:\text{WD}_{F}\longrightarrow{}^{L}\overline{G}

an L-parameter of
\overline{G}
. Let
\mathcal{L}(\phi)
be the associated hypothetical L-packet. We assume the strong form of the local Langlands correspondence that every
\phi\in\mathcal{L}(\phi)
is associated with an enhanced parameter

(\phi,\theta)\in\Phi^{\rm en}({}^{L}\overline{G}),

where
\theta
is an irreducible representation of the component group
\mathcal{S}_{\phi}:=\mathcal{S}(\phi)
of
\phi
. Denote

\phi^{\diamondsuit}=f_{G,H}\circ\phi,

and consider the naturally induced map

\mathcal{S}_{\phi}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\phi^{\diamondsuit}},

which is an embedding (see
[
Sol20
, Proposition 5.4]
). Let
\Phi({}^{L}\overline{G}_{0};\phi)
be the set of parameters
\phi_{0}:\text{WD}_{F}\to{}^{L}\overline{G}_{0}
such that
f_{G_{0},H}\circ\phi_{0}=\phi^{\diamondsuit}
. For every
\phi_{0}\in\Phi({}^{L}\overline{G}_{0};\phi)
, we have an embedding

\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{0}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\phi^{\diamondsuit}}

as well.

Conjecture 1.3

(Conjecture
3.2
)
.

Let
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})
be with central character
\omega_{\pi}
and associated enhanced parameter
(\phi_{\pi},\theta_{\pi})\in\Phi^{\rm en}({}^{L}\overline{G})
. Let
\tau\boxtimes\rho
be an irreducible representation of
\overline{G}^{\dagger}=\overline{Z(G)}\cdot\overline{G}_{0}
occurring in the restriction of
\pi
.

(i)

The L-parameters
\phi_{\omega_{\pi}},\phi_{\tau}
and
\phi_{\rho}
are such that the following hold:

\bullet

f_{G,z}\circ\phi_{\pi}=f_{c,z}\circ\phi_{\tau}
and is equal to the parameter
\phi_{\omega_{\pi}}
associated with the central character
\omega_{\pi}
of
\pi
;

\bullet

\phi_{\pi}^{\diamondsuit}=f_{G_{0},H}\circ\phi_{\rho}
.

(ii)

There exists
e(\pi)\in\mathbf{N}
such that for every
\tau\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G}_{0})
with enhanced parameter
(\phi_{\tau},\theta_{\tau})\in\Phi^{\rm en}({}^{L}\overline{G}_{0})
, one has

\dim{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi,\tau)=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }\phi_{\tau}\notin\Phi({}^{L}\overline{G}_{0};\phi_{\pi}),\\
e(\pi)\cdot{\left\langle{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\pi}}}^{\mathcal{S}_{\phi^{\diamondsuit}}}\theta_{\pi},{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\tau}}}^{\mathcal{S}_{\phi^{\diamondsuit}}}\theta_{\tau}\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}_{\phi^{\diamondsuit}}}&\text{ if }\phi_{\tau}\in\Phi({}^{L}\overline{G}_{0};\phi_{\pi}),\end{cases}

where
{\left\langle-,-\right\rangle}
denotes the pairing of two representations of
\mathcal{S}_{\phi^{\diamondsuit}}
; in particular, if
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})
is an isotypic-pair, then

\pi|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=e(\pi)\cdot\bigoplus_{\tau\in\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\pi}^{\diamondsuit})}{\left\langle\theta_{\tau}|_{\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\pi}}},\theta_{\pi}\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\pi}}}\cdot\tau,

where
(\phi_{\pi}^{\diamondsuit},\theta_{\tau})\in\Phi^{\rm en}({}^{L}\overline{G}_{0})
is the enhanced parameter associated with
\tau\in\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\pi}^{\diamondsuit})
.

(iii)

Let
\tau\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{Z(G)})
and
\rho\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G}_{0})
be compatible representations. Then every irreducible constituent
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(G)
of
{\rm Ind}_{\overline{G}^{\dagger}}^{\overline{G}}(\tau\boxtimes\rho)
has a parameter
\phi_{\pi}
which fits into a commutative diagram (
3.6
).

Part (ii) above is clearly motivated from the case of linear algebraic groups, see
[
AP1
]
and references therein. Conjecture
1.3
is not a statement that can be tackled at the moment for general
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})
, as it relies on an established local Langlands correspondence (LLC). For example, it would be interesting to see if the results as in
[
PatPr1
]
for
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(2)}
fit into Conjecture
1.3
, if one has an established LLC for
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(2)}
. For general covering torus for which LLC has been proved (see
[
KP
,
Mc1
,
We1
,
We5
,
We6
,
GG
]
), we verify this in §
3.3
, see especially Proposition
3.4
.

In the remaining of §
3
, we study the metaplectic tensor product and a metaplectic restriction (the latter construction as a counterpart of the former) for covers
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
. For Kazhdan–Patterson covers
{\rm GL}_{r}
, this problem was studied by P. Mezo
[
Mez04
]
and also in several works following it, as mentioned in §
1.1.1
. For a general Levi subgroup

M_{{\bf r}}={\rm GL}_{r_{1}}\times...\times{\rm GL}_{r_{k}},

the covering subgroups
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}
and
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{j}}
of
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r}
do not commute in general, and thus the representation theory of
\overline{M}_{\bf r}
is not obtained from the naive tensor product of a genuine representation of each
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}
. However, there is still a surjective map (see
[
Mez04
]
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\otimes}:({\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{1}})\times...\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{k}})\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r})))^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{\bf r}),}

where the superscript
(-)^{\heartsuit}
indicates the subset of

(\pi_{1},...,\pi_{r},\omega)

satisfying certain compatible relation. The map
\tilde{\otimes}
is called the metaplectic tensor product construction. The functorial interpretation of
\tilde{\otimes}
on the dual side was given by Gan
[
Gan17
]
.

As an analogue of
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r}
above, in §
3.4
we investigate the case of
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
. Let

{\bf r}=(r_{1},r_{2},...,r_{k};r_{0})
be a partition of
r
. Let

M_{\bf r}={\rm GL}_{r_{1}}\times{\rm GL}_{r_{2}}\times...\times{\rm GL}_{r_{k}}\times{\rm GSp}_{2r_{0}}

be the associated Levi subgroup. We show the following.

Theorem 1.4

(Theorem
3.8
)
.

Let
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
be a similitudes-splitting
n
-fold cover of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
with
n
odd. There is a well-defined bijective map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\otimes}_{i}:(\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})))^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{\bf r}),}

where the superscript
(-)^{\heartsuit}
indicates the subset of
(\pi_{1},...,\pi_{k},\pi_{0},\omega)
satisfying a certain equality (
3.15
). The inverse of
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}
is given by a natural and explicit “metaplectic restriction” map
\tilde{\rm R}
.

One has a conjectural dual side description of the two maps
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}
and
\tilde{\rm R}
given in the theorem above, on the relations among the parameters of the representations involved. This is Conjecture
3.10
, and is clearly motivated from that in
[
Gan17
]
for Kazhdan–Patterson covers; in fact, it is a simpler situation dealing with
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
here. For genuine principal series, we verify Conjecture
3.10
in Proposition
3.11
. We also explain the obstacles to realizing a similar metaplectic tensor product construction for even-fold cover of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
.

In fact, such metaplectic tensor product construction is ubiquitous for covering Levi subgroups. Indeed, it essentially amounts to the existence of a certain subgroup
\overline{H}\subset\overline{M}
satisfying two conditions which counteract against each other. On the one hand, we would like that every
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M})
is
\overline{H}
-concentrated, and thus
\overline{H}
should be taken as large as possible. On the other hand, suppose
\overline{M}_{i}\subset\overline{M}
are the covering blocks, then it is a desired property that
\overline{H}\cap\overline{M}_{i}
commutes with each other: this is satisfied only when
\overline{H}
is as small as possible. Whenever a subgroup
\overline{H}\subset\overline{M}
satisfying these two conditions exist, one could have a metaplectic tensor product. For
\overline{M}_{\bf r}
of the Kazhdan–Patterson covers, we take
\overline{H}
to be essentially the subgroup of elements with determinants lying in
F^{\times n}
; while for
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{(n)}
with
n
odd, we take the subgroup with similitudes in
F^{\times n}
. For more details, see §
3.4.3
.

1.1.3.

In §
4
, we study how the local coefficients matrices associated with certain intertwining operators behave with respect to the restriction. More precisely, suppose

\pi|_{\overline{G}_{\rm der}}=\bigoplus_{i}m_{i}\cdot\pi_{i},

with
\pi_{i}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G}_{\rm der})
. Then one has

{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi)=\bigoplus_{i}m_{i}\cdot{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{i}).

Let
\overline{M}\subset\overline{P}\subset\overline{G}
be a Levi subgroup and
\sigma\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M})
. Let

T(w,\sigma):I_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma)\longrightarrow I_{\overline{P}^{\prime}}^{\overline{G}}({}^{w}\sigma)

be the standard intertwining operator, where
\overline{P}^{\prime}
is a parabolic subgroup associated with
\overline{P}
by
w
. It is shown in
[
GSS2
, §3.2]
that there is a naturally induced homomorphism

\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma)^{*}:{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma))\longrightarrow{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma)).

With the choice of a basis
\mathfrak{B}
for
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma))
, one obtains a local coefficients matrix

\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}(w,\sigma)

representing
\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma)^{*}
.

For every
\sigma_{0}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{0})
, one has an analogous map

\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma_{0})^{*}:{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\overline{G}_{0}}(\sigma))\longrightarrow{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\overline{G}_{0}}(\sigma)).

We show that

\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma)^{*}=\bigoplus_{\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}}\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma_{i})^{*}.

This implies that there is a local coefficients matrix
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}(w,\sigma)
taking the form

\bigoplus_{\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{i}}(w,\sigma_{i}),

where
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{i}}(w,\sigma_{i})
is a local coefficients matrix associated with the triple
(\overline{P}_{0},w,\sigma_{i})
and a basis
\mathfrak{B}_{i}
of
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\overline{G}_{0}}(\sigma_{i}))
, see Proposition
4.1
. In particular, the arithmetic invariants (trace and determinant, for example) of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}(w,\sigma)
are completely determined by those of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{i}}(w,\sigma_{i})
.

Since the Whittaker space
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi)
is best understood for a genuine principal series, we specialize to this case and investigate the relation between
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi)
and
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{i}),\pi_{i}\subset\pi|_{\overline{G}_{\rm der}}
, where
\pi\in{\rm JH}(I(\chi))
is an irreducible constituent of a
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series. In fact, we consider the two special cases:

–

when
I(\chi)
is a regular unramified principal series in §
4.3
, and

–

when
I(\chi)
is a unitary unramified principal series in §
4.4
.

The analysis relies on certain results and conjectures from
[
Ga6
,
Ga7
]
. We elaborate below these two cases.

First, suppose
I(\chi)
is a regular unramified principal series. Let
\Phi(\chi)

be the set of rank-one reducibility points. Then every irreducible constituent of
I(\chi)
is uniquely parametrized by a subset
S\subset\Phi(\chi)
, which we denote by
\pi_{\chi,S}
or
\pi(\chi)_{S}
. If
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})
is an isotypic pair, then one has
\Phi(\chi)=\Phi(\omega)
where
\omega=\chi|_{Z(\overline{T}_{0})}
; in this case,

(\pi_{\chi,S})|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot\pi_{\omega,S},

which immediately yields the equality

(1.1)

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi,S})=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\omega,S}).

Assume

\Phi(\chi)\subset\Delta,

i.e., it is a subset of simple roots. Then we have a formula for
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi,S})
and
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\omega,S})
in terms of certain permutation representations
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}}
and
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}
of
W
respectively. It is expected that the equality (
1.1
) of Whittaker dimensions actually originates from a relation between
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}}
and
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}
, see Conjecture
4.6
.
If
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})
is not an isotypic pair, then the relation between
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi,S})
and
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\omega,S})
for an arbitrary
I(\omega)\subset I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}
is quite complicated, since
I(\omega)
may not be
(K_{0},s_{K})
-unramified. This already occurs for even-fold cover of
{\rm GL}_{2}
(odd-fold cover of
{\rm GL}_{2}
gives an isotypic pair). However, for
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}
, by using the work in
[
GSS2
]
we can show the following:

Theorem 1.5

(Theorem
4.9
)
.

Consider the Kazhdan-Patterson
n
-fold cover
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}
. Let
\chi:Z(\overline{T})\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be an unramified genuine character such that
\Phi(\chi)=\Delta
. Assume
n=2m
is even and
\mathfrak{f}(\psi)=O_{F}
. We always have

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j})_{\emptyset})+\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j})_{\Delta})=\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j}))=m.

Moreover, we have an explicit formula for every
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j})_{\Delta})
, and thus also
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j})_{\emptyset}
.

Second, assume
I(\chi)
is a unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified principal series. Then we have a decomposition

I(\chi)=\bigoplus_{\tau\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\chi})}\pi_{\tau}

of
I(\chi)
into irreducible constituents, where

\mathcal{S}_{\chi}:=\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}}

is the component group of the centralizer of
{\rm Im}(\phi_{\chi})\subset{}^{L}\overline{G}
. If
I(\omega^{\flat})\subset I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}
is a
(K_{0},s_{K})
-unramified constituent as in Theorem
1.2
, then one has analogously

I(\omega^{\flat})=\bigoplus_{\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})}\pi_{\rho}.

Recall the map

\phi^{\diamondsuit}:=f_{G,H}\circ\phi_{\chi}=f_{G_{0},H}\circ\phi_{\omega^{\flat}}

and the associated component group
\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}=\mathcal{S}_{\phi^{\diamond}}
. We have the inclusions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}.}

The main result of §
4.4
is regarding the multiplicity
\dim{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\pi_{\tau})
.

Theorem 1.6

(Theorem
4.12
)
.

Let
I(\chi)
be a unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series of
\overline{G}
and
I(\omega^{\flat})
a
(K_{0},s_{K})
-unramified constituent of
I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}
. Then

(1.2)

\dim{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\pi_{\tau})=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot{\left\langle{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}^{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}\rho,{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}^{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}\tau\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}

for every
\tau\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\chi})
and
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})
.

In fact, even if
I(\omega^{\flat})\subset I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}
is not
(K_{0},s_{K})
-unramified, the equality holds as well, see Corollary
4.13
.
In particular, this verifies Conjecture
1.3
(ii) for unitary unramified principal series of
\overline{G}
with

e(I(\chi))=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|.

If
n=1
and
\overline{G}=G
is a split linear group, then the above formula recovers that in
[
Key3
]
, the proof of which in fact motivates that for Theorem
1.6
above.

1.1.4.

In §
5
, we concentrate on unramified L-packets and consider the following two problems:

(i)

the variation of the parametrization of elements inside an unramified L-packet with respect to the change of hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup
K
of
G
and different choices of splittings,

(ii)

the variation of the Whittaker dimensions of elements inside an unramified L-packet with respect to changing orbits of the Whittaker datum.

Let
\chi:Z(\overline{T})\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be a genuine central character. Fix a splitting
s_{T}:{\bf T}(O)\hookrightarrow\overline{T}
, and assume that
i(\chi)
is
({\bf T}(O),s_{T})
-unramified. By the local Langlands correspondence for covering tori, one has a parameter

\phi_{\chi}:\text{WD}_{F}\longrightarrow{}^{L}\overline{T}\hookrightarrow{}^{L}\overline{G}.

It is postulated that the L-packet
\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})
associated with
\phi_{\chi}
consists of exactly the subquotients of the principal series
I(\chi)
, which are
(K,s_{K})
-unramified with respect to a hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup
K\subset G
and a splitting
s_{K}:K\hookrightarrow\overline{G}
such that
s_{K}
restricts to
s_{T}
on
{\bf T}(O)
. It is also expected that there is a bijection between

\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})\longleftrightarrow{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}})

which we denote by

\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)\leftrightarrow\rho.

Thus, we want to investigate how the
(K,s_{K})
-unramifiedness of an element
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)\in\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})
is reflected from its parameter
\rho
.

For this purpose, we briefly recall the linear algebraic case. Let

\mathcal{K}=\left\{G_{x}:\ x\text{ is a hyperspecial point in }\mathscr{B}(G)\right\}/_{\sim}

be the set of conjugacy classes of hyperspecial maximal compact subgroups of
G
, where
\mathscr{B}(G)
is the Bruhat–Tits building associated with
G
. It is known that
\mathcal{K}
is a torsor over

\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}:={\rm Hom}((X/X^{sc})_{\rm tor},\mathbf{Q}/\mathbf{Z}),

and there is a natural surjective map

f_{\Gamma}:\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}}),

which gives a natural action of
\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
on
{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}})
given by
f_{\Gamma}(y)\otimes\rho
.
It follows from
[
Mis2
, Theorem 1]
that for every
K\in\mathcal{K}
and
y\in\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
, the representation
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)\in\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})
is
K
-unramified if and only if
\pi(\phi_{\chi},f_{\Gamma}(y)\otimes\rho)
is
y\cdot K
-unramified.

To generalize the above results to covering groups, we note the following obstacles:

\bullet

first, not every splitting of
K
into
\overline{G}
is compatible with
s_{T}
, and thus it is necessary to filter only those compatible ones,

\bullet

second, although
\mathcal{K}
stays as a torsor over
\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
, there is no longer any obvious natural map from
\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
to
{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}})
.

For the second obstacle above, since
\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}}
is the component group of the image of
\phi_{\chi}
in
{}^{L}\overline{G}
, there is a natural finite abelian group
\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}
arising from the principal endoscopic group
G_{Q,n}
of
\overline{G}
and a surjection

\tilde{f}_{\Gamma}:\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}}).

The map
\tilde{f}_{\Gamma}
is defined analogous to
f_{\Gamma}
in the linear case. With this in view, it is important to bridge the two groups
\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
and
\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}
. A substantial part of §
5.2
is to analyze such a relation, which gives Conjecture
5.4
.

Regarding the Whittaker space, we fix a Borel subgroup
B=TU
and vary the non-degenerate character
\psi:U\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
. Let
T_{ad}\subset G_{ad}
be the split torus of
G_{ad}
. The set of
T
-orbits of non-degenerate characters of
U
is a torsor over
T_{ad}/T
under the conjugation action. There is a natural quotient

T_{ad}/T\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}}),

and thus an action of
t\in T_{ad}/T
on
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}})
, which we denote by
t\cdot\rho
. In this case, it was shown in
[
Mis1
]
that
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)
is
\psi
-generic if and only if
\pi(\phi_{\chi},t\cdot\rho)
is
{}^{t}\psi
-generic.
Now for a covering group
\overline{G}
, there is no obvious map from
T_{ad}/T
to
{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}})
. Similar to the above discussion on the unramified-ness, there is a natural surjection

T_{Q,n,ad}/T_{Q,n}\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}}),

where
T_{Q,n}
is the torus of
G_{Q,n}
and
T_{Q,n,ad}
is the torus for its adjoint group
G_{Q,n,ad}
. Again, it is important to inspect any natural relation between
T_{Q,n,ad}/T_{Q,n}
and
T_{ad}/T
, or at the level of character lattices, to relate
X_{Q,n}/X_{Q,n}^{sc}
and
X/X^{sc}
. We discuss this in §
5.3
and postulate Conjecture
5.7
on the
\psi
-Whittaker dimension of
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)
with respect to the action of
t
on the character
\psi
.

The following is the main result of §
5
, amalgamated from the discussions in §
5.4
, especially the main Propositions therein.

Theorem 1.7
.

Conjecture
5.4
and Conjecture
5.7
hold for unitary unramified principal series of covers of
{\rm Sp}_{2r},{\rm SO}_{3}
, and also for Kazhdan–Patterson covers of
{\rm GL}_{r}
.

1.1.5.

In §
6
, we consider exclusively covers of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
and its unitary unramified principal series
I(\chi)
.
The discussion is oriented towards Conjecture
5.4
and Conjecture
5.7
. In particular, we

\bullet

determine the component group
\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}}
and the pair
(K,s_{K})
with respect to which
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)
is unramified;

\bullet

determine the
\psi
-Whittaker dimension of each constituent
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)
, when
\mathfrak{f}(\psi)=\mathfrak{p}_{F}
or
O_{F}
;

\bullet

investigate the restriction of each
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)
to the derived subgroup
\overline{G}_{0}=\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}
and the pair
(K_{0},s_{K_{0}})
with respect to which an irreducible constituent of
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)|_{\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}}
is unramified, and also study the
\psi
-Whittaker dimension of such a constituent.

We summarize (in a compressed form) the main results for §
6
obtained from Theorems
6.1
,
6.2
and
6.4
.
Below
K
denotes the standard hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup and
K_{0}:=K\cap{\rm Sp}_{2r}
. Also,
\left\{K_{0},K_{0}^{\prime}\right\}
denotes the set of conjugacy classes of hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup of
{\rm Sp}_{2r}
, and
s_{0},s_{0}^{\prime}
denote the unique splittings of
K_{0}
and
K_{0}^{\prime}
into
\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}
respectively. Let
\psi
and
t\in T_{ad}/T
be such that

\mathfrak{f}(\psi)=O_{F}\text{ and }\mathfrak{f}({}^{t}\psi)=\mathfrak{p}_{F}.

We also write

n_{(r)}:=n/\gcd(n,r).

Theorem 1.8

(Theorems
6.1
,
6.2
and
6.4
)
.

Consider the
n
-fold cover
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
of similitudes-splitting type. Let
I(\chi)
be a unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series of
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
.

(i)

If
n
is odd, then
I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot I(\omega)
, where
\omega=\chi|_{Z(\overline{T}_{0})}
. If
I(\omega)=\pi(\phi_{\omega},\mathbbm{1})\oplus\pi(\phi_{\omega},\varepsilon)
is reducible, and we assume that
\pi(\phi_{\omega},\mathbbm{1})
is
(K_{0},s_{0})
-unramified, then
\pi(\phi_{\omega},\varepsilon)
is
(K_{0}^{\prime},s_{0}^{\prime})
-unramified.

(ii)

If
n
is even and
r
is odd, then
I(\chi)
is always irreducible and

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\frac{n_{(r)}}{2}\cdot(I(\omega_{0,0})\oplus I(\omega_{0,1}))\bigoplus\frac{n_{(r)}}{2}\cdot(I(\omega_{e_{0},0})\oplus I(\omega_{e_{0},1})),

where every
I(\omega_{\gamma,j})
is irreducible. Moreover,
I(\omega_{0,j}),j=0,1
is
(K_{0},s_{0})
-unramified and
I(\omega_{e_{0},j}),j=0,1
is
(K_{0}^{\prime},s_{0}^{\prime})
-unramified.

(iii)

If
n
is even and
r
is even, then

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=n_{(r)}\cdot(I(\omega_{0,0})\oplus I(\omega_{0,1}))\bigoplus n_{(r)}\cdot(I(\omega_{e_{0},0})\oplus I(\omega_{e_{0},1})),

where
I(\omega_{0,j})
is
(K_{0},s_{0})
-unramified and
I(\omega_{e_{0},j})
is
(K_{0}^{\prime},s_{0}^{\prime})
-unramified. Here every
I(\omega_{\gamma,j})
is irreducible. If
I(\chi)=\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})\oplus\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon)
is reducible, then

\displaystyle\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})|_{\overline{G}_{0}}\simeq\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{0,0})\bigoplus n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{e_{0},0})\simeq n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{0,1})\bigoplus n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{e_{0},1}).

In all cases above,
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\omega_{\gamma,j}},\rho))
and
\dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{t}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\omega_{\gamma,j}},\rho))
are determined explicitly for every
\rho\in\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\omega_{\gamma,j}})
.

The above theorem generalizes part of the results in
[
Szp5
]
.

1.2.
Several remarks

Some comments are in order pertaining to the results proved above.

(i)

In this paper, we mostly concentrate on genuine principal series of
\overline{G}
or even restrict to the unramified ones. It is desirable to work out for other classes of representations for
\overline{G}
in view of the conjectural formulas studied in §
3
regarding the multiplicity formula for the restriction. For example, the depth-zero supercuspidal representations for covers studied in
[
GW
]
form another test ground for such formulas. For restriction of certain supercuspidal representations from a linear group to its derived subgroup, see
[
Nev15
]
; for general discussion and results, see
[
AP1
,
Sol20
]
as well.

(ii)

Even for genuine principal series, we do not yet have a complete understanding of the local coefficients matrices or scattering matrices, which also play important roles in some other recent works such as
[
BBBF
,
Kap01
]
. Indeed, for general character (not necessarily unramified character) in the tame case, such a local coefficients matrix was already used in the proof of Theorem
1.5
, and subtleties were observed when comparing it with the unramified local coefficients matrix. Also, for the proof of Conjecture
5.7
on the
\psi
-Whittaker space of
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)
for varying
\psi
, it seems to be helpful, or perhaps even essential, to have an explicit description of the local coefficients matrix in the tame case. We leave the investigation of tame local coefficients matrix to a future work.

(iii)

To the best of our knowledge, in the literature there has not been any computation of the archimedean local coefficients matrix or scattering matrix for double covers
\overline{G}_{\mathbf{R}}
of
{\bf G}(\mathbf{R})
in the real case, except for the metaplectic double cover
{\rm Mp}_{2r}
of
{\rm Sp}_{2r}(\mathbf{R})
considered in
[
Szp2
,
Szp4
]
. In general, the
\psi
-Whittaker space of a genuine principal series of
\overline{G}_{\mathbf{R}}
may also be of high dimension. Besides
{\rm Mp}_{2r}
, the first examples to consider are the double covers
\overline{{\rm SL}}_{3,\mathbf{R}}
and
\overline{{\rm Spin}}_{7,\mathbf{R}}
. It is expected that analogous results regarding the invariants of such a local coefficients matrix hold in this archimedean setting, by employing the methods of partial zeta integrals as developed in
[
Szp6
]
and already used in
[
GSS2
]
.

(iv)

When the representation is not generic, an analogue of the local coefficients for certain classical groups was studied by Friedberg and Goldberg
[
FrGo
]
by considering the generalized Bessel models. The simplest family of genuine representations for covering groups concerns the theta representations
\Theta(\chi)
. If the degree of covering is “small” relative to the rank of the group, then
\Theta(\chi)
is not generic. It is interesting to investigate the Bessel models for these theta representations. On the other hand, the leading nilpotent orbit in the Harish-Chandra character expansion governs the generalized or degenerate Whittaker functionals for
\Theta(\chi)
, see
[
MW1
,
Var1
,
Pate
]
. It is interesting to see further how such generalized Bessel models and generalized Whittaker functionals are related to each other, in pursuit of the representation-theoretic invariants.

(v)

Especially lacking from the dual side is an interpretation of the high dimension of Whittaker models. Recall that for linear algebraic groups, it was a conjecture of the second-named author
[
Sha3
]
that there is a bijection between L-packets and tempered parameters, which now has been proved in many cases, see
[
Sha5
]
and references therein. Moreover, it follows from Gross–Reeder
[
GrRe2
]
that the adjoint
L
-function of a generic discrete series is necessarily holomorphic at
s=0
. For covering groups, it is expected that every L-packet associated with a tempered parameter also contains a generic representation; however, the converse already fails for theta representations mentioned in (iv) above. The L-parameter for the theta representation
\Theta(\chi)
is never tempered, although we could have
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\Theta(\chi))>0
if
n\gg r
. We do not yet know an interpretation from the dual side of the quantity
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\Theta(\chi))
, which was shown to be equal to the number of certain Weyl orbits. In fact, we are still very lacking any evidence for a general formula for
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi),\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})
.

It is clear that several speculations and results proved in this paper are motivated from its linear algebraic counterpart, while some others are new only in the covering setting, for example the metaplectic tensor product. We hope that the paper could provide a motivation to a further study on various aspects of the restriction problem for covers and the theory of local coefficients beyond the spectrum of genuine principal series. Again, as in
[
GSS2
]
, we strive to work with the most general setup if possible, and assume only the minimal requirement. Hopefully, this could provide some convenience for any further study on the topic.

1.3.
Acknowledgement

We would like to thank Wee Teck Gan, Eyal Kaplan, and Dipendra Prasad for some very helpful communications on certain topics discussed in this paper. The second author was partially supported by NSF grant DMS-1801273.

2.
Restriction and induction of genuine representations

2.1.
Covering groups and L-groups

We follow the exposition in
[
GSS2
, §2]
to summarize some important results on the structure of covering groups and the construction of their dual groups and L-groups. For more details, the reader is referred to
[
BD
,
Mc1
,
We2
,
We3
,
We6
,
We7
,
GG
]
.

2.1.1.
Covering groups

Let
{\bf G}
be a split connected linear reductive group over a
p
-adic field
F
with root datum

(X,\Phi,\Delta;Y,\Phi^{\vee},\Delta^{\vee}).

Here we fix a maximal split torus
{\bf T}\subset{\bf G}
, and
X
(resp.
Y
) is the character (resp. cocharacter) lattice of
{\bf T}
. Choose a set of simple roots
\Delta
from the set
\Phi
of roots. One has the corresponding simple coroots
\Delta^{\vee}
. Let
{\bf B}={\bf T}{\bf U}
be the Borel subgroup associated with
\Delta
. Let

W=N({\bf T})/{\bf T}

be the Weyl group of
({\bf G},{\bf T})
, which we identify with the Weyl group of the coroot system generated by the simply reflections
w_{\alpha},\alpha\in\Delta
. We also fix a Chevalley–Steinberg system of pinnings, i.e., a compatible system

\left\{e_{\alpha}:{\bf G}_{a}\to{\bf U}_{\alpha}\right\}_{\alpha\in\Phi},

where
{\bf U}_{\alpha}\subset{\bf G}
is the root subgroup associated with
\alpha
. Denote by
G,B,T
the
F
-rational points of
{\bf G},{\bf B},{\bf T}
respectively.

Let

D:Y\times Y\longrightarrow\mathbf{Z}

be a bilinear form such that

(2.1)

Q(y)=D(y,y)

is a Weyl-invariant bilinear form of
Y
. Here
D
may not be symmetric. If
Q
is an integral Weyl-invariant bilinear form, then it is known (see
[
We3
]
) that there exists
D
such that (
2.1
) holds. Let

B_{Q}:Y\times Y\longrightarrow\mathbf{Z}

be the Weyl-invariant bilinear form given by

B_{Q}(y,z)=D(y,z)+D(z,y),

which actually only depends on
Q
and thus justifies the notation used here. Furthermore, let

\eta:Y^{sc}\longrightarrow F^{\times}

be a homomorphism of the coroot lattice
Y^{sc}\subset Y
into
F^{\times}
. Denote by

\eta_{n}:Y^{sc}\longrightarrow F^{\times}\longrightarrow F^{\times}/F^{\times n}

the composite of
\eta
with the obvious quotient.

Every couple
(D,\eta)
gives rise to a
{\bf K}_{2}
-extension
\overline{{\bf G}}
of
{\bf G}
. Assuming
F^{\times}
contains the the full group of
n
-th roots of unity, denoted by
\mu_{n}
, one has an
n
-fold cover

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

obtained from the
n
-th Hilbert symbol

(-,-)_{n}:{\bf K}_{2}(F)\longrightarrow\mu_{n}.

A representation
(\pi,V_{\pi})
of
\overline{G}
is called genuine if
\mu_{n}
acts on
V_{\pi}
by a fixed embedding
\mu_{n}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
. In this paper, we denote by

{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})

the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible genuine representations of
\overline{G}
.

The covering group
\overline{G}
splits over unipotent subgroups canonically and
G
-equivariantly, where the action of
G
on
\overline{G}
is by conjugation. Namely, if
U\subset G
is a unipotent subgroup, then there is a unique splitting

s_{U}:U\longrightarrow\overline{G}

satisfying

s_{U}(gug^{-1})=\overline{g}s_{U}(u)\overline{g}^{-1},

where the right hand side is independent of the choice of lifting
\overline{g}\in\overline{G}
of
g\in G
.

Denote by
\overline{e}_{\alpha}(F)
the splitting of
e_{\alpha}(F)
in
\overline{G}
. For any
\alpha\in\Phi
and
x\in F^{\times}
, define

(2.2)

\overline{w}_{\alpha}(x):=\overline{e}_{\alpha}(x)\overline{e}_{-\alpha}(-x^{-1})\overline{e}_{\alpha}(x)

and

(2.3)

\overline{h}_{\alpha}(x):=\overline{w}_{\alpha}(x)\overline{w}_{\alpha}(-1).

For any
\alpha\in\Phi
, for simplicity we write

\overline{w}_{\alpha}:=\overline{w}_{\alpha}(1).

When we consider the case
n=1
(i.e.,
\overline{G}=G
), we will use the notations
e_{\alpha},w_{\alpha},h_{\alpha}
for
\overline{e}_{\alpha},\overline{w}_{\alpha},\overline{h}_{\alpha}
respectively.

The group
\overline{G}
is generated by the union of the sets
\mu_{n}
,
\left\{\overline{e}_{\alpha}(F)\right\}_{\alpha\in\Phi}
and
\left\{y(F^{\times})\right\}_{y\in Y}
(see
[
BLS
, Theorem 3]
). Relations among the generators include the following:

(A)

\overline{e}_{\alpha}(x)
is additive in
x
.

(B)

If
\alpha
and
\beta
are roots with
\alpha+\beta\neq 0
, then the commutator

[\overline{e}_{\alpha}(x),\overline{e}_{\beta}(y)]=\prod\overline{e}_{i\alpha+j\beta}(c_{i,j}x^{i}y^{j}),

where
i
and
j
are positive integers and
c_{i,j}
’s are certain integers.

(B)’

For any
\alpha\in\Phi
and
x\in F^{\times}
:

\overline{w}_{\alpha}(x)\overline{e}_{\alpha}(u)\overline{w}_{\alpha}(x)^{-1}=\overline{e}_{-\alpha}(-x^{2}u).

(C)

There exists a section
\mathbf{s}
of
\overline{T}
over
T
such that

\mathbf{s}(y_{1}(a))\cdot\mathbf{s}(y_{2}(b))=\mathbf{s}(y_{1}(a)\cdot y_{2}(b))\cdot(a,b)_{n}^{D(y_{1},y_{2})}

for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in Y
and
a,b\in F^{\times}
. For any
\alpha\in\Delta
and
x\in F^{\times}
, one has

\overline{h}_{\alpha}(x)=\mathbf{s}(\alpha^{\vee}(x))\cdot(\eta(\alpha^{\vee}),x)_{n}^{Q(\alpha^{\vee})}.

(D)

For
\overline{t}\in\overline{T}
whose image in
T
is denoted by
t
, one has

(2.4)

\overline{w}_{\alpha}\cdot\overline{t}\cdot\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}=\overline{t}\cdot\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\alpha(t)^{-1}).

By (C) above, the commutator

[-,-]:T\times T\longrightarrow\mu_{n}

is given by

[y_{1}(a),y_{2}(b)]=(a,b)_{n}^{B_{Q}(y_{1},y_{2})},

where
y_{i}\in Y
and
a,b\in F^{\times}
. Fix a uniformizer
\varpi\in F
. For any
y\in Y
, we write

\varepsilon:=(-1,\varpi)_{n}\in\mu_{n},\quad\mathbf{s}_{y}:=\mathbf{s}(y(\varpi))\in\overline{T}.

For every
n,r\in\mathbf{N}
, we also denote in this paper

n_{(r)}=n/\gcd(n,r).

It is clear that
n_{(r)}
and
r_{(n)}
are coprime.

2.1.2.
Dual group

For a cover
(\overline{{\bf G}},n)
associated to
(D,\eta)
, with
Q
and
B_{Q}
arising from
D
, we define

Y_{Q,n}:=Y\cap nY^{*},

where
Y^{*}\subset Y\otimes\mathbf{Q}
is the dual lattice of
Y
with respect to
B_{Q}
; more explicitly,

(2.5)

Y_{Q,n}=\left\{y\in Y:B_{Q}(y,y^{\prime})\in n\mathbf{Z}\text{ for all }y^{\prime}\in Y\right\}\subset Y.

For every
\alpha^{\vee}\in\Phi^{\vee}
, denote

n_{\alpha}:=n_{(Q(\alpha^{\vee}))}=\frac{n}{\text{gcd}(n,Q(\alpha^{\vee}))}

and

\alpha_{Q,n}^{\vee}=n_{\alpha}\alpha^{\vee},\quad\alpha_{Q,n}=\frac{\alpha}{n_{\alpha}}.

Let

Y_{Q,n}^{sc}\subset Y_{Q,n}

be the sublattice generated by
\Phi_{Q,n}^{\vee}=\{\alpha_{Q,n}^{\vee}:\alpha^{\vee}\in\Phi^{\vee}\}
. Denote
X_{Q,n}=\text{Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}}(Y_{Q,n},\mathbf{Z})
and
\Phi_{Q,n}=\left\{\alpha_{Q,n}:\alpha\in\Phi\right\}
. We also write

\Delta_{Q,n}^{\vee}=\{\alpha_{Q,n}^{\vee}:\alpha^{\vee}\in\Delta^{\vee}\}\text{ and }\Delta_{Q,n}=\left\{\alpha_{Q,n}:\alpha\in\Delta\right\}.

Then

\big(Y_{Q,n},\ \Phi_{Q,n}^{\vee},\ \Delta_{Q,n}^{\vee};\ X_{Q,n},\ \Phi_{Q,n}^{\vee},\Delta_{Q,n}\big)

forms a root datum with a choice of simple roots
\Delta_{Q,n}
. It gives a unique (up to unique isomorphism) pinned reductive group
\overline{{\bf G}}^{\vee}
over
\mathbf{Z}
, called the dual group of
(\overline{{\bf G}},n)
. In particular,
Y_{Q,n}
is the character lattice for
\overline{G}^{\vee}
and
\Delta_{Q,n}^{\vee}
the set of simple roots. Let

\overline{G}^{\vee}:=\overline{{\bf G}}^{\vee}(\mathbf{C})

be the associated complex dual group. The center of
\overline{G}^{\vee}
is

Z(\overline{G}^{\vee})={\rm Hom}(Y_{Q,n}/Y_{Q,n}^{sc},\mathbf{C}^{\times}).

Let
{\bf G}_{Q,n}
be the pinned split reductive group over
F
such that

{\bf G}_{Q,n}^{\vee}\simeq\overline{{\bf G}}^{\vee},

where
{\bf G}_{Q,n}^{\vee}
is the Langlands dual group of
{\bf G}_{Q,n}
. Then
{\bf G}_{Q,n}
is the principal endoscopic group of
(\overline{{\bf G}},n)
, and clearly
{\bf G}_{Q,n}={\bf G}
if
n=1
.

2.1.3.
L-group

In
[
We3
,
We6
]
, Weissman constructed the global L-group as well as the local L-group extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{G}^{\vee}_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{WD}_{F},}

which is compatible with the global L-group. Here
\text{WD}_{F}
is the Weil–Deligne group of
F
. His construction of L-group is functorial, and in particular it behaves well with respect to the restriction of
\overline{{\bf G}}
to parabolic subgroups. More precisely, let
{\bf M}\subset{\bf G}
be a Levi subgroup. By restriction, one has the
n
-fold cover
\overline{M}
of
M
. Then the L-groups
{}^{L}\overline{M}
and
{}^{L}\overline{G}
are compatible, i.e., there are natural homomorphisms of extensions:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{G}^{\vee}_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{WD}_{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{M}^{\vee}_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{WD}_{F}.}

For details on the construction and some properties regarding the L-group, we refer the reader to
[
We3
,
We6
,
GG
]
.

If
\eta_{n}=\mathbbm{1}
, then there exists a so-called distinguished genuine character

\chi_{\psi}:Z(\overline{T})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times},

depending on a nontrivial additive character
\psi
of
F
, such that the following properties hold:

\bullet

the character
\chi_{\psi}
takes values in
\mu_{4}\subseteq\mathbf{C}^{\times}
and is Weyl-invariant, i.e.,
\chi_{\psi}(w^{-1}\cdot\overline{t}\cdot w)=\chi_{\psi}(\overline{t})
for all
\overline{t}\in Z(\overline{T})
and
w
;

\bullet

\chi_{\psi}
gives rise to a splitting of
{}^{L}\overline{G}
over
W_{F}
, with respect to which one has an isomorphism

{}^{L}\overline{G}\simeq_{\chi_{\psi}}\overline{G}^{\vee}\times\text{WD}_{F}.

2.2.
The Clifford–Mackey theory

We recall some results by Gelbart and Knapp
[
GeKn1
,
GeKn2
]
(see also
[
Tad92
]
) on the Clifford–Mackey theory of restriction and induction of representations in the setting of
l
-adic groups (see
[
BZ1
]
). In particular, it applies to the
\mu_{n}
-cover of a linear algebraic group
G
discussed in §
2.1
. These results could be considered as a proper generalization of the finite group situation, as discussed in
[
CuRe1
, §11]
.

Let
M
be a totally disconnected group, i.e., a separable locally compact topological group whose open compact subgroups form a neighborhood base at the identity. For an admissible representation
\pi
and
e\in\mathbf{N}
, we will use

e\cdot\pi=\pi^{\oplus e}

interchangeably to denote the isotypic sum of
\pi
with multiplicity
e
. Let

H\subset M

be an open normal subgroup such that

M/H

is a finite abelian group. One of two operations we consider in this paper is the restriction of representations from
M
to
H
, while the other one is the induction from
H
to
M
. For every admissible representation
(\rho,V_{\rho})
of
H
and
g\in M
, there is the representation

{}^{g}\rho(h):=\rho(g^{-1}hg)

of
H
afforded by the same vector space
V_{\rho}
but with the action twisted. We denote

S_{\rho}:={\rm Stab}(\rho,M)=\left\{g\in M:{}^{g}\rho\simeq\rho\right\},

whenever the ambient group
M
is clear from the context. Denote by

\omega_{\rho}:Z(H)\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

the central character of
\rho
. Also, for every subgroup
K\subset M
and
g\in K
, we define a map

\varphi_{K,g}:K\longrightarrow K

by

\varphi_{g}(x):=[x,g]_{K},

where
[g,g^{\prime}]_{K}:=gg^{\prime}g^{-1}g^{\prime-1}
denotes the commutator of
K
.

Definition 2.1
.

For
H\subset K\subset M
, a representation
\sigma\in{\rm Irr}(K)
is called
H
-concentrated if

\left\{g\in K:\varphi_{K,g}^{-1}\big(Z(K)-{\rm Ker}(\omega_{\sigma}|_{Z(K)})\big)=\emptyset\right\}\subseteq H.

For every
\sigma\in{\rm Irr}(K)
, denote by
\Theta_{\sigma}
the Harish-Chandra character distribution of
\sigma
, which is conjugation invariant and can be represented by a locally-integrable function. The above definition is motivated from the following:

Lemma 2.2
.

Assume that
\sigma\in{\rm Irr}(K)
is
H
-concentrated. Then

{\rm supp}(\Theta_{\sigma})\subset H.

Proof.

Let
g\in K-H
. Since
\sigma
is
H
-concentrated by assumption, there exists
x\in K
such that

[x,g]\in Z(K)-{\rm Ker}(\omega_{\sigma}|_{Z(K)}).

One has

\Theta_{\sigma}(g)=\Theta_{\sigma}([x,g]\cdot g)=\omega_{\sigma}([x,g])\cdot\Theta_{\sigma}(g),

which shows that
\Theta_{\sigma}(g)=0
since
\omega_{\sigma}([x,g])\neq 1
. Thus,
{\rm supp}(\Theta_{\sigma})\subset H
.
∎

2.2.1.
Restriction

Let
\pi\in{\rm Irr}(M)
be an irreducible admissible representation of
M
. One has (see
[
GeKn1
, Lemma 2.1]
)

\pi|_{H}=\bigoplus_{g_{i}\in M/S_{\rho}}({}^{g_{i}}\rho)^{\oplus e},

where

–

\rho
is an irreducible representation of
H
that occurs in
\pi|_{H}
;

–

{}^{g}\rho
is the twist of
\rho
by
g\in M
, and elements in
\left\{{}^{g_{i}}\rho:g_{i}\in M/S_{\rho}\right\}
are mutually non-isomorphic representations of
H
;

–

e\in\mathbf{N}
is a certain ramification index (i.e., multiplicity) of
{}^{g}\rho
, and in particular
\rho^{\oplus e}
is an isotypic component of
\rho
in
\pi|_{H}
.

One has

S_{\rho}=S_{\rho^{\oplus e}},

and there is a natural irreducible representation of
S_{\rho}
on
\rho^{\oplus e}
. Moreover,

\pi={\rm Ind}_{S_{\rho}}^{M}(\rho^{\oplus e}).

Note that the irreducible representation of
S_{\rho}
on
\rho^{\oplus e}
depends on the
\pi
one started with. Regarding the restriction, there are two extreme cases:

–

if
S_{\rho}=M
, then
\pi|_{H}
is an isotypic sum of
\rho
, i.e.,
\pi|_{H}=\rho^{\oplus e}
;

–

if
S_{\rho}=H
, then
e=1
and in this case
\pi={\rm Ind}_{H}^{M}(\rho)
.

2.2.2.
Induction

Let
(\rho,V_{\rho})
be an irreducible admissible representation of
H
. The induced representation of
\rho
to
M
might not be irreducible. To obtain an irreducible representation of
M
from
\rho
, one approach is as follows.

Lemma 2.3
.

One can extend
\rho
to a representation
\rho^{\flat}
of a subgroup
H^{\flat}\subset M
(still acting on the same vector space
V_{\rho}
) such that
S_{\rho^{\flat}}=H^{\flat}
. In this case,
{\rm Ind}_{H^{\flat}}^{M}(\rho^{\flat})
is irreducible.

Proof.

The proof is the same as in
[
Mez04
, Page 89-90]
almost word for word, by noting that
M/H
is an abelian group of finite order by assumption.
∎

Using notations from the above lemma, one has

(2.6)

{\rm Ind}_{H}^{H^{\flat}}(\rho)=\bigoplus_{\chi\in{\rm Hom}(H^{\flat}/H,\mathbf{C}^{\times})}\chi\otimes\rho^{\flat},

where

\left\{\chi\otimes\rho^{\flat}:\chi\in{\rm Hom}(H^{\flat}/H,\mathbf{C}^{\times})\right\}

gives all the possible extensions of
\rho
to
H^{\flat}
. For a general
\chi\in{\rm Hom}(H^{\flat}/H,\mathbf{C}^{\times})
, one can extend it to a character

\chi^{\prime}:M/H\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}.

Then one has

{\rm Ind}_{H^{\flat}}^{M}(\chi\otimes\rho^{\flat})\simeq\chi^{\prime}\cdot{\rm Ind}_{H^{\flat}}^{M}(\rho^{\flat}),

which is an irreducible representation of
\overline{M}
. Therefore, we have a decomposition

{\rm Ind}_{H}^{M}(\rho)=\bigoplus_{\chi\in{\rm Hom}(H^{\flat}/H,\mathbf{C}^{\times})}{\rm Ind}_{H^{\flat}}^{M}(\chi\otimes\rho^{\flat})

of
{\rm Ind}_{H}^{M}(\rho)
into irreducible representations of
M
.

Proposition 2.4
.

Assume that every irreducible representation of
M
is
H
-concentrated. Let
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(H)
. Then the representation

{\rm Ind}_{H}^{M}(\rho)=\pi^{\oplus\left|H^{\flat}/H\right|}

is an isotypic sum with
\pi:={\rm Ind}_{H^{\flat}}^{H}(\rho^{\flat})\in{\rm Irr}(M)
given as in Lemma
2.3
.

Proof.

Again, the argument is essentially that of
[
Mez04
, Lemma 4.2]
which deals with covers of
{\rm GL}_{r}
; the desired result in that context is given explicitly in
[
Tak3
, Proposition 4.6]
. We sketch the argument for completeness.

Denote
\sigma:={\rm Ind}_{H^{\flat}}^{M}(\chi\otimes\rho^{\flat})
for an arbitrary
\chi\in{\rm Hom}(H^{\flat}/H,\mathbf{C}^{\times})
, we want to show
\Theta_{\sigma}=\Theta_{\pi}
. First, applying Lemma
2.2
to
K=M
shows that it suffices to prove
\Theta_{\sigma}(x)=\Theta_{\pi}(x)
for
x\in H
. Since

\Theta_{\sigma}(x)=\sum_{\gamma\in M/H^{\flat}}(\chi\cdot\Theta_{\rho^{\flat}})(\gamma^{-1}x\gamma),

it gives that for
x\in H
,

\Theta_{\sigma}(x)=\sum_{\gamma\in M/H^{\flat}}\Theta_{\rho^{\flat}}(\gamma^{-1}x\gamma)=\Theta_{\pi}(x).

This shows
\Theta_{\sigma}=\Theta_{\pi}
and thus

\sigma\simeq\pi,

see
[
BZ1
, Corollary 2.20]
. Since
\chi
is arbitrary, the proof is completed.
∎

If every irreducible representation of
M
is
H
-concentrated, then for given
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(H)
we denote by

\pi[\rho]\in{\rm Irr}(M)

the unique irreducible representation appearing in
{\rm Ind}_{H}^{M}(\rho)
, as in Proposition
2.4
. Since
\pi[\rho]\in{\rm Irr}(M)
is irreducible, by the discussion in §
2.2.1
and the Frobenius reciprocity, there exists
\tilde{\rho}\in{\rm Irr}(S_{\rho})

such that

(2.7)

\pi[\rho]={\rm Ind}_{S_{\rho}}^{M}(\tilde{\rho}),

where
\tilde{\rho}|_{H}=\rho^{\oplus e}
for some
e\in\mathbf{N}
. The relations among the two constructions are depicted in the following diagram

(2.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M,\pi[\rho])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S_{\rho},\tilde{\rho})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H^{\flat},\rho^{\flat})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\rho).}

Note that here
\tilde{\rho}
arises from the restriction of the irreducible representation
\pi[\rho]
. The left two arrows follow from the Clifford–Mackey theory. On the other hand, the right two arrows in (
2.8
) together give an analogue of the Stone–von Neumann construction of irreducible representations of Heisenberg groups, as illustrated by Proposition
2.4
.

Example 2.5
.

Consider the covering torus

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T,}

which is a Heisenberg type group. The center
Z(\overline{T})
is of finite index in
T
. Consider the pair
(M,H)=(\overline{T},Z(\overline{T}))
. Every irreducible representation of
\overline{T}
is
Z(\overline{T})
-concentrated. One has the diagram (
2.8
). To describe the right arrows of (
2.8
), let
\chi:Z(\overline{T})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be a genuine central character. We choose a maximal abelian subgroup
\overline{A}\subset\overline{T}
and an extension
\tilde{\chi}:\overline{A}\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
of
\chi
. Then
\pi[\chi]={\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}}^{\overline{T}}(\tilde{\chi})
is irreducible and independent of the choice of
\overline{A}
and
\tilde{\chi}
; also, one has

\dim\pi[\chi]=\sqrt{[\overline{T}:Z(\overline{T})]},

see
[
We1
,
We5
]
. For the left arrows in (
2.8
), we have

S_{\chi}=\overline{T}

and thus
\pi[\chi]|_{Z(\overline{T})}=\chi^{\oplus e}
, where
e=\sqrt{[\overline{T}:Z(\overline{T})]}
. We also use the notation

i(\chi):=\pi[\chi]

throughout the paper.

2.3.
Two special pictures

Henceforth, we consider covers arising from the Brylinski–Deligne framework exclusively. Recall that
T={\rm Hom}(X,F^{\times})=Y\otimes F^{\times}
.

Definition 2.6
.

A subgroup
\overline{H}\subset\overline{T}
is said to be associated with a sublattice
\mathbbm{L}\subset Y
if

\overline{H}=p^{-1}({\rm Im}(\iota)),

where
\iota:\mathbbm{L}\otimes F^{\times}\to T
is the map induced from the inclusion
\mathbbm{L}\subset Y
.

Write
\mathbbm{L}(\overline{H})
for the unique sublattice of
Y
associated with
\overline{H}
, whenever it exists. Assume that
Z({\bf G})
is connected i.e.,
X/X^{sc}
is a free
\mathbf{Z}
-module, or equivalently, the exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/X^{sc}}

of
\mathbf{Z}
-modules split. We thus have a
W
-equivariant embedding

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Hom}(X/X^{sc},\mathbf{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Hom}(X,\mathbf{Z})\simeq Y,}

the image of which we denote by
Y_{c}\subset Y
. In particular,
\overline{Z(G)}
is associated with the sublattice
Y_{c}\subset Y
, see Definition
2.6
. Let

i_{Q,n}:Y_{Q,n}\otimes F^{\times}\longrightarrow T=Y\otimes F^{\times}

be the isogeny induced from the inclusion
Y_{Q,n}\hookrightarrow Y
. It is known that (see
[
We1
]
)

Z(\overline{T})=p^{-1}({\rm Im}(i_{Q,n}));

that is,
Y_{Q,n}=\mathbbm{L}(Z(\overline{T}))
.
Now we define

Y_{z}:=Y_{Q,n}\cap Y_{c}

and let

i_{Q,n}^{z}:Y_{z}\otimes F^{\times}\longrightarrow T

be the map induced from the inclusion
Y_{z}\subset Y
.

The following has been observed and used in many places in the literature (for example see
[
KP
,
CO
,
Gan17
]
)

Lemma 2.7
.

Keeping notations as above, we have
Z(\overline{G})=\overline{Z(G)}\cap Z(\overline{T})
and

Z(\overline{G})=p^{-1}({\rm Im}(i_{Q,n}^{z})),

that is,
Y_{z}=\mathbbm{L}(Z(\overline{G}))
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the first equality. The inclusion

Z(\overline{G})\subset\overline{Z(G)}\cap Z(\overline{T})

is clear. On the other hand, since
\overline{G}=\overline{T}\cdot\overline{G}_{\rm der}
, we see that if
g\in\overline{Z(G)}\cap Z(\overline{T})
, then it centralizes
\overline{G}_{\rm der}
as well as
\overline{T}
. Therefore
\overline{Z(G)}\cap Z(\overline{T})\subset Z(\overline{G})
.
∎

We want to give a (hopefully proper) subgroup
\overline{H}\subset\overline{G}
such that every
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})
is
\overline{H}
-concentrated, and therefore

{\rm supp}(\Theta_{\pi})\subset\overline{H}.

For this purpose, we assume that

–

there exists
e_{s}\in Y
such that
\overline{G}=\overline{G}_{\rm der}\rtimes e_{s}(F^{\times})
;

–

one has
Y_{c}=\mathbf{Z}e_{c}
for some
e_{c}\in Y
.

We denote

n_{s}=n/\gcd(n,B_{Q}(e_{c},e_{s})).

Proposition 2.8
.

Keep notations as above and denote
\overline{G}_{n_{s}}:=\overline{G}_{\rm der}\rtimes\overline{e_{s}(F^{\times n_{s}})}
. Then every irreducible genuine representation
\pi
of
\overline{G}
is
\overline{G}_{n_{s}}
-concentrated, and thus
{\rm supp}(\Theta_{\pi})\subset\overline{G}_{n_{s}}
. Consequently,

{\rm Ind}_{\overline{G}_{n_{s}}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma)=m\cdot\pi

is an isotypic sum for every
\sigma\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G}_{n_{s}})
.

Proof.

Given with
e_{s}(b)\in e_{s}(F^{\times})\subset G
, we claim that if
[e_{c}(a),e_{s}(b)]=1
for all
a\in F^{\times}
, then
b\in F^{\times n_{s}}
. Noting that

[e_{c}(a),e_{s}(b)]=(a,b)_{n}^{B(e_{c},e_{s})},

the claim follows from the non-degeneracy of the Hilbert symbol. Therefore, if
b\notin F^{\times n_{s}}
, then there exists
e_{c}(a)
such that

1\neq[e_{c}(a),e_{s}(b)]\in\mu_{n}.

Thus, if
\overline{g}\notin\overline{G}_{\rm der}\rtimes\overline{e_{s}(F^{\times n_{s}})}
, then we have
\overline{g}=\overline{g}_{0}\cdot\overline{e_{s}(b)}
with
\overline{g}_{0}\in\overline{G}_{\rm der}
and
b\notin F^{\times n_{s}}
. It then gives

[\overline{g},\overline{e_{c}(a)}]=[\overline{g}_{0}\cdot\overline{e_{s}(b)},\overline{e_{c}(a)}]=[e_{c}(a),e_{s}(b)]\in\varphi_{\overline{G},\overline{g}}^{-1}\left(Z(\overline{G})-{\rm Ker}(\omega_{\pi}|_{Z(\overline{G})})\right),

and this shows that
\pi
is
\overline{G}_{n_{s}}
-concentrated. The rest is clear in view of Proposition
2.4
.
∎

Remark 2.9
.

Proposition
2.8
certainly does not give the sharpest bound for the support of all
\Theta_{\pi}
. For example, for Kazhdan–Patterson covers of
G={\rm GL}_{r}
, it is shown in
[
KP
, Proposition 0.1.4]
that

{\rm supp}(\Theta_{\pi})\subset Z(\overline{G})\cdot(\overline{G})^{n}

for every
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})
. This fact plays a crucial role in the metaplectic tensor product construction by Mezo
[
Mez04
]
, Takeda
[
Tak3
,
Tak4
]
and the functorial interpretation by Gan
[
Gan17
]
, when blocks in a covering Levi subgroup do not commute. In §
3.4
, we will discuss an analogue of the metaplectic tensor product (and in fact also a metaplectic restriction) for odd fold cover of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
, relying on Proposition
2.8
.

In this paper, we will consider restrictions and inductions from two types of normal subgroups of
\overline{G}
. Each of these two types has some advantage and we place focus on one of them in different contexts regarding different problems. To elaborate, in the first case we let

\overline{H}\subset\overline{G}

be a normal subgroup such that
\overline{G}/\overline{H}
is a finite abelian group, and that every genuine representation of
\overline{G}
is
\overline{H}
-concentrated. In the second case, we consider the subgroup

\overline{Z}_{0}\cdot\overline{G}_{0}\subset\overline{G},

which is normal of finite index, satisfying
\overline{Z}_{0}\subset\overline{Z(G)},\overline{G}_{\rm der}\subset\overline{G}_{0}
and that
\overline{Z}_{0}
commutes with
\overline{G}_{0}
, as depicted from the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{Z(G)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{G}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{Z}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{G}_{\rm der}.}

We have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{H}\subset\overline{G}\supset\overline{Z}_{0}\cdot\overline{G}_{0},}

and in general none of the two groups
\overline{H}
and
\overline{Z}_{0}\cdot\overline{G}_{0}
is contained in the other. These two types of groups play different roles in our paper as follows.

–

(First picture) Most often,
\overline{H}
is not equal to the
F
-rational points of an algebraic subgroup of
{\bf G}
, and thus the construction of dual group and L-group of
\overline{H}
does not apply directly. However, on the representation side, since
\overline{H}
contains the support of an arbitrary irreducible genuine representation of
\overline{G}
, it follows from Proposition
2.4
that the representation
{\rm Ind}_{\overline{H}}^{\overline{G}}(\rho)
is an isotypic sum of a certain
\pi[\rho]\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})
. In this case, the induction functor from
\overline{H}
to
\overline{G}
is simple. As mentioned in Remark
2.9
, some application of this includes the “metaplectic tensor product” construction of representations of the covering Levi subgroups of
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r}
as in
[
Mez04
,
Tak3
,
Tak4
,
Cai1
,
Gan17
]
.

–

(Second picture) The group
\overline{Z}_{0}\cdot\overline{G}_{0}
is almost a direct product of the commuting pair
\overline{Z}_{0}
and
\overline{G}_{0}
, as the intersection is a small finite group. For example, the two groups
\overline{Z(G)}
and
\overline{G}_{\rm der}
form a commuting pair inside
\overline{G}
. We consider both the restriction and induction of representations between
{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})
and
{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{Z}_{0}\cdot\overline{G}_{0})
. One important aspect of the restriction of representations from
\overline{G}
is how a local coefficients matrix for
\overline{G}
behaves with respect to the restriction to the subgroup
\overline{G}_{0}
. For the groups
