\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\chi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\omega_{\pi}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{G,z}}

Similarly, taking
J=Y_{0}
coupled with (
3.8
) give another diagram:

(3.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{WD}_{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{T}_{Y_{0},\natural}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{T}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{G}_{0},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{G,H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\chi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\chi_{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\chi_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{G_{0},H}}

where we have
\phi_{\chi_{0}}:=\phi_{\chi_{J}}
. The above two diagrams show the commutativity of (
3.6
). If
{}^{L}\overline{G}_{0}={}^{L}\overline{H}
, then
\phi_{\chi_{0}}
is uniquely determined by
\phi_{\chi}
, and thus

i(\chi)|_{\overline{T}_{0}}=e\cdot i(\chi_{0})

is an isotypic sum of
i(\chi_{0})\in\overline{T}_{0}
. This shows that
I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=e\cdot I(\chi_{0})
is an isotypic sum. The proof of (i) is completed.

For (ii), assume
\pi=I(\chi)\subset{\rm Ind}_{\overline{G}^{\dagger}}^{\overline{G}}(\tau\boxtimes I(\chi_{0}))
, it is easy to see that (
3.9
) commutes. On the other hand, using Frobenius reciprocity, one has
I(\chi_{0})\subset I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}
and thus
i(\chi_{0})\subset i(\chi)
. The commutativity of (
3.10
) then follows from Lemma
3.3
. This concludes the proof.
∎

3.4.
Metaplectic tensor product for
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}

In this subsection, we investigate for
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
an analogue of the metaplectic tensor product construction for Kazhdan–Patterson covers studied in
[
Mez04
,
Tak3
,
Tak4
,
Cai1
]
. The parameter side interpretation for such a construction is given in
[
Gan17
]
using the formalism of L-groups as in
[
We3
,
We6
]
. More precisely, consider a Levi subgroup

M={\rm GL}_{r_{1}}\times{\rm GL}_{r_{2}}\times...\times{\rm GL}_{r_{k}}\subset{\rm GL}_{r}.

It is well-known (by checking on the covering tori of
{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}
’s for example) that the blocks
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}\subset\overline{M}
do not commute. Thus, the representation of
\overline{M}
can not be simply reduced to that of each
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}
. However, given with
\pi_{i}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})
satisfying certain condition constrained by the central character of
Z(\overline{G})
, Mezo
[
Mez04
]
gave a natural construction of a representation of
\overline{M}
. Coarsely, the construction goes through several steps as follows:

(1)

For every
i
and
n\in\mathbf{N}
, we denote

{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}=\left\{g\in{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}:\det(g)\in F^{\times n}\right\}.

One can check that for
i\neq j
, the two covering groups
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
and
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{j}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
commute with each other. Hence one can define

\overline{M}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}:=\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{1}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\times_{\mu_{n}}\times...\times_{\mu_{n}}\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{k}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

For each
\pi_{i}\in\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}
, let
\sigma_{i}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle})
be an irreducible summand in the restriction of
\pi_{i}
. We have a representation

\sigma:=\sigma_{1}\boxtimes...\boxtimes\sigma_{k}

of
\overline{M}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\subset\overline{M}
.

(2)

Pick an irreducible genuine character
\omega:Z(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
such that

(3.11)

\omega=\sigma\text{ on }Z(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r})\cap\overline{M}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

This gives an irreducible representation
\omega\boxtimes\sigma
of
Z(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r})\cdot\overline{M}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
.

(3)

One extends
\omega\boxtimes\sigma
as much as possible to a representation
(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)^{\prime}
of a subgroup
\overline{M}^{\prime}
such that the Mackey’s irreducibility criteria are satisfied. We thus obtain

\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}:={\rm Ind}_{\overline{M}^{\prime}}^{\overline{M}}((\omega\boxtimes\sigma)^{\prime}),

which is an irreducible representation of
\overline{M}
.

It is shown in
[
Mez04
]
that the representation
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}
depends only on
\pi_{i}
’s and
\omega
, and is independent of the intermediate choices
\sigma_{i}
,
\overline{M}^{\prime}
and
(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)^{\prime}
. We highlight that such independence relies on the crucial property that for every
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M})
, one has

{\rm supp}(\Theta_{\pi})\subset Z(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r})\cdot\overline{M}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle};

thus, it follows from (the proof of) Proposition
2.4
that
{\rm Ind}_{\overline{M}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{M}}(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)
is an isotypic sum of an irreducible representation of
\overline{M}
, which is exactly
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}
in (3) above. This observation was explicated in
[
Tak3
, Proposition 4.6]
. In view of this, we could replace (3) above by the following:

(3)’

One has

{\rm Ind}_{\overline{M}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{M}}(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)=m\cdot\pi\text{ for a certain }\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}).

Now the representation
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}:=\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M})
is the one we seek.

The above construction gives a well-defined surjective map (for the surjectivity, see
[
Tak3
, Lemma 4.4]
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\otimes}:({\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{1}})\times...\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{k}})\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r})))^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}).}

The superscript
(-)^{\heartsuit}
indicates the subset of

(\pi_{1},...,\pi_{r},\omega)

satisfying the relation (
3.11
). For every character
\chi_{i}
of
F^{\times}
which is trivial on
F^{\times n}
, replacing
\pi
by
\pi\otimes(\chi_{i}\circ\det)
gives the same representation on
{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M})
, and this accounts for the non-injectivity of the map
\tilde{\otimes}
, see
[
Mez04
, Lemma 5.1]
. This metaplectic tensor product construction was further analyzed and refined in
[
Tak3
,
Tak4
]
especially in the global context, see also
[
Cai1
]
.

To proceed, in this subsection, we assume
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
is the type I (similitudes-splitting)
n
-fold cover associated with

Q(\alpha_{r}^{\vee})=-1\text{ and }Q(e_{0})=0,

see §
2.4.2
. One has
B(e_{0},e_{i})=1
for every
i
, and
B_{Q}(e_{c},e_{0})=Q(e_{c})=r
. Also,

(3.12)

Y_{Q,n}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\sum_{i=0}^{r}y_{i}e_{i}\in Y:\\
\bullet\quad n|(-2y_{i}+y_{0})\text{ for every }i,\\
\bullet\quad n|(y_{1}+y_{2}+...+y_{r}).\end{array}\right\}.

On the other hand,
Y_{Q,n}^{sc}
is spanned by

\left\{\alpha_{i,Q,n}^{\vee}=n_{2}\cdot\alpha_{i}^{\vee}:1\leqslant i\leqslant r-1\right\}\cup\left\{\alpha_{r,Q,n}^{\vee}=n\alpha_{r}^{\vee}\right\}.

We consider a partition

{\bf r}=(r_{1},r_{2},...,r_{k};r_{0})

of
r
with associated Levi subgroup

M_{\bf r}={\rm GL}_{r_{1}}\times{\rm GL}_{r_{2}}\times...\times{\rm GL}_{r_{k}}\times{\rm GSp}_{2r_{0}}.

For every
j\in\mathbf{N}
, let

{\rm GSp}_{2r}^{\left\langle j\right\rangle}=\left\{g\in{\rm GSp}_{2r}:e_{0}^{*}(g)\in F^{\times j}\right\}

be the subgroup of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
with similitudes lying in
F^{\times j}
, where

e_{0}^{*}:{\rm GSp}_{2r}\longrightarrow F^{\times}

is the similitude map.
Also define

M_{\bf r}^{\left\langle j\right\rangle}={\rm GL}_{r_{1}}\times{\rm GL}_{r_{2}}\times...\times{\rm GL}_{r_{k}}\times{\rm GSp}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle j\right\rangle}.

Accordingly, we have the covering subgroups
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{\left\langle j\right\rangle}\subset\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
and
\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle j\right\rangle}\subset\overline{M}_{\bf r}
. The covering blocks
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}
in
\overline{M}_{\bf r}
commute with each other; however, they may not commute with
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle j\right\rangle}
for general
j
.

Lemma 3.5
.

(i)

The covering group
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle j\right\rangle}
commutes with every
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}},1\leqslant i\leqslant k
if and only if
n|j
.

(ii)

For every genuine representation
\pi
of
\overline{M}_{\bf r}
, one has

{\rm supp}(\Theta_{\pi})\subset\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n_{(r)}\right\rangle}

for the character
\Theta_{\pi}
of
\pi
.

Proof.

For (i), we note that every element in
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}
can be written as
g_{0}\cdot e_{0}(a)
with
g_{0}\in\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r_{0}}
and
a\in F^{\times}
. We have block commutativity among the blocks
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}
and
\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r_{0}}
, thus
g_{0}
commutes with every element in
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}
. On the other hand,
e_{0}(a)
commutes with every
e_{\alpha_{i}}(a),1\leqslant i\leqslant r-1
, thus
e_{0}(a)
commutes with
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}
if and only if

[e_{0}(a),e_{i}(x)]=(a,x)_{n}^{B_{Q}(e_{0},e_{i})}

for every
e_{i}\in Y_{{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}}
and
x\in F^{\times}
. Since
B_{Q}(e_{0},e_{i})=1
, the above equality amounts to
a\in F^{\times n}
.
For (ii), the proof is the same as Proposition
2.8
by using the above fact that
g_{0}
commutes with
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}
for every
i
.
∎

Let
M_{\bf r}\subset{\rm GSp}_{2r}
be a Levi subgroup as above. We assume in the rest of this subsection that

\gcd(n,r_{0})=1

which implies
{\rm supp}(\Theta_{\pi_{0}})\subset\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
for every
\pi_{0}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})
, by Lemma
3.5
. It is also easy to check that

(3.13)

\mathbbm{L}(Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cap\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle})=\begin{cases}\mathbf{Z}(ne_{c})&\text{ if }2\nmid\gcd(n,r),\\
\mathbf{Z}(me_{c})&\text{ if }2|\gcd(n,r).\end{cases}

Also,

(3.14)

Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cdot\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}=\begin{cases}\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n_{(r)}\right\rangle}&\text{ if }2\nmid\gcd(n,r),\\
\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle 2n_{(r)}\right\rangle}&\text{ if }2|\gcd(n,r).\end{cases}

We will discuss the two cases separately: (1)
n
is odd, (2)
n
is even.

3.4.1.
The case of odd
n

For
n
odd, it is easy to obtain from (
3.12
) that

\left\{ne_{i}:1\leqslant i\leqslant r\right\}\cup\left\{n_{(r)}\cdot e_{c}\right\}

constitutes a
\mathbf{Z}
-basis for
Y_{Q,n}
. This shows that

\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{\vee}=\left\{(g,a)\in{\rm GSpin}_{2r+1}\times{\rm GL}_{1}:\lambda(g)=a^{\gcd(n,r)}\right\},

where

\lambda:{\rm GSpin}_{2r+1}\longrightarrow{\rm GL}_{1}

is the similitude map of
{\rm GSpin}_{2r+1}
associated with
ne_{c}\in Y_{Q,n}
. Since we have assumed
\gcd(n,r_{0})=1
, it gives

\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\vee}={\rm GSpin}_{2r_{0}+1}.

Now we describe a construction analogous to the metaplectic tensor product for
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r}
. In parallel, there are three steps as follows.

(S1)

Let
\pi_{i}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})
and
\pi_{0}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})
. Let
\sigma_{0}\subset\pi_{0}
be an irreducible summand in the restriction of
\pi_{0}
to
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
. The representation

\sigma:=\pi_{1}\boxtimes...\boxtimes\pi_{k}\boxtimes\sigma_{0}

is then a representation of
\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
.

(S2)

Let
\omega:Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be a central character such that

(3.15)

\omega=\sigma\text{ on }Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cap\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

We have
\omega\boxtimes\sigma\in{\rm Irr}(Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cdot\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle})
.

(S3)

One extends
\omega\boxtimes\sigma
to a representation
(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)^{\prime}
of a subgroup
\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\prime}\subset\overline{M}_{\bf r}
such that the representation

\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}:={\rm Ind}_{\overline{M}^{\prime}}^{\overline{M}}(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)^{\prime}

is irreducible. This representation
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}
is the sought metaplectic-tensor product of the
\pi_{i}
’s.

Similar to the case of Kazhdan–Patterson covers
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r}
discussed earlier, (S3) can be replaced by an equivalent statement as follows.

(S3’)

It follows from (ii) of Lemma
3.5
, the equality (
3.14
) and Proposition
2.8
that

{\rm Ind}_{{\rm Irr}(Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cdot\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle})}^{\overline{M}_{\bf r}}(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)=m\cdot\pi

is an isotypic sum of
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{\bf r})
. The representation
\pi
is just
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}
described in (S3) above. In particular, fixing
\omega\boxtimes\sigma
, the representation
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}
is independent of the choice of
\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\prime}
and the extension
(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)^{\prime}
.

We have to justify the above construction (S1)-(S3) (or (S1), (S2) and (S3’)) as follows.

Lemma 3.6
.

The representation
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}
is independent of the choice of
\sigma_{0}\subset\pi_{0}
, the choice of
\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\prime}
and the extension
(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Again, the argument is essentially the same as in
[
Mez04
]
. By the equivalence between (S3) and (S3’), it suffices to show the independence on the choice of constituent
\sigma_{0}
in (S1). Every summand in the restriction of
\pi_{0}
to
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}
is of the form
{}^{g}\pi_{0}
, where
g_{0}\in\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}/\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\simeq e_{0}(F^{\times})/e_{0}(F^{\times n})
. Taking
g=e_{0}(a),a\in F^{\times}
, since
\Theta_{\pi_{i}}\subset\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
for every
\pi_{i}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})
by Proposition
2.8
, we see that

{}^{g}\Theta_{\pi_{i}}=\Theta_{\pi_{i}}

and thus
{}^{g}\pi_{i}\simeq\pi_{i}
. It follows that

\pi_{1}\boxtimes...\boxtimes\pi_{k}\boxtimes{}^{g}\sigma_{0}={}^{g}\sigma.

If
\omega
and
\sigma
agrees on
Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cap\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
, then
\omega={}^{g}\sigma
on this intersection subgroup as well. Now we have

\displaystyle{\rm Ind}_{Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cdot\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{M}_{\bf r}}(\omega\boxtimes{}^{g}\sigma)

\displaystyle={\rm Ind}_{Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cdot\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{M}_{\bf r}}{}^{g}(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)

\displaystyle={}^{g}{\rm Ind}_{Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cdot\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{M}_{\bf r}}(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)

\displaystyle={\rm Ind}_{Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cdot\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{M}_{\bf r}}(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)

\displaystyle=e\cdot\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i},

where the last equality follows from Proposition
2.8
, see also (S3) and (S3’) above. This shows the independence on
{}^{g}\sigma_{0}
.
∎

We also have a reverse construction from
{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{\bf r})
to
\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})

as follows.

(RS1)

Let
\Pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{\bf r})
be an irreducible genuine representation. We pick a summand
\Pi_{0}\subset\Pi
in the restriction of
\Pi
to
\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
, it takes the form

\Pi_{0}=\pi_{1}\boxtimes\pi_{2}\boxtimes...\boxtimes\pi_{k}\boxtimes\sigma_{0}

with
\pi_{i}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})
and
\sigma_{0}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle})
. Every summand of
\Pi|_{\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}
is of the form

{}^{g}(\Pi_{0})={}^{g}\pi_{1}\boxtimes...\boxtimes{}^{g}\pi_{k}\boxtimes{}^{g}\sigma_{0}

for some
g\in\overline{M}_{\bf r}/\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}=e_{0}(F^{\times})/e_{0}(F^{\times n})
. Taking
g=e_{0}(a),a\in F^{\times}
, since we have
{\rm supp}(\Theta_{\pi_{i}})\subset Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cdot\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
by Proposition
2.8
, it follows that

{}^{g}\Theta_{\pi_{i}}=\Theta_{\pi_{i}}

as
g
commutes with every elements in
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
. This shows that
{}^{g}\pi_{i}\simeq\pi_{i}
. Thus, every constituent
\Pi_{0}^{\prime}\subset\Pi
is of the form

\Pi_{0}^{\prime}=\pi_{1}\boxtimes\pi_{2}\boxtimes...\boxtimes\pi_{k}\boxtimes{}^{g}\sigma_{0}

for some
g\in e_{0}(F^{\times})
. In particular, we have
\pi_{i}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})
uniquely determined by
\Pi
for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant k
.

(RS2)

Extend
\sigma_{0}
as much as possible to a representation
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}
of a subgroup
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\prime}\subset\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}
, such that the induced representation

\pi_{0}:={\rm Ind}_{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\prime}}^{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}}(\sigma_{0}^{\prime})

is irreducible. This gives the desired representation
\pi_{0}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})
. Using the crucial fact that
\Theta_{\pi}\subset\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
for every
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})
, it follows from Proposition
2.8
that

{\rm Ind}_{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}}(\sigma_{0})=m\cdot\pi_{0},

as an isotypic sum of
\pi_{0}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})
. Thus,
\pi_{0}
is the desired representation.

Proposition 3.7
.

Keep notations as above.

(i)

One has
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}\simeq\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}^{\prime}
if and only if
\pi_{i}\simeq\pi_{i}^{\prime}
for every
0\leqslant i\leqslant k
.

(ii)

Every representation
\Pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{\bf r})
is a metaplectic tensor product of the form
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}
, where the
\pi_{i}
’s are uniquely determined by
(RS1)-(RS2)
.

Proof.

For (i), it suffices to prove the “only if” part. Consider the restriction of
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}\simeq\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}^{\prime}
to
\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}
, we see that
there is
g\in e_{0}(F^{\times})
and
\sigma_{0}\subset\pi_{0},\sigma_{0}^{\prime}\subset\pi_{0}^{\prime}
such that

{}^{g}(\pi_{1}\boxtimes...\boxtimes\pi_{k}\boxtimes\sigma_{0})=\pi_{1}^{\prime}\boxtimes...\boxtimes\pi_{k}^{\prime}\boxtimes\sigma_{0}^{\prime},

where the left hand side is in fact
\pi_{1}\boxtimes...\boxtimes\pi_{k}\boxtimes{}^{g}\sigma_{0}
. It follows that

\pi_{i}\simeq\pi_{i}^{\prime}

for every
1\leqslant i\leqslant k
, and also
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}={}^{g}\sigma_{0}
. On the other hand, we have

{\rm Ind}_{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}}(\sigma_{0}^{\prime})=e^{\prime}\cdot\pi_{0}^{\prime}\text{ and }{\rm Ind}_{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}}(\sigma_{0})=e\cdot\pi_{0}

for some
e,e^{\prime}\in\mathbf{N}
. Since

{\rm Ind}_{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}}(\sigma_{0}^{\prime})={\rm Ind}_{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}}({}^{g}\sigma_{0})={}^{g}{\rm Ind}_{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}}(\sigma_{0})={\rm Ind}_{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}}(\sigma_{0}),

it follows that
e=e^{\prime}
and
\pi_{0}\simeq\pi_{0}^{\prime}
.

It is clear from the above argument that every
\Pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{\bf r})
is a metaplectic tensor product with
\pi_{i}
determined as above. This gives (ii) and completes the proof.
∎

The construction in (S1)-(S3) gives a well-defined map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\otimes}_{i}:(\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})))^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{\bf r}).}

The superscript
(-)^{\heartsuit}
indicates the subset of

(\pi_{1},...,\pi_{k},\pi_{0},\omega)

satisfying the equality (
3.15
) in (S2).
On the other hand, when restricted to
Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\subset\overline{M}_{\bf r}
, the representation
\Pi
acts by a character

\omega_{\Pi}:Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}.

Thus, the construction in (RS1)-(RS2) gives a “metaplectic restriction”

\tilde{\rm R}:{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{\bf r})\longrightarrow\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}))\Big)^{\heartsuit}

given by

\tilde{\rm R}(\Pi)=(\pi_{1},...,\pi_{k},\pi_{0};\omega_{\Pi}).

The following is immediate from Proposition
3.7
.

Theorem 3.8
.

Assume
n
is odd and
\gcd(n,r_{0})=1
. Then the map

\tilde{\otimes}_{i}:\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}))\Big)^{\heartsuit}\longrightarrow{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{\bf r})

is a bijection with the inverse given by
\tilde{\rm R}
, i.e.
\tilde{\rm R}\circ\tilde{\otimes}_{i}={\rm id}=\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\circ\tilde{\rm R}
.

It is expected that for the parameters associated with the representations involved in the constructions
\tilde{\otimes}
and
\tilde{\rm R}
, one should have a corresponding identification. To proceed, we note that the dual group of
\overline{M}_{\bf r}
is

\displaystyle\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\vee}

\displaystyle=\left\{(g_{1},g_{2},...,g_{k},g_{0};z):\ \lambda(g_{0})\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{k}\det(g_{i})=z^{g(n,r)}\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}\times{\rm GSpin}_{2r_{0}+1}\times{\rm GL}_{1}(\mathbf{C}).

Assuming that we have a local Langlands correspondence for the covers, then associated with
\pi_{i}
’s and
\omega
we have

\phi_{i}:\text{WD}_{F}\longrightarrow{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}\text{ for }1\leqslant i\leqslant k,\quad\phi_{0}:\text{WD}_{F}\longrightarrow{\rm GSpin}_{2r_{0}+1}

parametrizing
\pi_{i}
’s for
0\leqslant i\leqslant k
, and

\phi_{\omega}:\text{WD}_{F}\longrightarrow{\rm GL}_{1}(\mathbf{C})

parametrizing
\omega
. This gives a parameter

(\phi_{1}\times...\times\phi_{k}\times\phi_{0})\times\phi_{\omega}:\text{WD}_{F}\longrightarrow\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}\times{\rm GSpin}_{2r_{0}+1}\right)\times\mathbf{C}^{\times}.

Lemma 3.9
.

Assume the local Langlands correspondence with desiderata. Then the compatibility condition in (
3.15
) is equivalent to

\prod_{i=1}^{k}\det(\phi_{i})\cdot\lambda(\phi_{0})=\phi_{\omega}^{\gcd(n,r)}.

Thus, the parameter
(\phi_{1}\times...\times\phi_{k}\times\phi_{0})\times\phi_{\omega}
factors through
\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\vee}
.

Proof.

It follows from (
3.13
) that

Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cap\overline{M}_{\bf r}=\overline{Z({\rm GSp}_{2r})^{n}},

where
Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})=\overline{Z({\rm GSp}_{2r})^{n_{(r)}}}
. If we denote by
e_{i,c}\in Y_{{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}}
the natural generator of
Y_{{\rm GL}_{r_{i}},c}
, then

Z(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})=\overline{Z({\rm GL}_{r_{i}})^{n}}\text{ and }Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})=\overline{Z({\rm GSp}_{2r_{0}})^{n}}.

From the desiderata (i) of Conjecture (
3.2
), the center character

\omega_{i}:Z(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

of
\pi_{i}
has a parameter

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\det(\phi_{i}):\text{WD}_{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\vee}/[\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\vee},\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\vee}]\simeq\mathbf{C}^{\times},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\det}

where the second map is the determinant map of
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\vee}\simeq{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}
. The center character

\omega_{0}:Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

of
\pi_{0}
has a parameter

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda(\phi_{0}):\text{WD}_{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\vee}/[\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\vee},\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\vee}]\simeq\mathbf{C}^{\times},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}

where the second map is the similitude map of
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\vee}\simeq{\rm GSpin}_{2r_{0}+1}
. The identity is thus clear.
∎

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{M}:\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i=1}^{k}\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\vee}\times\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\vee}\times\mathbf{C}^{\times}}

be the natural inclusion. Assuming the local Langlands correspondence, we expect that the following diagram

(3.16)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{WD}_{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i=1}^{k}\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\vee}\times\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\vee}\times\mathbf{C}^{\times}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\vee}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{1}\times...\times\phi_{k}\times\phi_{0}\times\phi_{\omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\Pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{M}}

commutes, when
\phi_{\Pi}
is related to
\phi_{i}
’s and
\phi_{\omega}
via the two metaplectic constructions
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}
and
\tilde{\rm R}
. The following is in analogue with
[
Gan17
, §3.2, Conjecture]
, and in fact is simpler.

Conjecture 3.10
.

Keep notations as above.

(i)

The metaplectic tensor product
\tilde{\otimes}_{i}\pi_{i}
defined above has an associated
L
-parameter exactly
(\phi_{1}\times...\times\phi_{k}\times\phi_{0})\times\phi_{\omega}
, which is a parameter factoring through
i_{M}
and thus is valued in
\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\vee}
.

(ii)

The metaplectic restriction
\tilde{\rm R}(\Pi)
above has
L
-parameter exactly
i_{M}\circ\phi_{\Pi}
.

Proposition 3.11
.

Conjecture
3.10
holds when the
\pi_{i}
’s and
\Pi
are genuine principal series.

Proof.

We denote by

\overline{T}_{r_{i}}\subset\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}\text{ and }\overline{T}_{r_{0}}\subset\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}

the covering tori of the blocks
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}
and
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}
respectively. Every genuine principal series
\pi_{i}=I(i(\chi_{i}))\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})
is associated with

\chi_{i}:Z(\overline{T}_{r_{i}})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times},

a central genuine character; similarly, a genuine principal series
\pi_{i}=I(i(\chi_{0}))\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})
is associated with

\chi_{0}:Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}.

Denote by
Y_{r_{i}}
the character lattice of
{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}
, also by
Y_{r_{0}}
that of
{\rm GSp}_{2r_{0}}
.

We have

\mathbbm{L}(Z(\overline{T}_{r_{i}}))=nY_{r_{i}},1\leqslant i\leqslant k

and

\mathbbm{L}(Z(\overline{T}_{r_{0}}))=nY_{r_{0}}^{sc}\oplus\mathbf{Z}(ne_{c}),

where
Y_{r_{0}}^{sc}\subset Y_{r_{0}}
is the coroot lattice of
{\rm GSp}_{2r_{0}}
. Since

\mathbbm{L}(Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}))=\mathbf{Z}n_{(r)}e_{c}\text{ and }\mathbbm{L}(Z(\overline{T}))=nY^{sc}\oplus\mathbf{Z}\cdot n_{(r)}e_{c},

it follows that

\mathbbm{L}(Z(\overline{T}))=\mathbf{Z}n_{(r)}e_{c}+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbbm{L}(Z(\overline{T}_{r_{i}}))+\mathbbm{L}(Z(\overline{T}_{r_{0}}))\right).

Thus giving a genuine character
\chi:Z(\overline{T})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
is equivalent to giving

(\chi_{r_{1}},...,\chi_{r_{k}},\chi_{r_{0}};\omega)\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{T}_{r_{1}}))\times...\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{T}_{r_{k}}))\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{T}_{r_{0}}))\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}))

such that
\chi_{r_{1}}\boxtimes...\chi_{r_{k}}\boxtimes\chi_{r_{0}}
and
\omega
agree on

\left(Z(\overline{T}_{r_{1}})\times_{\mu_{n}}...\times Z(\overline{T}_{r_{k}})\times_{\mu_{n}}Z(\overline{T}_{r_{0}})\right)\cap Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}).

In particular,
\chi_{r_{i}},0\leqslant i\leqslant k
and
\omega
are obtained from restricting
\chi
to the corresponding subgroups of
Z(\overline{T})
. The equality between the parameter
\chi_{\Pi}
of
\Pi:=\Pi(\chi)
and
\phi_{1}\times...\times\phi_{k}\times\phi_{0}\times\phi_{\omega}
then easily follows from functoriality of the local Langlands correspondence for covering tori with respect to restriction, as discussed in §
3.3
.
∎

3.4.2.
Remarks on the even
n
case

The metaplectic tensor product
\tilde{\otimes}
and metaplectic restriction
\tilde{\rm R}
for odd fold cover of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
do not generalize naively in the even fold cover case. Now, assuming
2|n
, we highlight the obstacles to the constructions in
\tilde{\otimes}
and
\tilde{\rm R}
. Recall that we assume
\gcd(n,r_{0})=1
.

Let
n=2m
. It follows from Proposition
2.8
that
{\rm supp}(\Theta_{\pi_{i}})\subset\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\left\langle m\right\rangle}
. However, there may exist
\pi_{i}
such that

{\rm supp}(\Theta_{\pi_{i}})\nsubseteq\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

For such
\pi_{i}
, we may have
{}^{g}\pi_{i}\neq\pi_{i}
. This gives an obstruction to validating the constructions both in (S1)-(S3) and (RS1)-(RS2), as the seen from proof of Lemma
3.6
. In fact, if
2|\gcd(n,r)
, then it follows from (
3.14
) that

Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cdot\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}=\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{{\left\langle 2n_{(r)}\right\rangle}},

which however is not equal to
\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{{\left\langle n_{(r)}\right\rangle}}
. In this case,

(3.17)

{\rm Ind}_{Z(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r})\cdot\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\left\langle n\right\rangle}}^{\overline{M}_{\bf r}}=\bigoplus_{j\in F^{\times}/2}{\rm Ind}_{\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{{\left\langle n_{(r)}\right\rangle}}}^{\overline{M}_{\bf r}}(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)_{j}=\bigoplus_{j}e_{j}\cdot\Pi_{j},

where
(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)_{j}
is an extension of
(\omega\boxtimes\sigma)
and

F^{\times}/2:=e_{0}(F^{\times n_{(r)}})/e_{0}(F^{\times 2n_{(r)}})\simeq F^{\times}/F^{\times 2}.

The last equality in (
3.17
) follows from Proposition
2.8
with
\Pi_{j}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{\bf r})
. This gives another obstruction in the case
2|\gcd(n,r)
.

We also remark that the dual side seems to be deceivable when
n
is even, and this provides a counterexample where the naive version of functoriality might fail for general
n
. We illustrate this below according to the parity of
\gcd(n,r)
.

In the first case, we assume
n=2m
with
2\nmid\gcd(n,r)
. Setting

v_{0}:=n_{(r)}\cdot e_{c}\text{ and }v_{i}:=me_{i}+v_{0}/2,1\leqslant i\leqslant r,

It is easy to check that

Y_{Q,n}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathbf{Z}v_{i}

with
Y_{Q,n}^{sc}
spanned by
\left\{m\alpha_{i}^{\vee}:1\leqslant i\leqslant r-1\right\}\cup\left\{n\alpha_{r}^{\vee}\right\}
. Thus, for
2\nmid\gcd(n,r)
one has

\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{\vee}={\rm GSp}_{2r}.

It follows that (given with the partition
{\bf r}=(r_{1},...,r_{k},r_{0})
of
r
)

\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\vee}\simeq\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}\times{\rm GSp}_{2r_{0}}=\prod_{i=1}^{k}\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\vee}\times\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\vee}.

Analyzing the discussion in (S1)-(S3) and (RS1)-(RS2), one can define a
correspondence
between
\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}})\times{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}})
and
{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{\bf r})
. However, there is no
map
(instead of correspondence) from one to the other. This indicates that a naive analogue of the functoriality might fail when considering representations of
\overline{M}_{\bf r}
and those of its covering blocks inside.

In the second case, we assume
2|\gcd(n,r)
, then it follows that

Y_{Q,n}=Y_{Q,n}^{sc}\oplus\mathbf{Z}\cdot v_{0},

where
v_{0}=n_{(r)}e_{c}
as above. In this case,

\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{\vee}={\rm PGSp}_{2r}\times{\rm GL}_{1}

and thus one has a map

\prod_{i=1}^{k}\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r_{i}}^{\vee}\times\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r_{0}}^{\vee}\longrightarrow\overline{M}_{\bf r}^{\vee}.

However, this map does not seem to be associated from the representation-side by a “metaplectic tensor product” construction, the same as we remarked above for the
n=2m,2\nmid\gcd(n,r)
case.

3.4.3.
General metaplectic tensor product

From the example of Kazhdan–Patterson covers of
{\rm GL}_{r}
and the odd-fold cover of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
discussed in this subsection, we see that the formulation of the metaplectic tensor product construction could be carried out in quite general setting, provided the following constraints are satisfied. To elaborate, assume for simplicity that

M=M_{1}\times M_{2}\times...\times M_{k}

is a Levi subgroup of
G
. We like to have normal subgroups

M_{j}^{\natural}\subset M_{j},1\leqslant j\leqslant k

such that every
M_{j}/M_{j}^{\natural}
is a finite abelian group and satisfy the following two conditions:

(i)

the blocks
\overline{M}_{j}^{\natural},1\leqslant j\leqslant k
commute with each other,

(ii)

{\rm supp}(\Theta_{\pi})\subset\prod_{j}\overline{M}_{j}^{\natural}
for every
\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M})
.

If (i) and (ii) are satisfied, then the process described in steps (S1)–(S3) in §
3.4.1
can be applied and one has a metaplectic tensor product construction following the same procedure. Note that the two conditions (i) and (ii) counteract against each other: for (i) it is desirable that
M_{j}^{\natural}
is a smaller subgroup of
M_{j}
; on the other hand, for (ii) to be satisfied the group
M_{j}^{\natural}
should be closer to
M_{j}
.
Thus, a family
\left\{\overline{M}_{j}^{\natural}\right\}_{j}
satisfying both (i) and (ii) is a balancing choice, if it exists.

For
G={\rm GL}_{r}
and
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
, the natural candidate for
M_{j}^{\natural}
is essentially the subgroup of elements with determinant or similitude lying in
F^{\times n}
, respectively. Another reason of taking such
M_{j}^{\natural}
is to ensure we have a natural (even conjectural) interpretation of the tensor product construction on the dual side.

4.
Local coefficients matrix under restriction

4.1.
Local coefficients matrix

Let
\overline{B}=\overline{T}U
be the Borel subgroup of
\overline{G}
, where
U
is identified as a subgroup of
\overline{G}
via the canonical splitting given by
e_{\alpha}(x)\mapsto\overline{e}_{\alpha}(x)
. Let
\psi:F\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be a nontrivial character. By abuse of notation, denote by

\psi:U\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

the unique character such that

\psi(e_{\alpha}(x))=\psi(x)

for every
\alpha\in\Delta
and
x\in F
. Let
(\pi,V_{\pi})\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G})
be a genuine irreducible representation of
\overline{G}
. A functional

\lambda:V_{\pi}\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}

is called a
\psi
-Whittaker functional if

\lambda(\pi(u)v)=\psi(u)\cdot\lambda(v)

for all
u\in U
and
v\in V_{\pi}
. Denote by
\text{Wh}_{\psi}(\pi)
the space of
\psi
-Whittaker functionals for
\pi
.
It follows from the work of Patel
[
Pate
]
, which generalizes
[
MW1
]
by Mœglin-Waldspurger, that

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi)<\infty\text{ for every }\pi\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{G}).

See
[
KP
, Theorem I.5.2 (i)]
also for a proof of this finite dimensionality for covers of
{\rm GL}_{r}
.

We briefly recall the definition of a local coefficients matrix as in
[
Szp6
,
GSS2
]
. Let

{\bf P}={\bf M}{\bf N}\subset{\bf G}

be a parabolic subgroup of
{\bf G}
associated with
\theta\subset\Delta
.
Let
\sigma\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M})
. We have the parabolic subgroups
\overline{P}=\overline{M}N\subset\overline{G}
and
\overline{P}_{0}=\overline{M}_{0}N\subset\overline{G}_{0}
, see §
2.3.1
.

By dualizing, the intertwining operator
T(w,\sigma)
in (
2.11
), whenever holomorphic at
\sigma
, gives a linear map between finite dimensional vector spaces:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(w,\sigma)^{*}:\ {\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}^{\prime}}^{\overline{G}}({}^{w}\sigma))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma)).}

Let
\psi_{M}
be the restriction of
\psi
to the unipotent radical
U_{M}
of the Borel subgroup
TU_{M}
of
M
. Here
\psi_{M}
and
w
are compatible (in the sense of
[
Sha4
, Page 51]
). We have a natural isomorphism (see
[
CS
,
Rod1
]
,
[
Sha2
, §3]
or
[
Sha4
, §3.3]
)

J(\sigma):{\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M}}(\sigma)\longrightarrow{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma))

given by

J(\sigma)(\lambda)(f)=\int_{N^{\prime}}\lambda(f(\overline{w}_{0}^{-1}n^{\prime}))\cdot\psi(n^{\prime})dn^{\prime},

where
N^{\prime}=w_{0}N^{-}w_{0}^{-1}
with
w_{0}=w_{l}\cdot w_{l,M}
. Here
w_{l}
(resp.
w_{l,M}
) is the longest
Weyl element in the Weyl group
W=W(T,G)
(resp.
W_{M}=W(T,M)
).

Similarly, one has

J({}^{w}\sigma):{\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M^{\prime}}}({}^{w}\sigma)\longrightarrow{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}^{\prime}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma)).

At the same time, since
V_{{}^{w}\sigma}=V_{\sigma}
, we also have a canonical identification

\iota:{\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M}}(\sigma)={\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M^{\prime}}}({}^{w}\sigma),

which we briefly explained as follows. First, note that
l\in{\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M}}(\sigma)
if and only if

l(\sigma(u)(v))=\psi_{M}(u)\cdot v

for all
u\in U_{M},v\in V_{\sigma}
. For every
u^{\prime}\in U_{M^{\prime}}
one has
w^{-1}u^{\prime}w\in U_{M}
and

\psi_{M^{\prime}}(u^{\prime})=\psi(u^{\prime})=\psi(w^{-1}u^{\prime}w)=\psi_{M}(w^{-1}u^{\prime}w).

It is verified easily (cf.
[
GSS2
, §3.2]
) that

l({}^{w}\sigma(u^{\prime})(v))=l(\sigma(w^{-1}u^{\prime}w)(v))=\psi_{M}(w^{-1}u^{\prime}w)\cdot v=\psi_{M^{\prime}}(u^{\prime})\cdot v

for all
u^{\prime}\in U_{M^{\prime}}
and
v\in V_{{}^{w}\sigma}=V_{\sigma}
. This shows that
l\in{\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M^{\prime}}}({}^{w}\sigma)
and gives the canonical identification
\iota
.

This gives a natural isomorphism of vector spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(w,\sigma)=J({}^{w}\sigma)\circ\iota\circ J(\sigma)^{-1}:\ {\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}^{\prime}}^{\overline{G}}({}^{w}\sigma)).}

We therefore obtain an endomorphism

(4.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma)^{*}=T(w,\sigma)^{*}\circ\mathcal{C}(w,\sigma):\ {\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma))}

of the finite dimensional vector space
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma))
. A local coefficients matrix associated to
(\overline{P},w,\sigma)
is the matrix

\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}(w,\sigma)

representing
\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma)^{*}
with respect to an ordered basis
\mathfrak{B}\subset{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma))
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Fix
\sigma\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M})
. There is a local coefficients matrix
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}(w,\sigma)
of the form

\bigoplus_{\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{i}}(w,\sigma_{i}),

where
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{i}}(w,\sigma_{i})
is a local coefficients matrix associated with the triple
(\overline{P}_{0},w,\sigma_{i})
and a basis
\mathfrak{B}_{i}
of
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\overline{G}_{0}}(\sigma_{i}))
. In particular, for the two invariants trace and determinant, one has

(4.2)

{\rm Tr}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}(w,\sigma))=\sum_{\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}}{\rm Tr}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{i}}(w,\sigma_{i})),\quad{\rm det}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}(w,\sigma))=\prod_{\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}}{\rm det}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{i}}(w,\sigma_{i})).

Proof.

Clearly, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma)^{*}
decomposes as a direct sum of the operators
\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma_{i})^{*}
. First, we have an isomorphism of vector spaces

(4.3)

{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{G}}(\sigma))\simeq\bigoplus_{\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}}{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\overline{G}_{0}}(\sigma_{i})),

since
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(-)
is the dual of the twisted Jacquet module, which depends only on
\psi:U\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
and is an exact functor.
It follows from Lemma
2.14
that

T(w,\sigma)^{*}=\bigoplus_{\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}}T(w,\sigma_{i})^{*}.

Now we consider the decomposition of
\mathcal{C}(w,\sigma)
over the
\mathcal{C}(w,\sigma_{i})
’s with respect to the decomposition in (
4.3
). For this purpose, we note first for each
\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}
the isomorphism

J(\sigma_{i}):{\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M}}(\sigma_{i})\longrightarrow{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\overline{G}_{0}}(\sigma_{i}))

and the decomposition

{\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M}}(\sigma)=\bigoplus_{\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}}{\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M}}(\sigma_{i}).

It is easy to see from the defining formula of
J(\sigma_{i})
that

J(\sigma)=\bigoplus_{\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}}J(\sigma_{i}).

Similar decomposition for
J({}^{w}\sigma)
holds. Lastly, the canonical identification
\iota
also decomposes:

\iota=\bigoplus_{\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}}\iota_{\sigma_{i}},

where
\iota_{\sigma_{i}}:{\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M}}(\sigma_{i})\to{\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M^{\prime}}}({}^{w}\sigma_{i})
is the canonical identification.
Thus, the isomorphism
\mathcal{C}(w,\sigma)
decomposes as a direct product of the
\mathcal{C}(w,\sigma_{i})
’s. Hence we have

\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma)^{*}=\bigoplus_{\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}}\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma_{i})^{*},

which gives the desired result.
∎

The above proposition shows that the invariants associated to a local coefficients matrix
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}(w,\sigma)
is a priori determined by all the local coefficients matrices
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{i}}(w,\sigma_{i})
arising from the restriction of
\sigma
to
\overline{M}_{0}
. This has a striking consequence even in the case where
\sigma=i(\chi)
is a genuine representation of
\overline{T}\subset\overline{G}
, where
\chi
is a central character of
Z(\overline{T})
. Indeed, in this case, the two invariants

{\rm Tr}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}(w_{\alpha},\sigma))\text{ and }{\rm det}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}(w_{\alpha},\sigma))

associated with a simple reflection
w_{\alpha}
are computed in
[
GoSz
,
Szp6
,
GSS1
,
GSS2
]
. They are expressed in terms of the Plancherel measure and gamma or metaplectic-gamma factors associated with
i(\chi)
; in particular, these two invariants depend only on the central character
\chi
.

It is expected that
{\rm Tr}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{i}}(w_{\alpha},\sigma_{i}))
and
{\rm det}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{i}}(w_{\alpha},\sigma_{i}))
depend on
\sigma_{i}
only. Note that even if
\sigma\in\overline{G}
is an unramified representation, it is possible to have a ramified constituent
\sigma_{i}\subset\sigma|_{\overline{T}_{0}}
. In this case, the equalities in (
4.2
) entail that there is a cancellation of the ramified information encoded in the ramified constituents of
\sigma|_{\overline{T}_{0}}
. A prototype of such examples is the pair
(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(n)},\overline{{\rm SL}}_{2}^{(n)})
with even
n
, see
[
GSS2
, §8–§9]
.

A special case is the following.

Corollary 4.2
.

Let
\sigma\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M})
. Assume that
\sigma|_{\overline{M}_{0}}=\sigma_{0}^{\oplus m}
is an isotypic sum of
\sigma_{0}\in{\rm Irr}_{\rm gen}(\overline{M}_{0})
. Then

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M}}(\sigma)=m\cdot\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi_{M}}(\sigma)

and moreover

\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{0}}(w,\sigma_{0})^{\oplus m}
is a local coefficients matrix for
\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma)^{*}
, where
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{0}}(w,\sigma_{0})
is a local coefficients matrix for
\mathcal{T}(w,\sigma_{0})^{*}
. In particular,

{\rm Tr}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}(w,\sigma))=m\cdot{\rm Tr}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{0}}(w,\sigma_{0})),\quad{\rm det}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}}(w,\sigma))={\rm det}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{B}_{0}}(w,\sigma_{0}))^{m}.

One immediate application of Corollary
4.2
is the computation of the multiplicity
m
by considering the Whittaker dimensions. Indeed, such multiplicity (or ramification index
e
in the discussion of §
2.2.1
) is most often a subtle quantity to explicate.

4.2.
Genuine principal series

We assume
p\nmid n
, and fix a splitting
s_{K}
of
\overline{G}
over
K={\bf G}(O)
. We consider a
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series
I(\chi)=I(i(\chi))
of
\overline{G}
, where

\chi:Z(\overline{T})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

is an unramified character, i.e., it is trivial on
Z(\overline{T})\cap K
. In this case, as discussed in §
2.5
, the group

\overline{A}=Z(\overline{T})\cdot{\bf T}(O)\subset\overline{T}

is a maximal abelian subgroup. There is a unique extension also denoted by

\tilde{\chi}:\overline{A}\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times},

which is trivial on
{\bf T}(O)
. From now on, we will take

i(\chi)={\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}}^{\overline{T}}(\tilde{\chi})

for this
\tilde{\chi}
, which gives the unramified principal series
I(\chi)=I(i(\chi))
.

It follows from Theorem
2.24
that one has

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}\bigoplus_{\omega_{\gamma,j}\in\mathscr{E}(\chi,{}^{\gamma}\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))}I(\omega_{\gamma,j}),

where generically
I(\omega_{\gamma,j})
is a multiplicity-free genuine principal series of
\overline{G}_{0}
appearning in the double sum of the right hand side. We note that

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\omega_{\gamma,j}))=\left|\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}\right|

for every
\omega_{\gamma,j}\in\mathscr{E}(\chi,{}^{\gamma}\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))
, where

\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}:=Y_{0}/Y_{0,Q,n}.

One the other hand, we have

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\chi))=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}\right|.

The relations among various quotients of lattices are illustrated as follows.

(4.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0,Q,n}/(Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y/Y_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0}/(Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

If
Z(\overline{T}_{0})\subset Z(\overline{T})
, or equivalently
Y_{0,Q,n}=Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}
by Corollary
2.12
, then the above diagram becomes

(4.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Since
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}
is the “moduli space” of
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\chi))
, i.e., there is a natural parametrization of the Whittaker space by
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}
(see
[
GSS2
, §3.4]
), we see that there is a decomposition of
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}
into the disjoint union of the fibers of
f
, and thus a corresponding decomposition of
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\chi))
.
Each fibre of
f
is a torsor over
\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}
and in particular of the same size as

\left|\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}\right|=\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\chi|_{Z(\overline{T}_{0})})).

This decomposition is compatible with the fact that
I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=I(\chi|_{Z(\overline{T}_{0})})^{\oplus\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|}
is an isotypic sum, see Corollary
2.25
and Corollary
4.2
.

In the remaining of this section, we assume

\mathfrak{f}(\psi)=O_{F}

and investigate
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi)
and
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{0})
, where
\pi
is an irreducible constituent of a regular or unitary unramified principal series and
\pi_{0}\subset\pi|_{\overline{G}}
.

4.3.
Regular unramified principal series

In this subsection, we consider the restriction to
\overline{G}_{0}
of an irreducible constituent
\pi
of a regular unramified genuine principal series
I(\chi)
of
\overline{G}
. In particular, we will study how the Whittaker dimension
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi)
is distributed into the constituents of
\pi|_{\overline{G}_{0}}
.

Temporarily, we use
\overline{G}^{\sharp}
to denote either
\overline{G}
or
\overline{G}_{0}
, and
\overline{T}^{\sharp}\subset\overline{G}^{\sharp}
is the covering torus. Let

\chi^{\sharp}:Z(\overline{T}^{\sharp})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

be a central character satisfying:

–

if
\overline{T}^{\sharp}=\overline{T}
, then
\chi^{\sharp}
is unramified;

–

if
\overline{T}^{\sharp}=\overline{T}_{0}
, then
i(\chi^{\sharp})\subset i(\chi)|_{\overline{T}_{0}}
for an unramified
\chi:Z(\overline{T})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
.

We highlight that in the second case above,
\chi^{\sharp}
may not be unramified, unless
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})
is an isotypic pair; also, by Lemma
3.3
we have
i(\chi^{\sharp})\subset i(\chi)|_{\overline{T}_{0}}
if and only if
\chi^{\sharp}|_{\overline{T}_{0}\cap Z(\overline{T})}=\chi|_{\overline{T}_{0}\cap Z(\overline{T})}
. Denoting

Y_{Q,n}^{\sharp}:=Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}

and let
\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{\sharp}
be the preimage in
\overline{T}
of
{\rm Im}(i_{Q,n}^{\sharp})\subset T
, where

i_{Q,n}^{\sharp}:\ Y_{Q,n}^{\sharp}\otimes F^{\times}\longrightarrow Y_{Q,n}\otimes F^{\times}\longrightarrow Y\otimes F^{\times}

is induced from the inclusions
Y_{Q,n}^{\sharp}\subset Y_{Q,n}\subset Y
. One has

\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{\sharp}\subset Z(\overline{T}).

Similarly, we have the subgroup
\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{sc}\subset Z(\overline{T})
associated with
Y_{Q,n}^{sc}\subset Y_{Q,n}
. In any case, since

\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{\sharp}=\overline{T}_{0}\cap Z(\overline{T})=Z(\overline{T}_{0})\cap Z(\overline{T}),

we see that every
\chi^{\sharp}
is unramified when restricted to
\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{\sharp}
. In particular, the restriction

\chi^{\sharp}|_{\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{sc}}:\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{sc}\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

is always unramified.

In this subsection, we assume that
\chi^{\sharp}
is regular, i.e., the stabilizer subgroup of
\chi^{\sharp}
in the Weyl group is trivial. Thus the irreducible constituents of
I(\chi^{\sharp})
are multiplicity-free. We denote its Jordan–Holder set by
{\rm JH}(I(\chi^{\sharp}))
. Consider

\Phi(\chi^{\sharp}):=\left\{\alpha\in\Phi:\chi^{\sharp}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{n_{\alpha}}))=q^{-1}\right\}\subset\Phi.

Let
\mathscr{P}(\Phi(\chi^{\sharp}))
be the power set of
\Phi(\chi^{\sharp})
, and denote by

\mathscr{C}(X\otimes\mathbf{R};\chi^{\sharp})

the set of connected components of

X\otimes\mathbf{R}-\bigcup_{\alpha\in\Phi(\chi^{\sharp})}{\rm Ker}(\alpha^{\vee}).

Proposition 4.3
.

There are bijections between the three sets

\mathscr{P}(\Phi(\chi^{\sharp}))\longleftrightarrow{\rm JH}(I(\chi^{\sharp}))\longleftrightarrow\mathscr{C}(X\otimes\mathbf{R};\chi^{\sharp})

denoted by

S\leftrightarrow\pi_{S}\leftrightarrow\Gamma_{S},

which is given as follows. First, we have

\Gamma_{S}=\left\{x\in X\otimes\mathbf{R}:{\left\langle\alpha^{\vee},x\right\rangle}<0\text{ if and only if }\alpha\in S\right\}.

Second, the representation
\pi_{S}
is characterized by its Jacquet module

(\pi_{S})_{U}=\bigoplus_{w\in W_{S}}\delta_{B}^{1/2}\cdot i({}^{w^{-1}}\chi^{\sharp}),

where

W_{S}=\left\{w\in W:\Phi(\chi^{\sharp})^{\vee}\cap w(\Phi_{-}^{\vee})=S^{\vee}\right\}\subset W.

Proof.

For linear algebraic group and a regular character
\chi
of
T\subset G
(not necessarily unramified), the result was shown in
[
Rod4
]
. For covering groups and unramified
\chi
, it was extended in
[
Ga6
]
. We explain how the argument in
[
Ga6
]
, which is adopted from
[
Rod4
]
, actually works for
\chi^{\sharp}
here.

Indeed, the key property needed in the proof in
[
Ga6
]
is the computation of the Plancherel measure
\mu(w_{\alpha},i(\chi^{\sharp}))

associated with
T(w_{\alpha},i(\chi^{\sharp}))
with
w_{\alpha}
being a simple reflection. For unramified
\chi^{\sharp}
, this was explicitly computed in
[
Ga1
]
and used in
[
Ga6
, Proposition 3.5]
; it shows that

(4.6)

\mu(w_{\alpha},i(\chi^{\sharp}))^{-1}=\frac{1-q^{-1}\chi^{\sharp}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{n_{\alpha}}))}{1-\chi^{\sharp}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{n_{\alpha}}))}\cdot\frac{1-q^{-1}\chi^{\sharp}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{n_{\alpha}}))^{-1}}{1-\chi^{\sharp}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{n_{\alpha}}))^{-1}}.

One thus concludes that the poles of
\mu(w_{\alpha},i(\chi^{\sharp}))
are achieved when
\alpha\in\Phi(\chi^{\sharp})
or
-\alpha\in\Phi(\chi^{\sharp})
. This is the key ingredient used in the proof in
[
Rod4
]
, the rest of which applies in the covering setting.

Now if
\chi^{\sharp}
is for
\overline{G}_{0}
with
i(\chi^{\sharp})\subset i(\chi)|_{\overline{T}}
, then we can proceed in two ways in order to determine
\mu(w_{\alpha},i(\chi^{\sharp}))
. First, one can invoke
[
GoSz
, Theorem 5.1]
directly. Alternatively, it follows from Lemma
2.14
that

\mu(w_{\alpha},i(\chi^{\sharp}))=\mu(w_{\alpha},i(\chi_{0}))

for every
\omega:Z(\overline{T}_{0})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
such that
i(\omega)\subset i(\chi)|_{\overline{T}_{0}}
. In particular, considering the unramified
\omega
and using the formula of
\mu(w_{\alpha},i(\omega))
in
[
Ga1
]
, one also obtains the same formula (
4.6
) for
\mu(w_{\alpha},i(\chi^{\sharp}))
. Now the same argument in
[
Ga6
, Proposition 3.5]
applies to give the desired result.
∎

For every
S\subset\Phi(\chi^{\sharp})
we denote by

\pi_{\chi^{\sharp},S}=\pi(\chi^{\sharp})_{S}\in{\rm JH}(I(\chi^{\sharp}))

the associated irreducible constituent of
I(\chi^{\sharp})
.

Corollary 4.4
.

Let
\chi:Z(\overline{T})\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be a regular unramified genuine character.

(i)

For every
\omega:Z(\overline{T}_{0})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
such that
i(\omega)\subset i(\chi)|_{\overline{T}_{0}}
, one has

\Phi(\chi)=\Phi(\omega).

(ii)

For every
S\subset\Phi(\chi)
, we have

(\pi_{\chi,S})|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}\bigoplus_{\omega_{\gamma,j}\in\mathscr{E}(\chi,{}^{\gamma}\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))}\pi_{\omega_{\gamma,j},S}.

Proof.

Part (i) follows from the proof of Proposition
4.3
, since
\mu(w,i(\chi))=\mu(w,i(\omega))
for such
\omega
and depends only on
\chi|_{\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{sc}}=\omega|_{\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{sc}}
. For (ii), we note that the representation
\pi_{\chi,S}
is characterized by its Jacquet module, and since computing Jacquet module commutes with restriction to
\overline{G}_{0}
, it suffices to determine

{\rm Res}_{\overline{T}_{0}}^{\overline{T}}(\pi_{\chi,S})_{U}=\bigoplus_{w\in W_{S}}\delta_{B}^{1/2}\cdot i({}^{w^{-1}}\chi)|_{\overline{T}_{0}}.

Since

i(\chi)|_{\overline{T}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}\bigoplus_{\omega_{\gamma,j}\in\mathscr{E}(\chi,{}^{\gamma}\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))}i(\omega_{\gamma,j}),

and similar decomposition for
{}^{w^{-1}}\chi
for each
w\in W_{S}
holds, we get

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Res}_{\overline{T}_{0}}^{\overline{T}}(\pi_{\chi,S})_{U}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}\bigoplus_{\omega_{\gamma,j}\in\mathscr{E}(\chi,{}^{\gamma}\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))}\bigoplus_{w\in W_{S}}\delta_{B_{0}}^{1/2}\cdot i({}^{w^{-1}}\omega_{\gamma,j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}\bigoplus_{\omega_{\gamma,j}\in\mathscr{E}(\chi,{}^{\gamma}\chi_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))}(\pi_{\omega_{\gamma,j},S})_{U}.

As noted, the representation
\pi_{\omega_{\gamma,j},S}
is uniquely determined by its Jacquet module
(\pi_{\omega_{\gamma,j},S})_{U}
, this shows (ii) and concludes the proof.
∎

For a regular
(K,s_{K})
-unramified
I(\chi)
, we want to determine the Whittaker space
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi,S})
in terms of the
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\omega_{\gamma,j},S})
’s. As in general there is much difficulty, in the remaining part of this subsection, we will consider two special cases:

–

when
Z(\overline{T}_{0})\subset Z(\overline{T})
, and thus
\pi_{\chi,S}|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot\pi_{\omega,S}
is an isotypic sum, where
\omega=\chi|_{Z(\overline{T}_{0})}
;

–

when the pair is
(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(n)},\overline{{\rm SL}}_{2}^{(n)})
with arbitrary
n
.

We hope to illustrate the subtleties on determining even the relation between
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi,S})
and
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\omega_{\gamma,j},S})
.

4.3.1.
The isotypic case

If
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})
is an isotypic pair, then we denote by

\omega:=\chi|_{Z(\overline{T}_{0})}

the unramified character of
Z(\overline{T}_{0})
. We have
\pi_{\chi,S}|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot\pi_{\omega,S}
and

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi,S})=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\omega,S}).

Now we want to give an interpretation of this equality from the perspective of the short exact sequence (
4.5
).

Lemma 4.5
.

Assume that
\overline{G}_{0}
is a saturated cover. Then

(i)

\overline{G}
is also a saturated cover, and thus both
\overline{G}_{0}
and
\overline{G}
are persistent covers;

(ii)

one has
Z(\overline{T}_{0})\subset Z(\overline{T})
in this case.

Proof.

Recall that we have the inclusions

Y_{Q,n}^{sc}\subset(Y^{sc}\cap Y_{Q,n})\subset(Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n})\subset Y_{0,Q,n}.

The assumption implies that
Y_{Q,n}^{sc}=Y_{0,Q,n}
. This enforces the equality
(Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n})=Y_{0,Q,n}
and thus (ii) holds. This also enforces another equality
Y_{Q,n}^{sc}=Y^{sc}\cap Y_{Q,n}
, which exactly shows that
\overline{G}
is saturated and gives (i).
∎

Let
s
be any splitting, if it exists, of the short exact sequence of finite abelian groups:

(4.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

Every such splitting gives a set
s(\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma})
of representatives of
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}
in
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}
.
We have

\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}=\bigsqcup_{z^{\prime}\in\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}}f^{-1}(z^{\prime})=\bigsqcup_{z\in s(\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma})}(z+\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}).

Assume
\overline{G}_{0}
is a saturated cover. Also assume that

\Phi(\chi)\subset\Delta.

It then follows from Lemma
4.5
and
[
Ga6
, Theorem 6.6]
that

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi,S})={\left\langle\sigma_{\mathscr{X}},\sigma_{S}\right\rangle}_{W},\quad\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\omega,S})={\left\langle\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}},\sigma_{S}\right\rangle}_{W}.

Here
\sigma_{S}
is a certain Kazhdan–Lusztig representation of
W
of dimension
\left|W_{S}\right|
. On the other hand,

\sigma_{\mathscr{X}}:W\longrightarrow{\rm Perm}(\mathscr{X}_{Q,n})

is the permutation representation of
W
given by
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}}(w)(y):=w[y]
, where

w[y]=w(y-\rho)+\rho.

Similarly,

\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}:W\longrightarrow{\rm Perm}(\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n})

is also given by

\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}(w)(y)=w[y]

for every
y\in\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}
. In fact,
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}
is the subrepresentation of
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}}
restricted on
\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}\subset\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}
.

For every coset
z+\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}\subset\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}
, the action
w[\cdot]
is given by

w[z+y]=w(z)+w[y]=z+(w[y]+w(z)-z)

for every
y\in\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}
, where
w(z)
denotes the usual action arising from reflection.
As the image of
w(z)-z
in
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}
via
f
is trivial, we see that

w(z)-z\in\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}.

Thus,
w[z+y]
lies in
z+\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}
; that is, the action
w[\cdot]
on
z+\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}
is well-defined. This gives a permutation representation

\sigma_{z+\mathscr{X}_{0}}:W\longrightarrow{\rm Perm}(z+\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}).

On the other hand, we consider the permutation representation

\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0},z}:W\longrightarrow{\rm Perm}(\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n})

given by

\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0},z}(w)(y)=w\langle y\rangle:=w[y]+w(z)-z.

It is easy to check that the action
w\langle\cdot\rangle
is well-defined. Thus
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0},z}
is a well-defined permutation representation of
W
on
\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}
. In fact, we have:

(i)

The representation
\sigma_{z+\mathscr{X}_{0}}
is the one obtained from transporting
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0},z}
via the translation

\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}\to z+\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}

given by
y\mapsto z+y
. Thus, as representations of the Weyl group,
\sigma_{z+\mathscr{X}_{0}}
and
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0},z}
are equivalent. In particular, we have

\sigma_{\mathscr{X}}=\bigoplus_{z\in s(\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma})}\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0},z}.

(ii)

Denote by

\rho_{z}:W\longrightarrow{\rm Perm}(\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n})

the representation given by the translation
\rho_{z}(w)(y)=y+w(z)-z
. It is clear that
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0},z}=\rho_{z}\circ\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}
.

We note that the exact sequence (
4.7
) is
W
-equivariant with respect to the usual action
w(\cdot)
. Also, the action of
W
on
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}
is trivial. In general, the splitting
s
may not be
W
-equivariant. However, we believe that in our setting this is indeed the case:

Conjecture 4.6
.

Assume that
\overline{G}_{0}
is a saturated cover. Then there exists a
W
-equivariant splitting
s:\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\to\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}
with respect to the Weyl action
w(\cdot)
.
Consequently, with respect to such a splitting
s
, one has

\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0},z}\simeq\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}

for every
z\in s(\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma})
and thus
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}
. In particular,
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi,S})=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi_{0},S})
.

If
s
is
W
-equivariant, then

w(z)-z=0

and thus
\rho_{z}(w)={\rm id}
for every
z\in s(\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma})
; hence, it is clear that

\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0},z}=\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}

in this case. For a fixed splitting
s
, consider the following three statements:

(S1)

\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0},z}\simeq\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}
for every
z\in s(\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma})
;

(S2)

{\left\langle\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0},z},\sigma_{S}\right\rangle}_{W}={\left\langle\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}},\sigma_{S}\right\rangle}_{W}
for every
z\in s(\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma})
;

(S3)

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi,S})=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi_{0},S})
.

Clearly, we have the implications

{\rm(S1)}\Longrightarrow{\rm(S2)}\Longrightarrow{\rm(S3)}.

Here Conjecture (
4.6
) asserts the strongest (S1) in our context, and is certainly compatible with (S3) which holds a priori.

Example 4.7
.

Consider the cover
\overline{G}=\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(n)}
associated with
\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}\in\mathbf{Z}
such that
2\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}=-1
, as discussed from §
2.4.1
. Then the cover
\overline{G}_{0}=\overline{{\rm SL}}_{2}^{(n)}
is saturated if and only if
n
is odd. Thus, we assume that
n
is odd. An easy computation gives that

Y_{Q,n}=\left\{y_{1}e_{1}+y_{2}e_{2}\in\mathbf{Z}e_{1}\oplus\mathbf{Z}e_{2}:\ n|(4\mathbf{p}+1)y_{1}\text{ and }n|(y_{1}-y_{2})\right\}.

Setting
d=\gcd(4\mathbf{p}+1,n)
, we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma},}

with

\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}=\mathbf{Z}\alpha^{\vee}/(\mathbf{Z}n\alpha^{\vee}),\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}\right|=n^{2}/d\text{ and }\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|=n/d.

We claim that there is a
W
-equivariant splitting
s
given by

s(\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma})=\left\{ke_{c}:0\leqslant k\leqslant(n/d-1)\right\}\subset\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}.

For this purpose, it suffices to show that

ke_{c}\notin Y^{sc}+Y_{Q,n}

for every
1\leqslant k\leqslant(n/d-1)
. Suppose not, then there exists
m\alpha^{\vee}\in Y^{sc}
such that
ke_{c}+m\alpha^{\vee}\in Y_{Q,n}
. This implies that

(n/d)|(k+m)\text{ and }n|(k+a-k+a).

As
n
is odd, this implies that
(n/d)|k
, which is a contradiction. Thus, Conjecture
4.6
holds for this pair
(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2},\overline{{\rm SL}}_{2})
. We remark that when
d=n
(for example, if
n=3,\mathbf{p}=-1
), then
\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}=\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}
; in this case
\pi_{\chi,S}|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\pi_{\chi_{0},S}
with multiplicity one.

Example 4.8
.

We consider the cover
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{4}^{(3)}
from §
2.4.2
such that
Q(\alpha_{2}^{\vee})=-1
. Note that in this case,
\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{4}
is saturated, as its dual group is
{\rm SO}_{5}
. Using notations in §
2.4.2
, we see that

Y_{Q,n}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}y_{1}e_{1}+y_{2}e_{2}+y_{0}e_{0}\in\oplus_{i=0}^{2}\mathbf{Z}e_{i}:\\
\bullet\quad 3|(-2y_{1}+y_{0})\\
\bullet\quad 3|(-2y_{1}+y_{0})\\
\bullet\quad 3|(y_{1}+y_{2}+2y_{0}\cdot Q(e_{0}))\end{array}\right\}.

There are two cases as follows.

–

If
Q(e_{0})=\pm 1\mod 3
, then one can check easily that
\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}=\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}
in this case. Thus, Conjecture
4.6
holds trivially, and we have
\pi_{\chi,S}|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\pi_{\chi_{0},S}
with multiplicity one.

–

If
Q(e_{0})=0\mod 3
, then
Y_{Q,n}=3Y
. In this case, one has

\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}=Y_{0}/3Y_{0}.

On the other hand,
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}=Y/3Y
. Thus, a
W
-equivariant splitting is given by

s(\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma})=\left\{ke_{c}:0\leqslant k\leqslant 2\right\}.

In either case, we see that Conjecture
4.6
holds.

4.3.2.
Covers of
{\rm GL}_{2}
and
{\rm SL}_{2}

We consider the pair
(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2},\overline{{\rm SL}}_{2})
of
n
-fold covers associated with
\mathbf{p}
and
\mathbf{q}
such that
Q(\alpha^{\vee})=2\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}=-1
. For odd
n
, this was discussed in Example
4.7
; thus, we assume in this subsection that

n=2m

is even. Again, we set

d:=\gcd(4\mathbf{p}+1,n).

It is easy to see the diagram (
4.4
) becomes

(4.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}(m\alpha^{\vee})/\mathbf{Z}(n\alpha^{\vee})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y/Y_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}\alpha^{\vee}/\mathbf{Z}(n\alpha^{\vee})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}\alpha^{\vee}/\mathbf{Z}(m\alpha^{\vee})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n},}

where
\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}\right|=n^{2}/d
and
\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|=n/d
. Also, it is easy to obtain

Y^{\mathfrak{c}}=\left\{y_{1}e_{1}+y_{2}e_{2}\in Y:\ 2|(y_{1}-y_{2})\right\},

and thus we see that

[\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}:\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}]=2.

It follows that

\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}=\left\{ie_{1}:0\leqslant i\leqslant 1\right\}.

For every
\gamma_{i}\in\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}
, we have

\mathscr{E}(\chi,{}^{\gamma}\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))=\left\{\omega_{\gamma,j}:\ 0\leqslant j\leqslant 1\right\}.

Setting
\gamma_{i}:=ie_{1}
. It follows from Theorem
2.24
(see also
[
GSS2
, Theorem 8.15]
) that

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=(m/d)\cdot\bigoplus_{0\leqslant i\leqslant 1}\bigoplus_{0\leqslant j\leqslant 1}I(\omega^{0}_{\gamma_{i},j}).

Also, the two representations
I(\omega_{\gamma_{0},j}),j=0,1
are the two
(K_{0},s_{K})
-unramified constituents of
I(\chi)
. Again, for every
S\subset\Phi(\chi)
, we have

(4.9)

\pi_{\chi,S}|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=(m/d)\cdot\bigoplus_{0\leqslant i\leqslant 1}\bigoplus_{0\leqslant j\leqslant 1}\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j})_{S},

where we have written
\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j})_{S}
instead of
\pi_{\omega_{\gamma_{i},j},S}
for notational convenience.

In the rest of this subsection, we assume that
\Phi(\chi)=\Delta^{\vee}=\left\{\alpha^{\vee}\right\}
. In this case, we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\chi,\emptyset}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I(\chi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\chi,\Delta},}

where
\pi_{\chi,\emptyset}
is the covering analogue of the Steinberg representation, and
\pi_{\chi,\Delta}
is the theta representation associated with
\chi
. Since
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}
is always a saturated cover and thus persistent (see
[
Ga6
, Lemma 2.7]
), it follows from
[
Ga6
, Theorem 6.6]
that

–

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi,\emptyset})={\left\langle\sigma_{\mathscr{X}},\mathbbm{1}\right\rangle}_{W}=m(n+1)/d
, which is equal to the number of
W
-orbits in
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}
;

–

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\chi,\Delta})={\left\langle\sigma_{\mathscr{X}},\varepsilon_{W}\right\rangle}_{W}=m(n-1)/d
, which is equal to the number of free
W
-orbits in
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}
.

Here
\mathbbm{1}
(resp.
\varepsilon_{W}
) denotes the trivial character (resp. sign character) of
W
.

Theorem 4.9
.

Let
\chi:Z(\overline{T})\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be an unramified genuine character such that
\Phi(\chi)=\Delta
. We always have

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j})_{\emptyset})+\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j})_{\Delta})=\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j}))=m.

Moreover, the following fold.

(i)

If
4|n
(i.e.
2|m
), then

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j})_{\Delta})=\begin{cases}m/2&\text{ for }i=0,j\in\left\{0,1\right\},\\
m/2&\text{ for }i=1,j=0,\\
(m-2)/2&\text{ for }i=1,j=1.\end{cases}

(ii)

If
n=2m
with
m
odd, then

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j})_{\Delta})=\begin{cases}(m+1)/2&\text{ for }i=0,j=0,\\
(m-1)/2&\text{ for }i=0,j=1,\\
(m-1)/2&\text{ for }i=1,j\in\left\{0,1\right\}.\end{cases}

In either case,
\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{0},j})_{\Delta},j=0,1
are the two
(K_{0},s_{K})
-unramified theta representations associated with
I(\omega_{\gamma_{0},j})
.

Proof.

We first consider the case
m
is even. In this case, the cover
\overline{{\rm SL}}_{2}^{(n)}
is persistent. As
I(\omega_{\gamma_{0},j}^{0})
for
j\in\left\{0,1\right\}
is unramified, it thus follows from
[
Ga6
, Proposition 6.2]
that

(4.10)

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{0},j})_{\Delta})={\left\langle\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}},\varepsilon_{W}\right\rangle}_{W}=\frac{m}{2},

which is equal to the number of free
W
-orbits in
\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}
. To deal with
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j})_{\Delta})
, we consider a local coefficients matrix
\mathcal{M}(w,i(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j}))
associated with the map

\mathcal{T}(w,i(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j}))^{*}:{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j}))\longrightarrow{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j})),

which arises from the intertwining operator

T(w,i(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j})):I(i(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j}))\longrightarrow I({}^{w}i(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j})).

One has

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j})_{\Delta})=\text{ rank of }\mathcal{T}(w,i(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j}))^{*}.

To have a description of
\mathcal{M}(w,i(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j}))
, we first reconcile some notations used in
[
GSS2
]
. Let

\xi:F^{\times}\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

be a character such that

\xi(x)^{n}=\chi(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(x^{n})).

Now we have from
[
GSS2
, §9.5.3]
(especially the proof of Theorem 9.13 there) the following.

–

The matrix
\mathcal{M}(w,i(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j}))
, by swapping certain rows and columns, becomes a block-diagonal matrix with
m/2
-blocks and each block is of size two by two. In fact, each block is associated with a free
W
-orbit in
\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}
. We label these two by two blocks as

\mathcal{M}_{k},1\leqslant k\leqslant(m/2).

Every
\mathcal{M}_{k}
is a non-zero matrix, and thus in particular
{\rm rank}(\mathcal{M}_{k})\geqslant 1.

–

There is a special two by two block labelled by
\mathcal{M}_{1}
, which takes the form

\mathcal{M}_{1}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&\gamma(1,\xi^{-n})\\
\boldsymbol{\beta}(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j},s,\psi)&0\end{matrix}\right),

where

\boldsymbol{\beta}(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j},s,\psi)=\frac{(1-q^{-1/2}\cdot(\xi\eta_{u})^{-m_{\gamma_{\psi}}})(1-q^{-1/2}\xi^{m_{\gamma_{\psi}}})}{(1-(\xi\eta_{u})^{m_{\gamma_{\psi}}})(1-\xi^{-m_{\gamma_{\psi}}})}.

Here
u\in O^{\times}
is a representative of the unique nontrivial coset of
O^{\times}/O^{\times 2}
, and
\eta_{u}(a)=(u,a)_{n}
for
a\in F^{\times}
; also,

m_{\gamma_{\psi}}:=m\cdot\gamma_{\psi}(\varpi)\in\left\{\pm m\right\},

where
\gamma_{\psi}
is the Weil-index associated with
\psi
.

–

For every
2\leqslant k\leqslant(m/2)
, one has

\det(\mathcal{M}_{k})=_{\mathbf{C}^{\times}}\mu(w,i(\chi))^{-1},

where
=_{\mathbf{C}^{\times}}
means the equality holds modulo some elements in
\mathbf{C}^{\times}
(in fact modulo an element in
\left\{\pm\xi^{-m}\right\}
as shown in the proof of
[
GSS2
, Theorem 9.13]
), and
\mu(w,i(\chi))
denotes the Plancherel measure.

Since
\Phi(\chi)=\Delta
, we see that
\mu(w,i(\chi))^{-1}=0
and thus

{\rm rank}(\mathcal{M}_{k})=1\text{ for }2\leqslant k\leqslant(m/2).

Also, we have

\gamma(1,\xi^{-n})=(1-q^{-1}\xi^{-n})/(1-\xi^{n}),

which equals
0
in this case. Let
{\rm sgn}(\xi)\in\left\{\pm 1\right\}
be such that

\xi(\varpi)^{m}={\rm sgn}(\xi)\cdot q^{-1/2}.

Then it is not hard to see that

\boldsymbol{\beta}(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j},s,\psi)=0\Longleftrightarrow{\rm sgn}(\xi)=\eta_{u,(2)}(\varpi),

where
\eta_{u,(2)}(x)=(u,x)_{2}
. Thus, we could label
\omega_{\gamma_{1},0}
to be such that
{\rm sgn}(\xi)=-\eta_{u,(2)}(\varpi)
and thus
\boldsymbol{\beta}(\omega_{\gamma_{1},0},s,\psi)\neq 0
; in this case,
{\rm rank}(\mathcal{M}_{1})=1
and

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{1},0})_{\Delta})=1+(m-2)/2=m/2.

On the other hand,
\omega_{\gamma_{1},1}
is such that
\boldsymbol{\beta}(\omega_{\gamma_{1},1}^{0},s,\psi)=0
, and thus

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{1},1})_{\Delta})=(m-2)/2.

This completes the proof for the case
4|n
.

Now we assume that
m=2l+1
is odd. Again, we first consider the unramified theta representation
\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{0},j})_{\Delta},j\in\left\{0,1\right\}
. Note that the
n
-fold cover
{\rm SL}_{2}
is not persistent in this case, and thus formula (
4.10
) does not hold. The
W
-orbits in
\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}=\mathbf{Z}(\alpha^{\vee})/\mathbf{Z}(m\alpha^{\vee})
are

\left\{\mathcal{O}_{i\alpha^{\vee}}:1\leqslant i\leqslant l\right\}\cup\left\{\mathcal{O}_{-l\alpha^{\vee}}\right\}

with

\mathcal{O}_{i\alpha^{\vee}}=\left\{i\alpha^{\vee},(1-i)\alpha^{\vee}\right\}\text{ for }1\leqslant i\leqslant l\text{ and }\mathcal{O}_{-l\alpha^{\vee}}=\left\{-l\alpha^{\vee}\right\}.

Every free orbit
\mathcal{O}_{i\alpha^{\vee}},1\leqslant i\leqslant l
supports a one-dimensional subspace of
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{0},j})_{\Delta})
for both
j\in\left\{0,1\right\}
. On the other hand, it follows from
[
Ga2
, Theorem 3.14 and §4]
that
\mathcal{O}_{-l\alpha^{\vee}}
supports a one-dimensional subspace of
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{0},j})_{\Delta})
for exactly one of the unramified characters in
\left\{\omega_{\gamma_{0},j}:j=0,1\right\}
; we assume without loss of generality that

{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{0},0})_{\Delta})_{\mathcal{O}_{-l\alpha^{\vee}}}=1,\text{ and thus }{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{0},1})_{\Delta})_{\mathcal{O}_{-l\alpha^{\vee}}}=1.

This gives that

(4.11)

{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{0},0})_{\Delta})=l+1=(m+1)/2,\text{ and }{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{0},1})_{\Delta})_{\mathcal{O}_{-l\alpha^{\vee}}}=l=(m-1)/2.

To determine
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j})_{\Delta})
, we consider a local coefficients matrix

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(w,i(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j}))

of size
m\times m
associated with the map
\mathcal{T}(w,i(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j}))^{*}
as above. Again, let
\xi:F^{\times}\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be the character such that
\xi(x)^{n}=\chi(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(x^{n}))
for
x\in F^{\times}
. We may assume that
\xi
is ramified and
\xi^{2}
is unramified. Such a matrix
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(w,i(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j}))
is computed in the proof of
[
GSS2
, Theorem 9.12]
with the following properties.

–

The matrix
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}(w,i(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j}))
, by swapping certain rows and columns, becomes a block-diagonal matrix with every block associated to a
W
-orbits in
\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}
. Thus, we have
(m+1)/2
-many blocks
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\prime}
, each associated with
\mathcal{O}_{i\alpha^{\vee}},1\leqslant i\leqslant l
or
i=-l
. Also the size of
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\prime}
is exactly
\left|\mathcal{O}_{i\alpha^{\vee}}\right|
for every such
i
.

–

For every
1\leqslant i\leqslant l
, the matrix
\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\prime}
is a non zero two by two matrix with

\det(\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\prime})=_{\mathbf{C}^{\times}}\mu(w,i(\chi))^{-1}.

On the other hand,

\mathcal{M}_{-l}^{\prime}=\tilde{\gamma}(1,\xi^{-m},\psi),

which is the metaplectic-gamma factor defined in
[
Szp3
]
.

Now, since
\Phi(\chi)=\Delta
, we see that
\mu(w,i(\chi))^{-1}=0
and thus

{\rm rank}(\mathcal{M}_{i}^{\prime})=1\text{ for every }1\leqslant i\leqslant l.

On the other hand, one has from
[
GoSz
, Theorem A.1]
(see also
[
GSS2
, Theorem 4.4]
) that

\tilde{\gamma}(1,\xi^{-m},\psi)=_{\mathbf{C}^{\times}}\frac{\gamma(1/2,\xi^{m},\psi)}{\gamma(0,\xi^{n},\psi_{2})},

where
\psi_{2}(x)=\psi(2x)
. Since
\xi
is ramified with
\xi^{2}
unramified, we see that
\xi^{m}
is ramified and therefore

\gamma(1/2,\xi^{m},\psi)\in\mathbf{C}^{\times}.

Also,
\xi^{n}
is unramified with
\xi^{n}(\varpi)=q^{-1}
, and this implies that

\tilde{\gamma}(1,\xi^{-m},\psi)=_{\mathbf{C}^{\times}}\frac{1-q^{-1}\xi^{-n}(\varpi)}{1-\xi^{n}(\varpi)}=0.

This shows that
\mathcal{M}_{-l}^{\prime}=0
for either
j=0,1
, and thus

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j})_{\Delta})=\frac{m-1}{2}

for both
j=0,1
.

We remark that the above consideration also applies to the unramified
\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{0},j})_{\Delta},j=0,1
. Indeed, in this case, we may assume that
\xi
is unramified. Thus,

\tilde{\gamma}(1,\xi^{-m},\psi)=\frac{\gamma(1/2,\xi^{m},\psi)}{\gamma(0,\xi^{n},\psi_{2})}=\frac{1-q^{1/2}\xi^{m}(\varpi)}{1-q^{-1/2}\xi^{-m}(\varpi)}\cdot\frac{1-q^{-1}\xi^{-n}(\varpi)}{1-\xi^{n}(\varpi)}.

Recall that
\xi^{m}(\varpi)={\rm sgn}(\xi)\cdot q^{-1/2}
, and therefore

\tilde{\gamma}(1,\xi^{-m},\psi)=0\Longleftrightarrow{\rm sgn}(\xi)=-1.

This also gives the desired equalities for
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{0},j})_{\Delta}),j=0,1
. In any case, the proof of the case
m
is odd is completed.
∎

Remark 4.10
.

We always have the equality

\sum_{0\leqslant i,j\leqslant 1}\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j})_{\Delta})=n-1.

For
n=4
this equality and (
4.10
) together enforce
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j})_{\Delta})=0
for both
j=0,1
. For
n=2
, the above equality coupled with (
4.11
) also imply that

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\omega_{\gamma_{1},j})_{\Delta})=0\text{ for }j=0,1.

However, for
n
large, it is necessary to have an explicit description of a local coefficients matrix, as used in the proof of Theorem
4.9
.

4.4.
Unitary unramified principal series

In this subsection, we investigate the decomposition of an irreducible constituent of a unitary
(K,s_{K})
