-unramified principal series under the restriction from
\overline{G}
to
\overline{G}_{0}
, and also how the Whittaker dimension behaves with respect to the restriction.

We first recall some notations and results following
[
Ga7
]
. Let
I(\chi)
be a unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series of
\overline{G}
. Denote by

R_{\chi}\subset W(\chi)

the R-group associated with
I(\chi)
, where
W(\chi)\subset W
is the stabilizer subgroup of
\chi
. One has an algebra isomorphism

\mathbf{C}[R_{\chi}]\simeq{\rm End}_{\overline{G}}(I(\chi)),

given by

R_{\chi}\ni w\mapsto\mathcal{A}(w,i(\chi)),

where

\mathcal{A}(w,i(\chi))=\gamma(w,\chi)\cdot T(w,i(\chi))

is the normalized intertwining operator. Here
\gamma(w,\chi)
is the gamma factor associated with
w\in W
. One knows that
R_{\chi}
is abelian (see
[
Luo3
]
or
[
Ga7
, Theorem 4.6]
) and thus there is a multiplicity-free decomposition

I(\chi)=\bigoplus_{\tau\in{\rm Irr}(R_{\chi})}\pi_{\tau},

where
{\rm Irr}(R_{\chi})={\rm Hom}(R_{\chi},\mathbf{C}^{\times})
denotes the characters of
R_{\chi}
. One has

\mathcal{A}(w,i(\chi))|_{\pi_{\tau}}=\tau(w)\cdot{\rm id}

for every
w\in R_{\chi}
.

On the dual side, let

\phi_{\chi}:\text{WD}_{F}\longrightarrow{}^{L}\overline{T}\longrightarrow{}^{L}\overline{G}

be the parameter associated with
I(\chi)
. Recall the component group

\mathcal{S}_{\chi}:=\mathcal{S}_{\phi_{\chi}}

of the centralizer of
{\rm Im}(\phi_{\chi})
modulo
Z(\overline{G})^{\vee}
. By using the results of Keys
[
Key3
]
, it is shown in

[
Ga7
, Theorem 4.9]
that

(4.12)

R_{\chi}\simeq\mathcal{S}_{\chi}.

Note that the same consideration applies to unitary unramified principal series of
\overline{G}_{0}
, and analogous results hold.

Consider the decomposition from Theorem
2.24
(using slightly different notation)

(4.13)

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\bigoplus_{\gamma_{i}}\bigoplus_{j}I(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j}),

where

\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}=\left\{\gamma_{i}\right\}_{i}.

For every
\gamma_{i}
we have

\mathscr{E}(\chi,{}^{\gamma_{i}}\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))=\left\{\omega_{\gamma_{i},j}\right\}_{j},

which always has size
\left|Y_{0,Q,n}/(Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n})\right|
. For simplicity of notations, we may even write

\omega_{i,j}:=\omega_{\gamma_{i},j}.

For every constituent
I(\omega_{i,j}):=I(\omega_{\gamma_{i},j})
, we have the L-parameter
\phi_{\omega_{i,j}}
which fits into the following commutative diagram:

(4.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{G}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{L}\overline{H}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\chi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\omega_{i,j}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{G,H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{G_{0},H}}

Recall that we defined

\phi^{\diamondsuit}=f_{G,H}\circ\phi_{\chi}=f_{G_{0},H}\circ\phi_{\omega_{i,j}}

and thus have

\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}:=\mathcal{S}_{\phi^{\diamondsuit}}.

We denote

\Omega_{\chi}=\left\{\omega_{i,j}\in{\rm Hom}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{T}_{0}),\mathbf{C}^{\times}):I(\omega_{i,j})\subset I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}\right\},

and have from Lemma
3.3
that

\Omega_{\chi}=\left\{\omega_{i,j}\in{\rm Hom}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{T}_{0}),\mathbf{C}^{\times}):\omega_{i,j}|_{\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{\sharp}}=\chi|_{\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{\sharp}}\right\}.

We also denote

\displaystyle\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{E}(\chi,\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))

\displaystyle=\left\{\omega_{i,j}\in{\rm Hom}_{\rm gen}(Z(\overline{T}_{0}),\mathbf{C}^{\times}):I(\omega_{i,j})\subset I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}\text{ and is $(K_{0},s_{K})$-unramified}\right\}

For every
\omega^{\flat}\in\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}
, set

\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}(\omega^{\flat}):=\left\{{}^{w}(\omega^{\flat}):w\in W\right\}\cap\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}=\left\{{}^{w}(\omega^{\flat}):w\in W\right\}\cap\mathscr{E}(\chi,\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0})).

For
\omega^{\flat}\in\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}
there are two natural inclusions (see §
3.2
and
[
Ga7
, §4.3]
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}.}

Note that
\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}
depends only on
\phi_{\chi}
and not on choice of
\omega^{\flat}
. We write

\chi_{\rm re}:=\chi|_{\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{\sharp}}=\omega^{\flat}|_{\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{\sharp}},

where
\mathbbm{L}(\overline{T}_{Q,n}^{\sharp})=Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}
. Since
Y_{Q,n}^{sc}\subset(Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n})
, it follows from (
4.12
) and
[
Ga7
, §4.2]
that one has an isomorphism

\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}/\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}\simeq W(\chi_{\rm re})/W(\omega^{\flat}).

Lemma 4.11
.

For every
\omega^{\flat}\in\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}
, one has

\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}(\omega^{\flat})=\left\{{}^{w}(\omega^{\flat}):w\in W(\chi_{\rm re})\right\},

with
{\rm Stab}_{W(\chi_{\rm re})}(\omega^{\flat})=W(\omega^{\flat})
. Thus,
\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}(\omega^{\flat})
is a torsor over
\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}/\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}
.

Proof.

It follows from the definition of
\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}(\omega^{\flat})
that

\displaystyle{}^{w}(\omega^{\flat})\in\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}(\omega^{\flat})

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\text{ ${}^{w}(\omega^{\flat})$ is an unramified extension of $\chi_{\rm re}$ }

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle{}^{w}(\omega^{\flat})|_{Z(\overline{T})\cap\overline{T}_{0}}=\chi_{\rm re}=\omega^{\flat}|_{Z(\overline{T})\cap\overline{T}_{0}}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle w\in W(\chi_{\rm re}).

It is clear that the stabilizer subgroup of
\omega^{\flat}
is
W(\omega^{\flat})\subset W(\chi_{\rm re})
. It follows that
\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}(\omega^{\flat})
is a torsor over
\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}/\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}
.
∎

We have the short exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\chi}/(\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}/\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}/(\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}).}

The action of
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
on
\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}(\omega^{\flat})
factors through
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}/(\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})
, and every such orbit is in fact a torsor over
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}/(\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})
by Lemma
4.11
. Thus, we have a decomposition

\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}(\omega^{\flat})=\bigsqcup_{k=1}^{\left|\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}/(\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})\right|}\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}

into a disjoint union of
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
-orbits, where every
\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}
is a torsor over
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}/(\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})
.

To proceed, we analyze the space
{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\pi_{\tau})
with
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}),\tau\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\chi})
in more detail by adapting ideas from
[
Key3
]
. To set up some notations:

–

f:A\longrightarrow B
is a ring homomorphism;

–

W_{A}
is a
A
-module, and
W_{B},V
are both
B
-modules;

–

setting
E={\rm End}_{B}(V)
, then
{\rm Hom}_{B}(W_{B},V)
and
{\rm Hom}_{A}(W_{A},V_{f})
are both
E
-modules, where
V_{f}=V
is view as a
A
-module via
f
.

It is proved in
[
Key3
, Page 53]
that if
W_{B}
is a direct summand of
V
, then the natural homomorphism

(4.15)

{\rm Hom}_{A}(W_{A},(W_{B})_{f})\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{E}({\rm Hom}_{B}(W_{B},V),{\rm Hom}_{A}(W_{A},V_{f}))

of vector spaces is an isomorphism.
We apply the above to the following data

A=C_{c}^{\infty}[\overline{G}_{0}],\ B=C_{c}^{\infty}[\overline{G}],\ V=I(\chi),\ W_{A}=\pi_{\rho},\ W_{B}=\pi_{\tau}

to obtain

(4.16)

{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}}(\pi_{\rho},\pi_{\tau}|_{\overline{G}_{0}})={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]}\left(\tau,{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}})\right),

where we note that

\tau\simeq{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}}(\pi_{\tau},I(\chi))

as
\mathbf{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]
-module.
In view of the decomposition of
I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}
in (
4.13
), we first observe that (see
[
BZ2
, Theorem 2.9]
)

{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},I(\omega_{i,j}))\neq 0\Longleftrightarrow\omega_{i,j}\in\Omega^{\rm un}(\omega^{\flat}).

Denoting

\Pi(\omega^{\flat}):=\bigoplus_{\omega_{i,j}\in\Omega^{\rm un}(\omega^{\flat})}I(\omega_{i,j})

and

\Pi(\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}):=\bigoplus_{\omega_{i,j}\in\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}}I(\omega_{i,j}),

one clearly has

\Pi(\omega^{\flat})=\bigoplus_{k}\Pi(\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}).

Also,

{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}})=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\bigoplus_{k}{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\Pi(\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k})).

The action of
w\in\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
on
{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}})
is given by transporting the action of
\mathcal{A}(w,\chi)
on
I(\chi)
. Also, we have a decomposition

\mathcal{A}(w,\chi)=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\bigoplus_{i,j}\mathcal{A}(w,\omega_{i,j}),

where

\mathcal{A}(w,\omega_{i,j}):I(\omega_{i,j})\longrightarrow I({}^{w}\omega_{i,j})

is the normalized intertwining operator of each
I(\omega_{i,j})
. We see that the restriction
\mathcal{A}(w,\chi)|_{\Pi(\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k})}
is well-defined for every
k
. Thus, the space

{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\Pi(\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}))

affords a representation of
\mathbf{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]
of dimension
\left|\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k})\right|
, and
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
also acts on the
\left|\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}(\omega^{\flat})\right|
-dimensional space

{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\Pi(\omega^{\flat}))=\bigoplus_{k}{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\Pi(\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k})).

Theorem 4.12
.

Let
I(\chi)
be a unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series of
\overline{G}
and
I(\omega^{\flat})
an
(K_{0},s_{K})
-unramified constituent of
I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}
. Keep notations as above. Then as representations of
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
,

(4.17)

{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\Pi(\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}))\simeq{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}^{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}(\rho)

for every
k
. Consequently,

(4.18)

\dim{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\pi_{\tau})=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot{\left\langle{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}^{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}\rho,{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}^{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}\tau\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}

for every
\tau\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\chi})
and
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})
.

Proof.

First,

{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\Pi(\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}))=\bigoplus_{\omega_{i,j}\in\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}}{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},I(\omega_{i,j})).

Since
I(\omega_{i,j})\simeq I(\omega^{\flat})
for every
\omega_{i,j}\in\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}
,
it is easy to check that as representations of
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}
, one has

{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},I(\omega_{i,j})\simeq\rho|_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}.

Moreover, as
\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}
is a torsor over
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}/(\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})
for every
k
, the representation of
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
on
{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\Pi(\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}))
is indeed
{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}^{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}\rho
. This gives (
4.17
).

Now to prove (
4.18
), we see that as representations of
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
,

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}})

\displaystyle=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\bigoplus_{k}{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\Pi(\mathcal{O}(\omega^{\flat})_{k}))

\displaystyle=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\left|\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}/(\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})\right|\cdot{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}^{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}\rho.

On the other hand, the Frobenius reciprocity gives

{\left\langle{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}^{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}\rho,{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}^{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}\tau\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}={\left\langle{\rm Res}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}^{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}^{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}\rho,\tau\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}},

and by Mackey’s theory one has

{\rm Res}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}^{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}^{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}\rho=\bigoplus_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}/(\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})}{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}^{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}\rho=\left|\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}/(\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})\right|\cdot{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}^{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}\rho.

This coupled with (
4.16
) give the desired equality (
4.18
). The proof is completed.
∎

Corollary 4.13
.

Let
I(\chi)
be a unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series of
\overline{G}
and
I(\omega)\subset I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}},\omega\in\Omega(\chi)
an arbitrary constituent. Then

\dim{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\pi_{\tau})=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot{\left\langle{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\omega}}^{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}\rho,{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}^{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}\tau\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}}

for every
\tau\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\chi})
and
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\omega})
. In particular, this verifies Conjecture
3.2
(ii) for unitary unramified principal series of
\overline{G}
with
e(I(\chi))=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|
.

Proof.

Assume that
\omega=\omega_{\gamma,j}
in the notation of (
4.13
). Since
I(\chi)
is
(K,s_{K})
-unramified, it is also
(\gamma\cdot K,\gamma\cdot{s_{K}})
-unramified. Since the genuine principal series
I(\omega)
is
(\gamma\cdot K_{0},\gamma\cdot s_{K})
-unramified in this case (see Remark
2.26
), we can apply Theorem
4.12
to obtain the desired equality regarding
\dim{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\pi_{\tau})
. The rest is clear.
∎

A special case is the following

Corollary 4.14
.

Assume that
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})
is an isotypic-pair. Let
I(\chi)
be a unitary unramified principal series of
\overline{G}
and let
\omega^{\flat}=\chi|_{Z(\overline{T}_{0})}
. Then

\dim{\rm Hom}_{\overline{G}_{0}}(\pi_{\rho},\pi_{\tau})=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot{\left\langle\rho|_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}},\tau\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}.

Proof.

For an isotypic pair
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})
, one has
\overline{G}_{0}^{\vee}=H^{\vee}
and thus
\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}=\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}
. In particular,
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\subset\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}
. The result follows from Theorem
4.12
.
∎

Remark 4.15
.

Corollary
4.14
confirms for unitary unramified genuine principal series part (ii) of Conjecture
3.2
. In fact, Theorem
4.12
generalizes this to the case where
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})
may not be an isotypic-pair.

It follows from Corollary
4.14
that for an isotypic pair
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})
, one has

(4.19)

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\tau})=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot\sum_{\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})}{\left\langle\rho|_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}},\tau\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}\cdot\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\rho})

for every
\tau\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\chi})
and
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})
. We want to justify the equality (
4.19
) from
[
Ga7
, Theorem 5.6]
which we briefly recall as follows:

–

there is a representation

\sigma_{0}^{\rm Wh}:\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}\longrightarrow{\rm GL}(\mathbf{C}^{\left|\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}\right|})

which is given by
\sigma_{0}^{\rm Wh}(w)=\mathcal{A}(w,\omega^{\flat})^{*}
, the induced isomorphism of
{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\omega^{\flat}))
of dimension
\left|\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}\right|
. One has

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\rho})={\left\langle\rho,\sigma_{0}^{\rm Wh}\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}

for every
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})
.

Note that one also has a representation

(4.20)

\sigma^{\rm Wh}:\mathcal{S}_{\chi}\longrightarrow{\rm GL}(\mathbf{C}^{\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}\right|})

given by
\sigma^{\rm Wh}(w):=\mathcal{A}(w,\chi)^{*}
for
w\in\mathcal{S}_{\chi}
, and similarly

(4.21)

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\tau})={\left\langle\tau,\sigma^{\rm Wh}\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}

for every
\tau\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\chi})
.
The decomposition

{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\chi))=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|}{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\omega^{\flat})),

which is compatible with the decomposition of
\mathcal{A}(w,\chi)=\bigoplus_{i}\mathcal{A}(w,\omega^{\flat})
, shows that

(4.22)

\sigma^{\rm{\rm Wh}}\simeq\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot(\sigma_{0}^{\rm Wh})|_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}.

It is easy to see that the three equalities (
4.20
), (
4.21
) and (
4.22
) together imply (
4.19
) as well.

Now we explain relations among (
4.19
), Conjecture
4.6
and
[
Ga7
, Conjecture 5.3]
. Thus, for the rest of this subsubsection, we assume that
G_{0}
is simply-connected and
\overline{G}_{0}
is a saturated cover. We have

–

(
[
Ga7
, Conjecture 5.3]
) the equality
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\rho})={\left\langle\rho,\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}}
holds for every
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}})
.

Here

\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}:\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}\hookrightarrow W\longrightarrow{\rm Perm}(\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n})

is the permutation representation given by
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}}(w)=w[\cdot]
. The following is straightforward.

Proposition 4.16
.

Consider a pair
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})
with
G_{0}
being simply-connected and
\overline{G}_{0}
a saturated cover.

(i)

Assume
[
Ga7
, Conjecture 5.3]
, then

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\tau})=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot{\left\langle\tau,(\sigma_{\mathscr{X}_{0}})|_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}

for every
\tau\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\chi})
.

(ii)

Assume both
[
Ga7
, Conjecture 5.3]
and Conjecture
4.6
, then

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi_{\tau})={\left\langle\tau,\sigma_{\mathscr{X}}\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}

for every
\tau\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\chi})
.

Note that if
I(\chi)
is irreducible, then the equalities in Proposition
4.16
hold trivially and unconditionally.
We give two examples below in the non isotypic-pair case.

Example 4.17
.

Let
\overline{G}=\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(n)}
be associated with
\mathbf{p}=0,\mathbf{q}=1
, and
n=2m
is even.
One has

Y_{0,Q,n}=\mathbf{Z}(m\alpha^{\vee}),\ Y_{Q,n}=nY,\ Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}=nY_{0}.

Thus,

\overline{G}^{\vee}={\rm GL}_{2},\ \overline{G}_{0}^{\vee}={\rm SL}_{2}\text{ and }H^{\vee}={\rm PGL}_{2}.

For every
(K,s_{K})
-unramified
I(\chi)
, one has

\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}=\left\{\omega_{0,0},\omega_{0,1}\right\}.

Note that we always have
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}=\left\{1\right\}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\omega_{0,j}}=\left\{1\right\}
for
j=0,1
. Regarding
\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}
, there are two cases:

–

if
\chi(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{n}))\neq-1
, then
\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}=\left\{1\right\}
and thus
\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}(\omega_{0,j})=\left\{\omega_{0,j}\right\}
for each
j
.

–

if
\chi(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{n}))=-1
, then
\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}=W
and we have
\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}(\omega_{0,j})=\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}
for
j=0,1
.

In the second case above, on the dual side the relative Satake parameter
{\rm sat}(\omega_{0,j})
(in the sense of
[
GG
]
if
m
is odd) for
\omega_{0,j}
is

{\rm sat}(\omega_{0,j})=\left(\begin{matrix}\sqrt{-1}^{2j+1}&0\\
0&\sqrt{-1}^{-(2j+1)}\end{matrix}\right)\in\overline{G}_{0}^{\vee}.

This example is easily generalizable to the pair
(\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r}^{(n)},\overline{{\rm SL}}_{r}^{(n)})
with
r|n
. For such a pair, one always has
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}=\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}=\left\{1\right\}
. However, the group
\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}
will be more complicated.

Example 4.18
.

Consider the pair of double covers
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})=(\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{(2)},\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}^{(2)})
as from §
2.4.2
, see also
[
Szp4-1
,
Szp5
]
and
[
Ga7
, §5.5]
. Regarding the dual groups, one has

\overline{G}^{\vee}=\begin{cases}{\rm GSp}_{2r}(\mathbf{C}),\text{ if $r$ is odd;}\\
{\rm PGSp}_{2r}(\mathbf{C})\times{\rm GL}_{1}(\mathbf{C}),\text{ if $r$ is even.}\end{cases}

On the other hand, we have

\overline{G}_{0}^{\vee}={\rm Sp}_{2r},\ \overline{H}^{\vee}={\rm PGSp}_{2r}.

Assume
r
is even. It follows from
[
Key2
, Page 399]
that the only nontrivial
\mathcal{S}_{\chi}=R_{\chi}
is
\left\{1,w\right\}\simeq\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}
which is generated by

w:=w_{\alpha_{1}}w_{\alpha_{3}}...w_{\alpha_{r-1}},

with the character
\chi
satisfying

\chi(\overline{h}_{\alpha_{i}}(\varpi^{n_{\alpha_{i}}}))=-1\text{ for all }i=2k-1,1\leqslant k\leqslant r/2.

For such
\chi
, one has
\Omega_{\chi}^{\rm un}=\left\{\omega_{0,0},\omega_{0,1}\right\}
; also,

\mathcal{S}_{\chi}=\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}=\left\{1,w\right\}\text{ and }\mathcal{S}_{\omega^{\flat}}=\left\{1\right\}

for every
\omega^{\flat}\in\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}
.
In this case, for any
j\in\left\{0,1\right\}
,

\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi}(\omega_{0,j})=\Omega^{\rm un}_{\chi},

which is a torsor over
\mathcal{S}^{\diamondsuit}
.
It follows from Theorem
4.12
that

\dim_{\overline{G}_{0}}(I(\omega_{0,j}),\pi_{\tau})=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot{\left\langle\tau,\mathbf{C}[\mathcal{S}_{\chi}]\right\rangle}_{\mathcal{S}_{\chi}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|=1

for every
\tau\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}_{\chi})
and
j=0,1
.

5.
Unramified L-packets

In this section, we consider the internal structure of an unramified L-packets, the linear algebraic analogue has been investigated in
[
Mis2
]
. The following two topics are pertaining to our discussion:

(i)

the parametrization of elements inside an L-packet with respect to changing the hyperspecial maximal compact subgroups of
G
,

(ii)

the variation of the Whittaker dimension of elements inside an unramified L-packet with respect to different orbits of the Whittaker datum.

Regarding (ii), some earlier work include
[
Kuo1
,
Kuo2
,
Mis1
]
. In this section, we adopt some ideas from these works in the covering setting. The fact that the three groups
G
, its
n
-fold cover
\overline{G}
and the principal endoscopic group
G_{Q,n}
all play a role here necessitates a careful analysis of certain relations among them, which allows us to postulate some natural problems and prove certain results for covers. For this purpose, we first briefly recall the results for (split) linear algebraic groups.

5.1.
Unramified L-packets for linear groups

Let
G
be the
F
-rational points of a linear algebraic group
{\bf G}
, and
T
the
F
-points of
{\bf T}
. For a character

\underline{\chi}:T\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

which is trivial on
T_{0}:={\bf T}(O)
, one obtains on the dual side an unramified parameter

\phi_{\underline{\chi}}:\text{WD}_{F}\longrightarrow{}^{L}T

which is trivial on
I_{F}\subset W_{F}
and
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbf{C})\subset\text{WD}_{F}
. We call
\phi_{\underline{\chi}}
an unramified L-parameter valued in
{}^{L}T
. Post-composing
\phi_{\underline{\chi}}
with the natural inclusion

{}^{L}T\longrightarrow{}^{L}G

gives a parameter which is still denoted by

\phi_{\underline{\chi}}:W_{F}\longrightarrow{}^{L}G.

The local Langlands correspondence postulates that the L-packet
\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})
associated with
\phi_{\underline{\chi}}
consists exactly of the subquotients of the principal series
I(\underline{\chi})
, which are
K
-unramified with respect to a hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup
K
of
G
, see
[
Bor
, §10.4]
. Moreover, it is expected that there is a bijection

\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})\longleftrightarrow{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})),

where
\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})
is the component group of the image of the parameter
\phi_{\underline{\chi}}
in
{}^{L}G
.

If we conveniently index the packet as

\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})=\left\{\pi(\phi_{\underline{\chi}},\rho):\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}}))\right\},

then as remarked each
\pi(\phi_{\underline{\chi}},\rho)
is
K
-unramified for some hyperspecial subgroup
K\subset G
. It is natural to ask how the indexing by
\rho
varies with respect to changing the hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup
K
. Following
[
Mis2
]
, we describe the answer to this question which embodies part of the internal structure of
\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})
.

Consider the root datum

(X,\Phi,\Delta;\ Y,\Phi^{\vee},\Delta^{\vee})

of the group
{\bf G}
, and also recall the root lattice
X^{sc}=\mathbf{Z}[\Delta]
and coroot lattice
Y^{sc}=\mathbf{Z}[\Delta^{\vee}]
. One has the standard affine Weyl group

W_{\rm a}=X^{sc}\rtimes W

acting on the vector space
X^{sc}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{R}
. On the other hand, there is also a natural action of the extended affine Weyl group

\tilde{W}_{\rm a}=X\rtimes W

on
X\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{R}
. Defining
\Gamma:=\tilde{W}_{\rm a}/W_{\rm a}
, one has a split short exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\rm a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{W}_{\rm a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}

with a splitting given by

s(\Gamma)=\left\{x\in\tilde{W}_{\rm a}:\ x\cdot C=C\right\},

where
C\subset X\otimes\mathbf{R}
is an alcove for the action of
\tilde{W}_{\rm a}
on
X\otimes\mathbf{R}
.
That is, we have

\tilde{W}_{\rm a}=W_{\rm a}\rtimes\Gamma

with

\Gamma\simeq X/X^{sc}.

Note that
Z(G)={\rm Hom}(\Gamma,F^{\times})
. For every lattice
L
, we write

L_{\mathbf{Q}}:=L\otimes\mathbf{Q}

for the
\mathbf{Q}
-vector space. It is easy to see that

\Gamma^{\rm tor}=(X\cap X^{sc}_{\mathbf{Q}})/X^{sc},

where
\Gamma^{\rm tor}\subset\Gamma
denotes the torsion-subgroup. Denote by
Y_{ad}
the cocharacter group of
{\bf G}_{ad}
, the adjoint quotient group of
{\bf G}
. Then
Y_{ad}\subset\mathbf{Q}[\Delta^{\vee}]
is the lattice of coweights, which is
\mathbf{Z}
-dual to
\mathbf{Z}[\Delta]
. Consider

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}:={\rm Coker}(Y\longrightarrow Y_{ad}),

we see that there is an embedding

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\Gamma}:={\rm Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbf{Q}/\mathbf{Z}).

In fact, the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma^{\rm tor}}}

is an isomorphism, see
[
Mis2
, Lemma 11]
.

Consider the set

\mathcal{K}=\left\{G_{x}:\ x\text{ is a hyperspecial point in }\mathscr{B}(G)\right\}/_{\sim}

of conjugacy classes of hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup of
G
, where
\mathscr{B}(G)
is the Bruhat-Tits building associated with
{\bf G}
. Here
\mathcal{K}
is a torsor over
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}
with the action
y\cdot G_{x},y\in\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}
inherited from the conjugation action of
G_{ad}:={\bf G}_{ad}(F)
on
G
. One has

y\cdot G_{x}=G_{y\cdot x}\in\mathcal{K},

where
y\cdot x
is the action of
G_{ad}
on
\mathscr{B}(G)
, see
[
Tits
, §2.5]
.

On the other hand, there is also a natural map from
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}
to
\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})
given as follows, by reducing to the case of unitary
\underline{\chi}
first (see
[
Mis2
, §10.3]
). More precisely, denoting
\phi:=\phi_{\underline{\chi}}
for simplicity, it gives a commuting pair

\phi_{o},\ \phi_{+}:W_{F}\longrightarrow{}^{L}T\subset{}^{L}G

of parameters such that
\phi_{o}
is tempered and

\phi(a)=\phi_{o}(a)\cdot\phi_{+}(a)

for every
a\in W_{F}
. Let

\underline{\chi}_{+}:T\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

be the character attached to
\phi_{+}
. Then the set

\Delta_{M}:=\left\{\alpha\in\Delta:\ \left|\underline{\chi}_{+}(\alpha^{\vee}(\varpi))\right|=1\right\}\subset\Delta

gives a Levi subgroup
{\bf M}\subset{\bf G}
such that
{\rm Im}(\phi)\subset M^{\vee}
and

\mathcal{S}_{M^{\vee}}(\phi_{o})=\mathcal{S}_{G^{\vee}}(\phi).

Moreover, it is shown (see
[
Mis2
, Lemma 15]
) that one has two embeddings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{M^{\vee}}(\phi_{o})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{M}^{\rm tor}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}.}

This shows in particular that the group
\mathcal{S}(\phi)
is abelian. By applying
{\rm Hom}(-,\mathbf{Q}/\mathbf{Z})
we obtain a surjection (writing
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}=\widehat{\Gamma}_{G}^{\dagger}
and omitting
G^{\vee}
in
\mathcal{S}_{G^{\vee}}(\phi)
)

(5.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\Gamma}:\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi)).}

Theorem 5.1

(
[
Mis2
, Theorem 1]
)
.

For every conjugacy class
K\in\mathcal{K}
and every
y\in\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}
, the representation
\pi(\phi_{\underline{\chi}},\rho)\in\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})
is K-unramified if and only if
\pi(\phi_{\chi},f_{\Gamma}(y)\otimes\rho)
is
y\cdot K
-unramified.

If we choose
K\in\mathcal{K}
, and require that
\pi(\phi_{\underline{\chi}},\mathbbm{1})\in{\rm JH}(I(\underline{\chi}))
is the unique
K
-unramified constituent of
I(\chi)
, then the above theorem implies that
\pi(\phi_{\underline{\chi}},\rho),\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}}))
is the unique
y\cdot K
-unramified constituent in
I(\underline{\chi})
, where
y\in f_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\rho)
is any lifting of
\rho
.

5.2.
Variation of
(K,s_{K})
for covers

5.2.1.
Splittings over
K

Now we consider an
n
-fold cover
\overline{G}
of
G
, and still denote by
\mathcal{K}
the set of conjugacy classes of hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup of
G
.

Lemma 5.2
.

With the assumption that
\gcd(n,p)=1
and
\eta_{n}:Y^{sc}\to F^{\times}/F^{\times n}
is trivial, the group
\overline{G}
splits over every hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup
K\in\mathcal{K}
.

Proof.

We presents two arguments. First, since
K
is hyperspecial, one has
K={\bf G}_{O}(O)
for a smooth group scheme
{\bf G}_{O}
over
{\rm Spec}(O)
. By
[
BD
, Construction 12.11]
, one knows that
\overline{K}\subset\overline{G}
is the pull-back of a residual extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{{\bf G}_{O}(\kappa)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf G}_{O}(\kappa).}

The proof of
[
GG
, Proposition 4.1]
, which was written for
{\bf G}(O)
, also applies for such
K
and gives that the above extension splits, if we assume the triviality of
\eta_{n}
. Thus, we have a splitting of
\overline{K}
over
K
. Note that the existence of such a splitting also follow from
[
We4
, Theorem 4.3]
.

Alternatively, we note that
\overline{G}
splits over
K_{\natural}={\bf G}(O)
by
[
GG
, Theorem 4.2]
, and thus we let
s:K_{\natural}\hookrightarrow\overline{G}
be a fixed splitting. The conjugation action of
G_{ad}
on
G
extends to
\overline{G}
(see
[
BD
]
), and the group
G_{ad}
acts transitively on
\mathcal{K}
. We see that for
t\cdot K_{\natural}\in\mathcal{K},t\in G_{ad}
, one has a splitting

t\cdot s:\ t\cdot K_{\natural}\hookrightarrow\overline{G}

given by

(t\cdot s)(t\cdot k):=t\cdot s(k)\cdot t^{-1},k\in K_{\natural}.

This is a well-defined splitting of
\overline{G}
over the hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup
y\cdot K_{\natural}\subset G
.
∎

We impose the same assumption in the Lemma
5.2
throughout the remaining part of this section. For every
K\in\mathcal{K}
, the set

{\rm Spl}(\overline{G},K)

of splittings of
\overline{G}
over
K
is a torsor over
{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})
. Since
G=T\cdot G_{\rm der}
and thus
K=(T\cap K)\cdot(G_{\rm der}\cap K)
, we see that every homomorphism
h\in{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})
must be trivial on
K\cap G_{\rm der}
. Hence, the restriction map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Hom}(T\cap K,\mu_{n})}

is an injection, where
T\cap K={\bf T}(O)
. As the restriction of
h
to
T_{\rm der}\subset T
is also trivial, and the above injection factors through the inclusion

{\rm Hom}({\bf T}(O)/{\bf T}_{\rm der}(O),\mu_{n})\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}({\bf T}(O),\mu_{n})

we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{K}:{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Hom}({\bf T}(O)/{\bf T}_{\rm der}(O),\mu_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Hom}({\bf T}(O),\mu_{n}).}

Note that we do not consider the whole set
{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})
, but only a subset of “admissible” elements constrained as follows.
Let
(K,s_{K})
be such that
K\in\mathcal{K}
and
s_{K}\in{\rm Spl}(\overline{G},K)
. If a representation
\pi
is
(K,s_{K})
-unramified, then it is also
(K,h\otimes s_{K})
-unramified if
\iota_{K}(h)
is trivial on
{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O)\subset T
, where by abuse of notation we use
{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O)
to denote the image of
{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O)
in
{\bf T}(O)
with respect to the natural map
T_{Q,n}\to T
. For this reason, we define

{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})^{\natural}:=\iota_{K}^{-1}\left({\rm Hom}({\bf T}(O)/{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O),\mu_{n})\right).

We thus have natural injections (still denoted by
\iota_{K}
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{K}:{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})^{\natural}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Hom}({\bf T}(O)/{\bf T}_{\rm der}(O)\cdot{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O),\mu_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Hom}({\bf T}(O)/{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O),\mu_{n}).}

Since
\overline{A}:=C_{\overline{T}}({\bf T}(O))={\bf T}(O)\cdot Z(\overline{T})
is a maximal abelian subgroup of
\overline{T}
, the commutator map gives a perfect pairing

[-,-]:\overline{T}/\overline{A}\times{\bf T}(O)/{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O)\longrightarrow\mu_{n},

which gives a group isomorphism

{\rm Hom}({\bf T}(O)/{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O),\mu_{n})\longrightarrow Y/Y_{Q,n}\text{ given by }[y(\varpi),-]\leftrightarrow y.

By composing with
\iota_{K}
, we obtain a natural injection

(5.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{K}^{\natural}:{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})^{\natural}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y/Y_{Q,n}.}

We denote

(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}:={\rm Im}(i_{K}^{\natural})\subset Y/Y_{Q,n}

and also for
z\in(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}
, we write

f_{z}\in{\rm Hom}(K,\mu)^{\natural}

for the unique element such that
i_{K}^{\natural}(f_{z})=z
.

If we define

(5.3)

Y_{K}^{\natural}=\left\{y\in Y:B_{Q}(y,z)\in n\mathbf{Z}\text{ for all }z\in Y_{0}\right\},

which clearly contains
Y_{Q,n}
, then one has the inclusions

(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}\hookrightarrow Y_{K}^{\natural}/Y_{Q,n}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}.

The first inclusion is often an identity. It is also easy to see that there is a commutative diagram

(5.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0,Q,n}/Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0}/Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0}/Y_{0,Q,n},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\Gamma}}

where the map
p_{\Gamma}:(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}\to\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}
obtained from restriction is well-defined in view of (
2.20
) and (
2.21
), and the middle horizontal and left vertical maps are short exact sequences.

5.2.2.
A relation between
\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
and
\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}

For
G
we have the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{Q,n}/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{Q,n}/X_{G}^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/X_{G}^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/(X\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/X_{G}^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/X_{G}^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}.}

Here
X_{Q,n}={\rm Hom}(Y_{Q,n},\mathbf{Z})
and we have used the subscript
G
in
X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc}=X_{G}^{sc}\otimes\mathbf{Q}
(for example) to emphasize the underlying group involved. Similarly, one has for a Levi subgroup
M
the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{Q,n}/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{Q,n}/X_{G}^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/X_{M}^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{M,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/(X\cap X_{M,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{M,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/X_{M}^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X\cap X_{M,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/X_{M}^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{M}^{\rm tor}.}

The above two diagrams are compatible, and for the bottom two exact sequences this is illustrated from the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{Q,n}/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/(X\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/X_{G}^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{M,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/(X\cap X_{M,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{M,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/X_{M}^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{M}^{\rm tor}.}

Denote by

P(L)\subset\mathbf{Q}[\Delta^{\vee}]

the
\mathbf{Z}
-dual of the lattice
L\subset\mathbf{Q}[\Delta]
. Applying
{\rm Hom}(-,\mathbf{Q}/\mathbf{Z})
to the above diagram, we obtain

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y/Y_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(X\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/P(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(X_{G}^{sc})/P(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}.}

For the principal endoscopic group
G_{Q,n}
of
\overline{G}
, one has the group
\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}
and
\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}
defined analogously as for
\Gamma_{G}
and
\Gamma_{G}^{sc}
. Thus,

\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}=X_{Q,n}/X_{Q,n}^{sc}\text{ and }\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}=(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/X_{Q,n}^{sc},

where the last equality follows from the fact that
X_{G_{Q,n},\mathbf{Q}}^{sc}=X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc}
. This gives that

\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}=P(X_{Q,n}^{sc})/P(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc}).

The discussion in §
5.1
applies to
G_{Q,n}
. In particular, the set of conjugacy classes of hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup of
G_{Q,n}
is a torsor over
\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}
.
Moreover, for every genuine character
\chi:Z(\overline{T})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
, which is
s_{Q,n}({\bf T}(O))
-unramified with respect to a splitting
s_{Q,n}
of
{\bf T}(O)
, there is the associated parameter

\phi_{\chi}:W_{F}\longrightarrow{}^{L}\overline{T}\hookrightarrow{}^{L}\overline{G}.

One has a surjection

\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})).

To relate the two group
\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
and
\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}
, we note that there is a natural map

\varphi:P(X_{G}^{sc})/P(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})\longrightarrow\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}

given as follows. Recall that
X_{Q,n}^{sc}
is generated by
\left\{\alpha/n_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\Delta\right\}
, and thus there is a group homomorphism

\varphi_{0}:X_{Q,n}^{sc}\longrightarrow X_{G}^{sc}

uniquely determined by

\varphi_{0}(\alpha/n_{\alpha})=\alpha\text{ for every }\alpha\in\Delta.

This gives a well-defined map
\varphi_{0}
as in

(5.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/(X\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/X_{G}^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/X_{G}^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/X_{Q,n}^{sc}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{0}}

By taking applying
{\rm Hom}(-,\mathbf{Q}/\mathbf{Z})
, we obtain the desired homomorphism
\varphi
. In fact, let

\left\{\omega_{\alpha_{i}}\right\}\subset\mathbf{Q}[\Delta^{\vee}]

be the set of fundamental coweights such that

{\left\langle\omega_{\alpha_{i}},\alpha_{j}\right\rangle}=\delta_{ij}

for every
\alpha_{j}\in\Delta
. We have

P(X_{G}^{sc})=\mathbf{Z}[\omega_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\Delta].

Similarly,

P(X_{Q,n}^{sc})=\mathbf{Z}[n_{\alpha}\omega_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\Delta]

Then
\varphi
is induced from the map

P(X_{G}^{sc})\longrightarrow P(X_{Q,n}^{sc})

given by

\omega_{\alpha}\mapsto n_{\alpha}w_{\alpha}

for every
\alpha\in\Delta
. In particular,
\varphi
is surjective.

As a summary of the above discussion, we have the following diagram

(5.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y/Y_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(X\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/P(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(X_{G}^{sc})/P(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\varphi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(X_{Q,n}^{sc})/P(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}/H_{\varphi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

Here by definition
H_{\varphi}
is the image of
P(X\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/P(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})
via
\varphi
. The following compressed diagram will play a key role in our subsequent discussion:

(5.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})^{\natural}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y/Y_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\varphi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}/H_{\varphi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{K}^{\natural}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi\circ h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\chi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{\varphi}?}

5.2.3.
The packet
\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})
versus unramifiedness

For every
K\in\mathcal{K}
and
y\in\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
the action
y\cdot K
is realized by the conjugacy action of
y(\varpi)\in T_{\rm ad}
on
K
. For every
f\in{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})
, by transport of structure, we define

y\cdot f:\ y\cdot K\longrightarrow\mu_{n}

by

(y\cdot f)(y(\varpi)\cdot k\cdot y(\varpi)^{-1}):=f(k).

Recall that
G_{ad}
and thus
T_{ad}
acts on
\overline{G}
. As used in Lemma
5.2
, if
s_{K}:K\hookrightarrow\overline{G}
is a splitting, then we obtain a splitting

y\cdot s_{K}:\ y\cdot K\longrightarrow\overline{G}

of
y\cdot K\in\mathcal{K}
given by

(y\cdot s_{K})(y(\varpi)\cdot k\cdot y(\varpi)^{-1}):=y(\varpi)\cdot s_{K}(k)\cdot y(\varpi)^{-1}.

Definition 5.3
.

For
K,K^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}
, two splittings
s_{K}:K\hookrightarrow\overline{G}
and
s_{K^{\prime}}:K^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\overline{G}
are called associated if
they agree on
{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O)
.

In particular, if
K=K^{\prime}
, then
s_{K}
and
s_{K}^{\prime}=f\otimes s_{K},f\in{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})
are associated only if
f\in{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})^{\natural}
.

Conjecture 5.4
.

There is a canonical splitting
s_{\varphi}
of
\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}
over
\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}/H_{\varphi}
, as depicted in (
5.7
). Moreover, there is a bijection

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi}))}

which we denote conveniently by
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)\leftrightarrow\rho
, such that

–

for every
K\in\mathcal{K}
and splitting
s_{K}:K\hookrightarrow\overline{G}
with
s_{K}=s_{Q,n}
on
{\bf T}(O)
,

–

for every
f_{z}\in{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})^{\natural}
,

–

for every
y\in\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
with
y\cdot s_{K}
being associated with
s_{K}
,

one has that
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)
is
(K,s_{K})
-unramified if and only if
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\gamma_{z,y}\cdot\rho)
is
(y\cdot K,(y\cdot f_{z})\otimes(y\cdot s_{K}))
-unramified, where

\gamma_{z,y}=\big(\varphi\circ h\circ i_{K}^{\natural}(f_{z})\big)\cdot\big(s_{\varphi}\circ\varphi(y)\big)\in\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}.

Note that
y\cdot f_{z}\in{\rm Hom}(y\cdot K,\mu_{n})^{\natural}
and in fact
y\cdot f_{z}\otimes y\cdot s_{K}=y\cdot(f_{z}\otimes s_{K})
.

Remark 5.5
.

There is an action of
\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
on the set

\tilde{\mathcal{K}}=\left\{(K,s_{K}):K\in\mathcal{K}\text{ and $s_{K}$ is a splitting of $K$ into }\overline{G}\right\}/_{\sim},

where
\sim
means modulo the conjugation action of
G
. The action is still free, but will be not be transitive in general. This implies that the notion of unramifiedness is more delicate for covering groups.

5.2.4.
Different models of
I(\chi)

To facilitate later computations, we provide some further analysis of a
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series
I(\chi)
of
\overline{G}
, especially on its different concrete realizations. Recall that
\chi:Z(\overline{T})\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
is a central character and

\tilde{\chi}:\overline{A}\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

an extension to a maximal abelian subgroup
\overline{A}\subset\overline{T}
. The isomorphism class
i(\chi)
only depends on
\chi
, and not on the chosen
\overline{A}
and the extension
\tilde{\chi}
. The commutator pairing

[-,-]:\overline{T}/Z(\overline{T})\times\overline{T}/Z(\overline{T})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

is non-degenerate, and thus every character of
\overline{T}/Z(\overline{T})
is of the form
[-,t]
for some
t\in\overline{T}
. In particular, if we fix
\overline{A}
and consider two extensions
\tilde{\chi},\tilde{\chi}^{\prime}
of
\chi
to
\overline{A}
, we get a character

\tilde{\chi}/\tilde{\chi}^{\prime}:\overline{A}/Z(\overline{T})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times},

then there exists
t\in\overline{T}
such that

\tilde{\chi}^{\prime}={}^{t}\tilde{\chi},

where

{}^{t}\tilde{\chi}(x)=\tilde{\chi}(t^{-1}xt)=[x,t]\cdot\tilde{\chi}(x).

For any fixed lifting
\overline{t}\in\overline{T}
of
t
, we have an isomorphism

\phi_{\overline{t}}:{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}}^{\overline{T}}(\tilde{\chi})\longrightarrow{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}}^{\overline{T}}({}^{t}\tilde{\chi})

given by

\phi_{\overline{t}}(f)(x)=f(\overline{t}^{-1}x).

Hence, there is an induced isomorphism

(5.8)

\Phi_{\overline{t}}:{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(\tilde{\chi})\longrightarrow{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}({}^{t}\tilde{\chi})

given by

\Phi_{\overline{t}}(f)(g)=f(\overline{t}^{-1}g)\text{ for }g\in\overline{G},

where we note that the two sides are different models for the same isomorphism class
I(\chi)
.

5.3.
Whittaker datum varied

We consider in this subsection how the Whittaker dimension of an element inside an unramified L-packet varies with respect to different Whittaker data.

5.3.1.
Linear case

First we recall the results in the linear case, see
[
Kuo1
,
Kuo2
,
GGP1
,
Kal4
,
Mis1
]
.
Let

\mathfrak{w}=(B,U,\psi)

be a fixed Whittaker datum such that
\psi:U\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
is a non-degenerate character. The group
T={\bf T}(F)
acts on the set of such non-degenerate characters
\psi
and gives finite orbits. On the other hand, from the short exact sequence

Z({\bf G})\hookrightarrow{\bf G}\twoheadrightarrow{\bf G}_{ad}

we obtain by Galois cohomology

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z(G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{ad}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(F,Z({\bf G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...,}

where
G
and
G_{ad}
denote the
F
-points of
{\bf G}
and
{\bf G}_{ad}
respectively. One has an embedding

G_{ad}/G\hookrightarrow H^{1}(F,Z({\bf G}))

and also an isomorphism

T_{ad}/T\simeq G_{ad}/G,

which acts simply and transitively on the set of
T
-orbits of non-degenerate characters of
U
. We denote the action by
{}^{t}\mathfrak{w}
for every
t\in T_{ad}
, or simply
{}^{t}\psi
, since the action of
t
is only on
\psi
.

On the other hand, for every unramified
\underline{\chi}
, there is an embedding

\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})\hookrightarrow X/X^{sc},

and by applying
{\rm Hom}(-,\mathbf{Q}/\mathbf{Z})
we obtain the surjection

Y_{ad}/Y=P(X^{sc})/Y\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})),

where
Y_{ad}=P(X^{sc})
is the cocharacter lattice of
{\bf G}_{ad}
. Note that the valuation map
v_{F}:F^{\times}\to\mathbf{Z}
induces a well-defined map

v_{F}:T_{ad}/T\longrightarrow Y_{ad}/Y.

We thus have a surjection

(5.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{ad}/T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})).}

In fact, by using Tate duality, one can define a natural map (see
[
Mis1
, §3.1]
)

H^{1}(F,Z({\bf G}))\longrightarrow{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})),

the composite of which with the inclusion
T_{ad}/T\hookrightarrow H^{1}(F,Z({\bf G}))
gives rise to (
5.9
).

It is shown in
[
Mis1
, Theorem 12]
that for an unramified L-packet
\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})
associated with an unramified
\underline{\chi}
, the representation
\pi(\phi_{\underline{\chi}},\rho)
is
\psi
-generic if and only if
\pi(\phi_{\underline{\chi}},t\cdot\rho)
is
{}^{t}\psi
-generic, for every
t\in T_{ad}
. Here
\psi
-genericity of
\pi\in{\rm Irr}(G)
means that

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi)=1.

Moreover,
\pi(\phi_{\underline{\chi}},t\cdot\rho)
is the unique
{}^{t}\psi
-generic element in
\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\underline{\chi}})
for the non-degenerate character
{}^{t}\psi
, see
[
Var1
,
Ato1
]
and references therein.

5.3.2.
Speculations for coverings

It is natural to consider the analogous question for covering groups and an unramified L-packet
\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})
, by viewing
U
as a subgroup of
\overline{G}
via the canonical splitting. However, similar to the earlier discussion in the preceding subsection, we encounter the problem that on the one hand the Whittaker datum has a natural action by
T_{ad}/T
, depending on
G
; on the other hand, the
\mathcal{S}
-group pertains to the dual group
\overline{G}^{\vee}
and thus to the principal endoscopic group
G_{Q,n}
. It is therefore crucial to relate the two aspects.

We denote by
G_{Q,n,ad}
and
T_{Q,n,ad}
the
F
-rational points of the quotient adjoint group
{\bf G}_{Q,n,ad}
of
{\bf G}_{Q,n}
and the split torus
{\bf T}_{Q,n,ad}
inside
{\bf G}_{Q,n,ad}
, respectively. We ask when is there a natural map

T_{ad}/T\longrightarrow T_{Q,n,ad}/T_{Q,n}?

In fact, for our purpose, it suffices to consider if there is a natural map

X_{Q,n}/X_{Q,n}^{sc}\longrightarrow X/X^{sc},

or equivalently, a map

Z({\bf G})\longrightarrow Z({\bf G}_{Q,n}).

For simplicity of discussion, we assume that
{\bf G}
is a semsimple group through the rest of this subsection. In this case, one has from (
5.5
) the following diagram

(5.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{Q,n}/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{Q,n}/X^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/X^{sc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{Q,n}/X_{Q,n}^{sc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\varphi}_{0}}

Taking
\varphi_{0}
to be the natural candidate, the question is whether it factors through
j_{0}
. In the special case of
G
being almost simple and simply-connected, the answer is as follows.

Proposition 5.6
.

Assume
{\bf G}
is almost simple and simply-connected. If

(G,\overline{G}^{\vee})\neq({\rm Spin}_{2r+1},{\rm SO}_{2r+1}),

where the equality holds exactly when
r
is odd and
n_{\alpha}\equiv 2\mod 4
for every short coroot
\alpha^{\vee}
of
{\rm Spin}_{2r+1}
, then the map
\varphi_{0}
factors through
j_{0}
, i.e.,

\varphi_{0}(X_{Q,n}/X_{Q,n}^{sc})\subset X/X^{sc}.

Proof.

For every
x=\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta}c_{\alpha}\alpha\in X_{Q,n},c_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Q}
, we want to show that

\varphi_{0}(x)=\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta}c_{\alpha}n_{\alpha}\alpha

lies in
X
, or equivalently,

{\left\langle\varphi_{0}(x),y\right\rangle}=\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta}c_{\alpha}n_{\alpha}{\left\langle\alpha,y\right\rangle}

lies in
\mathbf{Z}
for every
y\in Y
. Our argument is essentially a case by case discussion. First, assume
{\bf G}
is simply-laced. Then
\alpha\mapsto n_{\alpha}
is constant on
\Delta
and we have

Y_{Q,n}^{sc}=n_{\alpha}\cdot Y\subset Y_{Q,n}.

In this case,

{\left\langle\varphi_{0}(x),y\right\rangle}={\left\langle x,n_{\alpha}y\right\rangle}\in\mathbf{Z},

since
x\in X_{Q,n}
and
n_{\alpha}y\in Y_{Q,n}
. Thus, we are left with
{\bf G}
being of type
B_{r},C_{r}
. Note that though
F_{4}
and
G_{2}
have two lengths for the simple roots, we always have
X_{Q,n}=X_{Q,n}^{sc}
for such groups, and thus there is nothing to check. The two nontrivial cases will be for
B_{r},C_{r}
and when
[X_{Q,n}:X_{Q,n}^{sc}]=2
, i.e., when the dual group
\overline{G}^{\vee}
of
\overline{G}
is of adjoint type. In this case
Y_{Q,n}=Y_{Q,n}^{sc}
.

First, if
{\bf G}={\rm Sp}_{2r}
and
\overline{G}^{\vee}
is adjoint, then
G_{Q,n}={\rm Sp}_{2r}
as well. In particular,
G_{Q,n}
has the same root system type as
G
, and thus
\alpha\mapsto n_{\alpha}
is constant on
\Delta
. In this case,

Y_{Q,n}^{sc}=n_{\alpha}\cdot Y^{sc}=Y.

Hence
{\left\langle\varphi_{0}(x),y\right\rangle}\in\mathbf{Z}
for all
x\in X_{Q,n}
and
y\in Y
, and this gives
\varphi_{0}(x)\in X
.

Second, if
{\bf G}={\rm Spin}_{2r+1}
and
\overline{G}^{\vee}
is adjoint, then there are exactly two possibilities as follows. Assume that
\alpha_{r}^{\vee}
is unique long simple coroot in
\Delta^{\vee}
, and
\alpha_{i}^{\vee}\in\Delta^{\vee}
is short for
1\leqslant i\leqslant r-1
.

\overline{G}^{\vee}=\begin{cases}{\rm PGSp}_{2r}&\text{ if $n_{\alpha_{1}}$ is odd},\\
{\rm SO}_{2r+1}&\text{ if $r$ is odd and $n_{\alpha_{1}}\equiv 2\mod 4$}.\end{cases}

Now if
\overline{G}^{\vee}={\rm PGSp}_{2r}
, then
G_{Q,n}={\rm Spin}_{2r+1}
and thus
\alpha\mapsto n_{\alpha}
is constant on
\Delta
. In this case, the same argument as above gives that
\varphi_{0}(x)\in X
for every
x\in X_{Q,n}
. Now if
\overline{G}^{\vee}={\rm SO}_{2r+1}
, then we get
G_{Q,n}={\rm Sp}_{2r}
with

X_{Q,n}^{sc}=\mathbf{Z}[\alpha_{1}/n_{\alpha_{1}}]\oplus\mathbf{Z}[\alpha_{2}/n_{\alpha_{2}}]\oplus...\oplus\mathbf{Z}[\alpha_{r}/n_{\alpha_{r}}]

where
n_{\alpha_{r}}=n_{\alpha_{1}}/2
, and also

X_{Q,n}^{sc}=\mathbf{Z}[\alpha_{1}/n_{\alpha_{1}}]\oplus\mathbf{Z}[\alpha_{2}/n_{\alpha_{1}}]\oplus...\oplus\mathbf{Z}[\alpha_{r}/n_{\alpha_{1}}]

Thus,
\varphi_{0}(\alpha_{r}/n_{\alpha_{1}})=\alpha_{r}/2\notin X
. This completes the proof.
∎

To proceed with our discussion, we assume
\varphi_{0}
factors through
j_{0}
and thus one has a well-defined map

\tilde{\varphi}_{0}:X_{Q,n}/X_{Q,n}^{sc}\longrightarrow X/X_{G}^{sc}.

Note that since we assumed
G
is semisimple, one has
\Gamma_{G}=\Gamma^{\rm tor}
, and similarly for
G_{Q,n}
. For every unramified genuine character
\chi:Z(\overline{T})\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
, this gives a commutative diagram

(5.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(X^{sc})/Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{ad}/T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(F,Z({\bf G}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(X_{Q,n}^{sc})/Y_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{Q,n,ad}/T_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(F,Z({\bf G}_{Q,n}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\chi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{F}}

where
\varphi
is induced from
\varphi_{0}
and the right vertical map arises from
\tilde{\varphi}_{0}^{*}:Z({\bf G})\longrightarrow Z({\bf G}_{Q,n})
and the functoriality of Galois cohomology. By our assumption on
\varphi_{0}
, the map

\varphi:P(X^{sc})/Y_{Q,n}\twoheadrightarrow P(X_{Q,n}^{sc})/Y_{Q,n}

as in (
5.6
) factors through the quotient
\varphi:P(X^{sc})/Y_{Q,n}\twoheadrightarrow\widehat{\Gamma_{G}}
, giving rise to the map (still denoted by)
\varphi
in (
5.11
). For convenience, we denote by

\zeta_{\chi}:=q_{\chi}\circ\varphi\circ v_{F}:T_{ad}/T\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi}))

for the composed surjection.

Conjecture 5.7
.

Assume that
\tilde{\varphi}_{0}:X_{Q,n}/X_{Q,n}^{sc}\longrightarrow X/X_{G}^{sc}
is well-defined. Then for every Whittaker data
\mathfrak{w}=(B,U,\psi)
and
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series
I(\chi)
, one has

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho))=\dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{t}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\zeta_{\chi}(t)\otimes\rho))

for every
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)\in\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})
and
t\in T_{ad}/T
.

Remark 5.8
.

It seems to us that the map
\varphi_{0}:X_{Q,n}/X_{Q,n}^{sc}\to X_{Q,n}/X^{sc}
considered here is the most natural one, though its image may not be in
X/X^{sc}
, as shown in Proposition
5.6
even for
G
almost simple and simply-connected. On the one hand, one could definitely consider any other “natural” map
\varphi_{0}^{\prime}:X_{Q,n}/X_{Q,n}^{sc}\to X_{Q,n}/X^{sc}
whose image lies in
X/X^{sc}
, for example,
x\mapsto nx
. However, such a map may not be injective and thus the induced map

\varphi^{\prime}:P(X^{sc})/Y\to P(X_{Q,n}^{sc})/Y_{Q,n}

may not be surjective. It is expected that the analogue of Conjecture
5.7
may not hold for such
\varphi^{\prime}
. Indeed, if
\varphi_{0}^{\prime}
is the trivial map, then the action of
t\in T_{ad}/T
on
{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi}))
via
\varphi^{\prime}
is trivial; however,
\dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{t}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho))
may not equal to
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho))
. On the other hand, we expect that a cover
\overline{G}
such that
G_{Q,n}
has simply-connected derived group but of a different root system type is quite unusual and exhibits many interesting properties. The pair

(G,\overline{G}^{\vee})=({\rm Spin}_{2r+1},{\rm SO}_{2r+1})

in Proposition
5.6
is such an example. Another example is

(G,\overline{G}^{\vee})=({\rm GSp}_{2r},{\rm PGSp}_{2r}\times{\rm GL}_{1})

for even fold cover of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
with
r
even, see §
3.4.2
. For this latter example, we will have a more detailed analysis in the last section §
6
of this paper.

5.3.3.
Scattering matrix for
\mathfrak{f}(\psi)=\mathfrak{p}_{F}

To facilitate some computations later, we recall briefly the scattering matrix associated with
T(w,i(\chi))
when

\mathfrak{f}(\psi)=\mathfrak{p}_{F}.

We use freely the notations in
[
GSS2
]
and those in earlier sections of this paper. There is a square matrix

[\tau(w,\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})]_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\overline{T}/\overline{A}}

of size
\left|Y/Y_{Q,n}\right|
representing the operator

T(w,\chi;r_{w}^{\rm un})^{*}:{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I({}^{w}\chi))\longrightarrow{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\chi))

such that

T(w,\chi;r_{w}^{\rm un})^{*}(\lambda_{\gamma}^{{}^{w}\chi})=\sum_{\gamma^{\prime}\in\overline{T}/\overline{A}}\tau(w,\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\cdot\lambda_{\gamma^{\prime}}^{\chi}.

In particular, one has a local scattering matrix

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{R}}(w,\chi):=[\tau(w,\chi,\mathbf{s}_{y_{1}},\mathbf{s}_{y})]_{y_{1},y\in\mathfrak{R}}

indexed by
\mathfrak{R}
for every ordered set
\mathfrak{R}\subset Y
of representatives
Y/Y_{Q,n}
. Some immediate properties are:

\bullet

For
w\in W
and
\overline{z},\overline{z}^{\prime}\in\overline{A}
, the identity holds:

\tau(w,\chi,\gamma\cdot\overline{z},\gamma^{\prime}\cdot\overline{z}^{\prime})=({}^{w}\chi)^{-1}(\overline{z})\cdot\tau(w,\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\cdot\chi(\overline{z}^{\prime}).

\bullet

For
w_{1},w_{2}\in W
such that
l(w_{2}w_{1})=l(w_{2})+l(w_{1})
, one has

\tau(w_{2}w_{1},\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})=\sum_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{T}/\overline{A}}\tau(w_{2},{}^{w_{1}}\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime\prime})\cdot\tau(w_{1},\chi,\gamma^{\prime\prime},\gamma^{\prime}),

which is referred to as the cocycle relation.

To explicate the matrix for a simple reflection
w=w_{\alpha}
associated with
\alpha\in\Delta
, we fix the Haar measure
\mu
of
F
such that
\mu(O_{F})=1
; thus,
\mu(O_{F}^{\times})=1-1/q
. The Gauss sum is defined by

G_{\psi}(a,b)=\int_{O^{\times}_{F}}(u,\varpi)_{n}^{a}\cdot\psi(\varpi^{b}u)\mu(u),\quad a,b\in\mathbf{Z}.

It satisfies (compared with
[
GSS2
, §3.6]
, noting that
\psi
has conductor
\mathfrak{p}_{F}
)

G_{\psi}(a,b)=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }b<0,\\
1-1/q&\text{ if }n|a,b\geqslant 1,\\
0&\text{ if }n\nmid a,b\geqslant 1,\\
-1/q&\text{ if }n|a,b=0,\\
G_{\psi}(a,0)\text{ with }|G_{\psi}(a,0)|=q^{-1/2}&\text{ if }n\nmid a,b=0.\end{cases}

One has
\overline{G_{\psi}(a,b)}=(-1,\varpi)_{n}^{a}\cdot G_{\psi}(-a,b)
. For every
k\in\mathbf{Z}
, we write

\mathbf{g}_{\psi}(k):=G_{\psi}(k,0).

Proposition 5.9
.

Suppose
\gamma=\mathbf{s}_{y_{1}}
is represented by
y_{1}
and
\gamma^{\prime}=\mathbf{s}_{y}
by
y
. Then we can write

\tau(w_{\alpha},\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})=\tau^{1}(w_{\alpha},\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})+\tau^{2}(w_{\alpha},\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})

with the following properties:

\bullet

\tau^{i}(w_{\alpha},\chi,\gamma\cdot\overline{z},\gamma^{\prime}\cdot\overline{z}^{\prime})=({}^{w_{\alpha}}\chi)^{-1}(\overline{z})\cdot\tau^{i}(w_{\alpha},\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\cdot\chi(\overline{z}^{\prime})\text{ for every }\overline{z},\overline{z}^{\prime}\in\overline{A}
,

\bullet

\tau^{1}(w_{\alpha},\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})=0
unless
y_{1}\equiv y\mod Y_{Q,n}
,

\bullet

\tau^{2}(w_{\alpha},\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})=0
unless
y_{1}\equiv w_{\alpha}(y)\mod Y_{Q,n}
.

Moreover,

\bullet

if
y_{1}=y
, then

\tau^{1}(w_{\alpha},\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})=(1-q^{-1})\frac{\chi(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{n_{\alpha}}))^{k_{y,\alpha}}}{1-\chi(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{n_{\alpha}}))},\text{ where }k_{y,\alpha}=\left\lceil\frac{1+{\left\langle y,\alpha\right\rangle}}{n_{\alpha}}\right\rceil;

\bullet

if
y_{1}=w_{\alpha}(y)
, then

\tau^{2}(w_{\alpha},\chi,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})=(-1,\varpi)_{n}^{{\left\langle y,\alpha\right\rangle}\cdot D(y,\alpha^{\vee})}\cdot\mathbf{g}_{\psi^{-1}}({\left\langle y,\alpha\right\rangle}Q(\alpha^{\vee})).

Proof.

The computation in
[
Mc2
]
(with refinement given in
[
Ga2
]
) applies here mutatis mutantis, by noting that
\psi
has conductor
\mathfrak{p}_{F}
instead of
O_{F}
as in the references mentioned. We omit the details of the computation.
∎

5.4.
Some simple examples

We illustrate on the previous discussion, especially on Conjectures
5.4
and Conjecture
5.7
by considering some simple examples. These include covers of
{\rm SL}_{2},{\rm SO}_{3}
and
{\rm GL}_{r}
. We focus only on unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified principal series of these groups.

5.4.1.
Covers of
{\rm SL}_{2}

Let
\overline{{\rm SL}}_{2}
be the
n
-fold cover of
{\rm SL}_{2}
associated with

Q(\alpha^{\vee})=-1.

Let
K={\rm SL}_{2}(O)
and

K^{\prime}=e\cdot K=e(\varpi)\cdot K\cdot e(\varpi)^{-1},

where
e:=\alpha^{\vee}/2\in Y_{ad}
is a generator for the cocharacter lattice for
{\rm PGL}_{2}
. Here
e\in\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
is the nontrivial element and

\mathcal{K}=\left\{K,K^{\prime}\right\}.

Consider the two unique splittings
s_{K}
and
s_{K^{\prime}}
of
K
and
K^{\prime}
respectively, one has

(5.12)

s_{K}^{\prime}=[e(\varpi),-]\otimes s_{K}

on
{\bf T}(O)
, where

(5.13)

[e(\varpi),\alpha^{\vee}(a^{k})]=(\varpi,a)_{n}^{-k}

for all
a\in F^{\times}
and
k\in\mathbf{Z}
, see
[
BD
, §4.13]
. There are two cases depending on the parity of
n
.

First, if
n
is odd, then it is easy to see

Y_{Q,n}=Y_{Q,n}^{sc}=\mathbf{Z}[n\alpha^{\vee}],\ X_{Q,n}=\mathbf{Z}[\alpha/2n],\ X_{Q,n}^{sc}=\mathbf{Z}[\alpha/n].

This immediately gives that the diagram in (
5.6
) becomes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[\alpha^{\vee}]/\mathbf{Z}[n\alpha^{\vee}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[\alpha^{\vee}]/\mathbf{Z}[n\alpha^{\vee}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[\alpha^{\vee}/2]/\mathbf{Z}[n\alpha^{\vee}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[\alpha^{\vee}/2]/\mathbf{Z}[\alpha^{\vee}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left\{0\right\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[n\alpha^{\vee}/2]/\mathbf{Z}[n\alpha^{\vee}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[n\alpha^{\vee}/2]/\mathbf{Z}[n\alpha^{\vee}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where
\varphi(x)=n\cdot x
for middle vertical map. The existence of the splitting
s_{\varphi}
is clear. As mentioned, we concentrate on the case where
\chi
is unramified and unitary.

If
n|k
, or equivalently
k\alpha^{\vee}\in Y_{Q,n}
and thus
k\alpha^{\vee}(a)\in Z(\overline{T})
, then

[e(\varpi),\alpha^{\vee}(a^{k})]=1.

This shows that
s_{K}
and
s_{K}^{\prime}
agree on
{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O)
, and thus are associated in the sense of Definition
5.3
. It follows that in this case
I(\chi)
is both
(K,s_{K})
and
(K^{\prime},s_{K^{\prime}})
-unramified.

If
I(\chi)
is unitary unramified and irreducible, then

\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})=\left\{I(\chi)\right\}.

It is easy to see that Conjecture
5.4
holds. On the other hand, if
I(\chi)
is a unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified and reducible, then
it occurs exactly when

\chi_{\alpha}:=\chi(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{n_{\alpha}}))=-1

and thus
R_{\chi}=W
. In particular,
{}^{w_{\alpha}}\chi=\chi
. Note that we have an isomorphism
R_{\chi}\simeq\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})
(see
[
Ga7
]
) and thus we always make such an identification. In this case,

\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})={\rm JH}(I(\chi))=\left\{\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1}),\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon_{W})\right\},

where
\varepsilon_{W}
is the nontrivial character of
\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})
.

Proposition 5.10
.

There is exactly one
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)
which is
(K,s_{K})
-unramified, while the other is
(K^{\prime},s_{K^{\prime}})
-unramified. In particular, this verified Conjecture
5.4
.

Proof.

The proof uses the same idea as in the linear case, that is, to compute the coefficient of the intertwining operator applied to the unramified vector. More precisely, recall that the normalized intertwining operator

\mathcal{A}(w_{\alpha},\chi)=\gamma(w_{\alpha},\chi)\cdot T(w_{\alpha},\chi;r_{w_{\alpha}}^{\rm un})

acts on
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)\subset I(\chi)
by
\rho(w_{\alpha})\cdot{\rm id}
for each
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi}))={\rm Irr}(W)
.

Note that as character of
\overline{A}
, we have

{}^{e(\varpi)}\tilde{\chi}=[-,e(\varpi)]\otimes\tilde{\chi}.

However, the problem is that
e(\varpi)\notin\overline{T}
. Setting

t:=\varpi^{(n+1)\alpha^{\vee}/2}\in\overline{T},

it is easy to see

{}^{t}\tilde{\chi}={}^{e(\varpi)}\tilde{\chi},

since
[-,t]=[-,e(\varpi)]
as a function on
T
. Let
\tilde{\chi}=\chi\boxtimes\tilde{\chi}_{O}
be the unique extension of
\chi
such that
\tilde{\chi}_{O}\circ s_{K}=\mathbbm{1}
on
{\bf T}(O)
. We see that

{}^{t}\tilde{\chi}\circ s_{K^{\prime}}|_{{\bf T}(O)}=\mathbbm{1}

and

(5.14)

s_{K^{\prime}}=[t,-]\otimes s_{K}

on
{\bf T}(O)
. Here
\tilde{\chi}
and
{}^{t}\tilde{\chi}
are both extensions of
\chi
and give rise to two models

{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(\tilde{\chi}),\ {\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}({}^{t}\tilde{\chi})

of the principal series
I(\chi)
. For any fixed lifting
\overline{t}
of
t
, we have the isomorphism

\Phi_{\overline{t}}:{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(\tilde{\chi})\longrightarrow{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}({}^{t}\tilde{\chi})

given by
\Phi_{\overline{t}}(f)(g)=f(\overline{t}^{-1}g)
, see (
5.8
).

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})
is
(K,s_{K})
-unramified and thus

\mathcal{A}(w_{\alpha},\chi)(f_{K})=f_{K},

where
f_{K}\in{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(\tilde{\chi})
is the normalized
(K,s_{K})
-unramified vector. We want to compute the scalar arising from
\mathcal{A}(w_{\alpha},\chi)
applied to the
(K^{\prime},s_{K^{\prime}})
-unramified vector. Note that in the model
{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}({}^{t}\tilde{\chi})
the
(K^{\prime},s_{K^{\prime}})
-unramified vector is given by

f_{K^{\prime}}(b\cdot s^{\prime}(k^{\prime}))=\begin{cases}\delta_{B}^{1/2}(t)\cdot\tilde{\chi}(a)&\text{ if }b=au\text{ with }a\in\overline{A},u\in U\text{ and }k^{\prime}\in K^{\prime},\\
0&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Thus,
\Phi_{t}^{-1}(f_{K^{\prime}})\in{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(\tilde{\chi})
is the
(K^{\prime},s_{K^{\prime}})
-unramified vector. We want to determine the scalar
c\in\mathbf{C}
such that

T(w_{\alpha},\chi;r_{w_{\alpha}}^{\rm un})(\Phi_{\overline{t}}^{-1}(f_{K^{\prime}}))=c\cdot\Phi_{\overline{t}}^{-1}(f_{K^{\prime}}).

We see that

\displaystyle c=

\displaystyle T(w_{\alpha},\chi;r_{w_{\alpha}}^{\rm un})(\Phi_{\overline{t}}^{-1}(f_{K^{\prime}}))(\overline{t}^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{U}\Phi_{\overline{t}}^{-1}(f_{K^{\prime}})(\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}u\overline{t}^{-1})du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{U}f_{K^{\prime}}(\overline{t}\cdot\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}u\cdot\overline{t}^{-1})du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{U}f_{K^{\prime}}(t\cdot\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}t^{-1}\overline{w}_{\alpha}\cdot\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}\cdot tut^{-1})du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{U}f_{K^{\prime}}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{n})\cdot\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi)\cdot\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}\cdot tut^{-1})du,

where the last equality follows from (
2.4
). We have

c=q\cdot\chi_{\alpha}\cdot\int_{U}f_{K^{\prime}}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi)\cdot\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}\cdot u)du.

Now to compute the integral, we split
U
into
\mathfrak{p}_{F}
and
U-\mathfrak{p}_{F}
.
If
u\in\mathfrak{p}_{F}
, it is easy to see that
\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi)\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}u\in s_{K^{\prime}}(K^{\prime})
and thus

\int_{\mathfrak{p}_{F}}f_{K^{\prime}}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi)\cdot\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}\cdot u)du=d(\mathfrak{p})=q^{-1}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\int_{U-\mathfrak{p}_{F}}f_{K^{\prime}}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi)\cdot\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}\cdot\overline{e}_{\alpha}(u))du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{U-\mathfrak{p}}f_{K^{\prime}}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi)\cdot\overline{h}_{\alpha}(u^{-1})\cdot\overline{e}_{\alpha}(-u)\cdot\overline{e}_{-\alpha}(-u^{-1}))du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leqslant 0}\int_{u\in O^{\times}}f_{K^{\prime}}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi)\cdot\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{-k}u^{-1})\cdot\overline{e}_{\alpha}(-u))d(\varpi^{k}u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leqslant 0}\left|\varpi^{k}\right|\int_{u\in O^{\times}}f_{K^{\prime}}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi)\cdot\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{-k})\overline{h}_{\alpha}(u^{-1})\cdot(u,\varpi)_{n}^{kQ(\alpha^{\vee})})d(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leqslant 0}\left|\varpi^{k}\right|\int_{u\in O^{\times}}f_{K^{\prime}}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi)\cdot\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{-k})s_{K}(h_{\alpha}(u^{-1}))\cdot(u,\varpi)_{n}^{kQ(\alpha^{\vee})})d(u).

Now by (
5.14
) we have

s_{K^{\prime}}(h_{\alpha}(u^{-1}))=s_{K}(h_{\alpha}(u^{-1}))\cdot(u,\varpi)_{n}^{Q(\alpha^{\vee})}.

This gives that the above integral over
U-\mathfrak{p}_{F}
equals

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leqslant 0}\left|\varpi^{k}\right|\int_{u\in O^{\times}}f_{K^{\prime}}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi)\cdot\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{-k})s_{K^{\prime}}(h_{\alpha}(u^{-1}))\cdot(u,\varpi)_{n}^{(k-1)Q(\alpha^{\vee})})d(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leqslant 0,n|(k-1)}\left|\varpi^{k}\right|\int_{u\in O^{\times}}f_{K^{\prime}}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{1-k}))d(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-1}\frac{\chi_{\alpha}(1-q^{-1})}{1-\chi_{\alpha}}.

Combining the above we get that

c=\chi_{\alpha}\cdot c_{\sf gk}(w_{\alpha},\chi)=(-1)\frac{1-q^{-1}\chi_{\alpha}}{1-\chi_{\alpha}}

and therefore in our setting

\mathcal{A}(w_{\alpha},\chi)(f_{K^{\prime}})=(-1)\cdot f_{K^{\prime}}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Second, we consider the case
n=2m
is even, which gives then

Y_{Q,n}=\mathbf{Z}[m\alpha^{\vee}],\ Y_{Q,n}^{sc}=\mathbf{Z}[n\alpha^{\vee}],\ X_{Q,n}=X_{Q,n}^{sc}=\mathbf{Z}[\alpha/n].

In this case, diagram (
5.6
) becomes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[\alpha^{\vee}]/\mathbf{Z}[m\alpha^{\vee}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[\alpha^{\vee}]/\mathbf{Z}[m\alpha^{\vee}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[\alpha^{\vee}/2]/\mathbf{Z}[m\alpha^{\vee}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[\alpha^{\vee}/2]/\mathbf{Z}[\alpha^{\vee}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left\{0\right\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[m\alpha^{\vee}]/\mathbf{Z}[m\alpha^{\vee}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left\{0\right\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

As
\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}=\left\{0\right\}
, every
\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})
is a singleton and if
\chi
is unitary, then

\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})=\left\{I(\chi)\right\}.

Note that in this case, if
I(\chi)
is
(K,s_{K})
-unramified, then it is not
(K^{\prime},s_{K^{\prime}})
-unramified. Indeed, for every
z=im\alpha^{\vee}\in Y_{Q,n}
and
u\in O^{\times}
, it follows from (
5.12
) and (
5.13
) that

s_{K^{\prime}}(z\otimes u)=(\varpi,u)_{n}^{-mi}\cdot s_{K}(z\otimes u),

where
(\varpi,u)_{n}^{mi}=(\varpi,u)_{2}^{i}
may not be trivial in general. Thus, the two splittings
s_{K}
and
s_{K^{\prime}}=y\cdot s_{K}
are not associated. This agrees with Conjecture
5.4
.

Regarding the Whittaker dimension with respect to varying Whittaker datum, we assume that for the conductor,

\mathfrak{f}(\psi)=O_{F}.

For simplicity of notation, we denote
{}^{e}\psi:={}^{e(\varpi)}\psi
where
e(\varpi)\in T_{ad}/T
such that

\mathfrak{f}({}^{e}\psi)=\mathfrak{p}_{F}.

Consider the
n
-fold cover
\overline{{\rm SL}}_{2}
such that
n
is odd and

I(\chi)=\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})\oplus\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon_{W}),

where the labelling is such that
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})
is the unique
(K,s_{K})
-unramified constituent, and thus
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon_{W})
is
(K^{\prime},s_{K^{\prime}})
-unramified by Proposition
5.10
.

Proposition 5.11
.

Keep notations and conventions as above, one has

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1}))=\dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{e}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon_{W}))=(n+1)/2

and

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon_{W}))=\dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{e}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1}))=(n-1)/2.

In particular, Conjecture
5.7
is verified in this case.

Proof.

We first note that for any non-degenerate character
\psi:U\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
, there is a representation (see
[
Ga7
, §5.4]
)

\sigma^{\rm Wh_{\psi}}:R_{\chi}\longrightarrow{\rm GL}({\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\chi)))

given by

\sigma^{\rm Wh_{\psi}}(w)=\mathcal{A}(w,i(\chi))^{*},

the induced map from
\mathcal{A}(w,i(\chi))=\gamma(w,\chi)\cdot T(w,\chi;r_{w}^{\rm un})
, where
\gamma(w,\chi)
is the gamma-factor associated with
w
and
i(\chi)
. It is proved in
[
Ga7
, Theorem 5.6]
that one has

(5.15)

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho))={\left\langle\rho,\sigma^{\rm Wh_{\psi}}\right\rangle}_{R_{\chi}}.

If
\mathfrak{f}(\psi)=O_{F}
and
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})
is
(K,s_{K})
-unramified, as is assumed here, then it is shown in
[
Ga7
, Theorem 7.1]
that

\sigma^{\rm Wh_{\psi}}=\frac{n+1}{2}\mathbbm{1}\bigoplus\frac{n-1}{2}\varepsilon_{W},

which immediately gives that

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1}))=\frac{n+1}{2}\text{ and }\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon_{W}))=\frac{n-1}{2}.

The proof relies on an explicit form of the scattering matrix for
\mathfrak{f}(\psi)=O
, as described in
[
Ga2
]
and
[
GSS2
]
.

Now to compute
\sigma^{{\rm Wh}_{{}^{e}\psi}}
, we use Proposition
5.9
instead, since
\mathfrak{f}({}^{e}\psi)=\mathfrak{p}_{F}
. We take the set

\mathfrak{R}=\left\{i\alpha^{\vee}:-(n-1)/2\leqslant i\leqslant(n-1)/2\right\}\subset Y

of representatives of
Y/Y_{Q,n}
. In view of Proposition
5.9
, by permuting the set
\mathfrak{R}
properly, the scattering matrix

[\tau(w_{\alpha},\chi,\mathbf{s}_{z},\mathbf{s}_{y})]_{z,y\in\mathfrak{R}}

is equal to a block-diagonal matrix with
(n+1)/2
blocks
\mathcal{M}_{i}:0\leqslant i\leqslant(n-1)/2
such that

–

for
i=0
we get the size-one matrix

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{R}}(w_{\alpha},\chi)_{0}=[\tau(w_{\alpha},\chi,\mathbf{s}_{0},\mathbf{s}_{0})]=\frac{\chi_{\alpha}-q^{-1}}{1-\chi_{\alpha}}=-\frac{1+q^{-1}}{2},

where the second equality follows from
\chi_{\alpha}=-1
and Proposition
5.9
.

–

for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant(n-1)/2
, we have the two by two matrix
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{R}}(w_{\alpha},\chi)_{i\alpha^{\vee}}=[\tau(w_{\alpha},\chi,\mathbf{s}_{z},\mathbf{s}_{y})]
with
z,y\in\left\{\pm i\alpha^{\vee}\right\}
, which by Proposition
5.9
is equal to

(5.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{R}}(w_{\alpha},\chi)_{i\alpha^{\vee}}=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{matrix}\chi_{\alpha}(1-q^{-1})/(1-\chi_{\alpha})&{\bf g}_{{}^{t}\psi^{-1}}(2i))\\
{\bf g}_{{}^{t}\psi^{-1}}(-2i)&(1-q^{-1})/(1-\chi_{\alpha})\end{matrix}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{matrix}-(1-q^{-1})/2&{\bf g}_{{}^{t}\psi^{-1}}(2i))\\
{\bf g}_{{}^{t}\psi^{-1}}(-2i)&(1-q^{-1})/2\end{matrix}\right).

Here the left-upper and right-lower entry is
\tau(w_{\alpha},\chi,\mathbf{s}_{i\alpha^{\vee}},\mathbf{s}_{i\alpha^{\vee}})
and
\tau(w_{\alpha},\chi,\mathbf{s}_{-i\alpha^{\vee}},\mathbf{s}_{-i\alpha^{\vee}})
respectively.

Note that in this case

\gamma(w_{\alpha},\chi)=\frac{1-\chi_{\alpha}}{1-q^{-1}\chi_{\alpha}}=\frac{2}{1+q^{-1}}.

This coupled with the above equalities for the
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{R}}(w_{\alpha},\chi)_{i\alpha^{\vee}}
’s gives that

\sigma^{{\rm Wh}_{{}^{t}\psi}}=\frac{n+1}{2}\varepsilon_{W}\bigoplus\frac{n-1}{2}\mathbbm{1},

and it thus follows from (
5.15
) that

\dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{e}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1}))=\frac{n-1}{2}\text{ and }\dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{e}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon_{W}))=\frac{n+1}{2},

as desired. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.12
.

Results in this subsection generalize to covers of
{\rm Sp}_{2r}
without too much difficulty. Indeed, for even-fold cover every unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified
I(\chi)
is irreducible. If
n
is odd, then
I(\chi)
could be reducible with
R_{\chi}=\left\{1,w_{\alpha_{r}}\right\}
, where
\alpha_{r}
is the unique long simple root. The same proof as Proposition
5.10
applies to
\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}
to give the same result. Write
I(\chi)=\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})\oplus\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon_{W})
, assuming
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})
is
(K,s_{K})
-unramified. Regarding the Whittaker dimension, one
has

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1}))=\dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{e}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon_{W}))=\frac{n^{r-1}(n+1)}{2}

and

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon_{W}))=\dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{e}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1}))=\frac{n^{r-1}(n-1)}{2}.

For these equalities, one can either apply Rodier’s heredity coupled with Proposition
5.11
, or argue as in the proof of Proposition
5.11
directly.

5.4.2.
Covers of
{\rm SO}_{3}

Let
Y=\mathbf{Z}\cdot e
