be the cocharacter lattice of
{\rm SO}_{3}
with
\alpha^{\vee}=2e
generating the co-root lattice
Y^{sc}
. Let

Q:Y\to\mathbf{Z}

be the Weyl-invariant quadratic form such that
Q(e)=1
. Thus,
Q(\alpha^{\vee})=4
. We get

\alpha_{Q,n}^{\vee}=\frac{n}{\text{gcd}(4,n)}\alpha^{\vee}\text{ and thus }Y_{Q,n}^{sc}=n_{(4)}\alpha^{\vee}.

On the other hand,

Y_{Q,n}=\mathbf{Z}\frac{n}{\text{gcd}(2,n)}e=\mathbf{Z}[n_{(2)}e].

Let
\chi
be a unitary unramified genuine character of
Z(\overline{T})
. The diagram (
5.6
) becomes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[e]/\mathbf{Z}[n_{(2)}e]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[e]/\mathbf{Z}[n_{(2)}e]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[e]/\mathbf{Z}[n_{(2)}e]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left\{0\right\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[n_{(4)}e]/\mathbf{Z}[n_{(2)}e]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Z}[n_{(4)}e]/\mathbf{Z}[n_{(2)}e]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left\{0\right\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where one has
\varphi(e)=n_{(4)}\cdot e
. It is shown in
[
Ga7
, §8.1]
that
\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})=W
if and only if
4|n
and
\underline{\chi}_{\alpha}
is a non-trivial quadratic character; otherwise
\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})=\left\{1\right\}
.

Note that
K={\rm PGL}_{2}(O)={\rm SO}_{3}(O)\in\mathcal{K}
is the unique conjugacy class of hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup.
If we fix a splitting
s_{K}:K\hookrightarrow\overline{G}
, then the other splitting
s_{K}\otimes f_{\xi}
is given by the twist

f_{\xi}:{\rm PGL}_{2}(O)\longrightarrow\mu_{n}

where

f_{\xi}=\xi\circ\det:{\rm GL}_{2}(O)\longrightarrow\mu_{n}

for a quadratic character

\xi:O^{\times}/O^{\times 2}\longrightarrow\mu_{n}.

In particular,

\bullet

if
n
is odd, then
{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})=\left\{1\right\}
as
\xi
is always trivial, and thus there is only one splitting
s_{K}
of
K
;

\bullet

if
n=2k
with
k
odd, then
{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})=\left\{\mathbbm{1},f_{\xi}\right\}
, and for the unique nontrivial
\xi
two splittings
s_{K}\otimes f_{\xi}
and
s_{K}
are not associated;

\bullet

if
4|n
, then we have
{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})=\left\{\mathbbm{1},f_{\xi}\right\}
for the unique nontrivial
\xi
, and in the case the two splittings
s_{K}\otimes f_{\xi}
and
s_{K}
are associated, i.e.,
f_{\xi}:{\bf T}(O)\to\mu_{n}
is trivial on
{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O)
.

From the perspective of §
5.2.1
, we see that

(Y/Y_{Q,n})^{\natural}\simeq\mathbf{Z}n_{(4)}e/\mathbf{Z}n_{(2)}e.

If we consider an
(K,s_{K})
-unramified
I(\chi)
, then there are two cases as follows.

\bullet

First, if
4\nmid n
, then every unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified
I(\chi)
is irreducible and one has

\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})=\left\{I(\chi)\right\}.

then
{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})^{\natural}=\left\{1\right\}
.
In particular, if
n=2k
with
k
odd, then
I(\chi)
is not
(K,s_{K}\otimes f_{\xi})
-unramified, where
{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})=\left\{\mathbbm{1},f_{\xi}\right\}
. In this case, Conjecture
5.4
is vacuously true since
{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})^{\natural}=\left\{1\right\}
.

\bullet

Second, if
4|n
, then
{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})^{\natural}={\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})=\left\{\mathbbm{1},f_{\xi}\right\}
, and the two splittings
s_{K}
and
s_{K}\otimes f_{\xi}
are associated.
Thus, if
I(\chi)
is irreducible, then it is both
(K,s_{K})
and
(K,s_{K}\otimes f_{\xi})
-unramified; in this case, Conjecture
5.4
holds as well.

In fact, for
4|n
the nontrivial element in
{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})^{\natural}
is

f_{\xi}=f_{ne/4}:{\bf T}(O)\longrightarrow\mu_{n},

explicitly given by

f_{ne/4}(y\otimes u)=[\varpi^{ne/4},y\otimes u]=(\varpi,u)_{n}^{nB_{Q}(e,y)/4}.

Proposition 5.13
.

Keep notations as above. For a unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified principal series
I(\chi)
, if

\mathcal{L}(\phi_{\chi})={\rm JH}(I(\chi))=\left\{\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1}),\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon)\right\},

which occurs exactly for
4|n
and that
\chi_{\alpha}
is the non-trivial quadratic character, then
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)
is
(K,s_{K})
-unramified for exactly one
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi}))
, and the other is
(K,s_{K}\otimes f_{\xi})
-unramified. In particular, Conjecture
5.4
holds in this case.

Proof.

For simplicity, in the proof we write
s_{K}^{\prime}:=s_{K}\otimes f_{\xi}
. We first remark that the setting is different from Proposition
5.10
, since here we only have one conjugacy class
K
of hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup, but equipped with two splittings. However, the idea of proof in Proposition
5.10
applies. That is, we will consider the eigenvalue of the normalized intertwining operator
\mathcal{A}(w_{\alpha},\chi)
applied to the normalized unramified vectors
f_{s_{K}}
and
f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}
.

We take the model
{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}}^{\overline{T}}(\tilde{\chi})
for
i(\chi)
, where

\tilde{\chi}:\overline{A}\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

is trivial on
s_{K}({\bf T}(O))
. In this case
{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(\tilde{\chi})
is
(K,s_{K})
-unramified and the normalized
(K,s_{K})
-unramified vector
f_{s_{K}}\in{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(\tilde{\chi})
is given by

(5.17)

f_{s_{K}}(b\cdot s_{K}(k))=\begin{cases}\delta_{B}^{1/2}(t)\cdot\tilde{\chi}(a)&\text{ if }b=tu\text{ with }t\in\overline{A},u\in U\text{ and }k\in K,\\
0&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

One has

\mathcal{A}(w_{\alpha},\chi)(f_{s_{K}})=f_{s_{K}}.

To compute
\mathcal{A}(w_{\alpha},\chi)(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}})
, we first note that in the model
{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(\tilde{\chi})
above, the normalized
(K,s_{K}^{\prime})
-unramified vector
f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}
is not given by the formula (
5.17
) above.

Recall that the character

s_{K}^{\prime}/s_{K}:{\bf T}(O)/{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O)\longrightarrow\mu_{n}

is given by

(s_{K}^{\prime}/s_{K})(k)=\xi\circ\det(k)=[e(\varpi^{n/4}),\det(k)]

for every
k\in{\bf T}(O)
. Fix a lifting

t:=\overline{e(\varpi^{n_{\alpha}})}\in\overline{T}

of
e(\varpi^{n_{\alpha}})\in T
, where
n_{\alpha}=n/4
. We see that the model
{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(^{t}\tilde{\chi})
contains the normalized
(K,s_{K}^{\prime})
-unramified vector
f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}
given by

f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}(b\cdot s_{K}^{\prime}(k))=\begin{cases}\delta_{B}^{1/2}(a)\cdot(^{t}\tilde{\chi})(a)&\text{ if }b=au\text{ with }a\in\overline{A},u\in U\text{ and }k\in K,\\
0&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

In view of the
\overline{G}
-isomorphism (see (
5.8
))

\Phi_{t}:{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(\tilde{\chi})\longrightarrow{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}({}^{t}\tilde{\chi})

we see that
\Phi_{t}^{-1}(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}})
is the
(K,s_{K}^{\prime})
-unramified vector in the model
{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(\tilde{\chi})
. We want to compute the constant
c\in\mathbf{C}
such that

T(w_{\alpha},\chi;r_{w_{\alpha}}^{\rm un})\big(\Phi_{t}^{-1}(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}})\big)=c\cdot\Phi_{t}^{-1}(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}).

By noting that
\Phi_{t}^{-1}(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}})(t^{-1})=f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}(1)=1
, one obtains

\displaystyle c=T(w_{\alpha},\chi;r_{w_{\alpha}}^{\rm un})(\Phi_{t}^{-1}(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}))(t^{-1})=

\displaystyle\int_{U}(\Phi_{t}^{-1}(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}))(\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}ut^{-1})du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{U}f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}(t\cdot(w_{\alpha}^{-1}t^{-1}w_{\alpha})\cdot\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}tut^{-1})du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{U}f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}(\overline{h}_{\alpha}(\varpi^{n_{\alpha}})\cdot\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}tut^{-1})du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\chi_{\alpha}\cdot\int_{U}f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}(\overline{w}_{\alpha}^{-1}u)du.

Now similar (and in fact simpler) argument as in Proposition
5.10
gives that the last integral is equal to
c_{\sf gk}(w_{\alpha},\chi)
and thus

c=\chi_{\alpha}\cdot c_{\sf gk}(w_{\alpha},\chi)=(-1)\gamma(w_{\alpha},\chi)^{-1}=(-1)\frac{1+q^{-1}}{2}.

This shows that
\mathcal{A}(w_{\alpha},\chi)(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}})=-f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}
as desired and the proof is completed.
∎

Consider the Whittaker dimension, note that:

–

if
4\nmid n
, then the hypothesis of Conjecture
5.7
is satisified, or equivalently the map
\varphi
is trivial. However, in this case,
I(\chi)
is always irreducible, and thus Conjecture
5.7
holds trivially.

–

if
4|n
, then the cover
\overline{{\rm SO}}_{3}
does not fit into the hypothesis of Conjecture
5.7
. On the other hand, since
T_{ad}/T=\left\{1\right\}
, we see that a priori one expects

(5.18)

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho))=\dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{t}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho))

for every
\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\chi_{\chi}))
and
t\in T
.

For
4|n
we verify below the equality (
5.18
) for reducible
I(\chi)
, for which
\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})=W
. We assume
\mathfrak{\psi}=O_{F}
and
t=e(\varpi)\in T
.

First, writing
n=2k
with
k
even, we have the ordered set

\mathfrak{R}=\left\{ie:-k/2+1\leqslant i\leqslant k/2\right\}

of representatives of
Y/Y_{Q,n}
. To apply Proposition
5.9
, we consider the action
w(-)
on
Y/Y_{Q,n}
. There are two trivial orbits
\left\{0\right\}
and
\left\{ke/2\right\}
in
Y/Y_{Q,n}
. The remaining elements in
Y/Y_{Q,n}
are formed by free
W
-orbits with respect to the action
w(-)
. Accordingly, up to a permutation of elements in
\mathfrak{R}
, we see that the scattering matrix

[\tau(w_{\alpha},\chi,\mathbf{s}_{z},\mathbf{s}_{y})]_{z,y\in\mathfrak{R}}

is a block-diagonal matrix with each block
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{R}}(w_{\alpha},\chi)_{ie}
described as follows.

–

There is a size-one block

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{R}}(w_{\alpha},\chi)_{0}=\tau(w_{\alpha},\chi,\mathbf{s}_{0},\mathbf{s}_{0})=\frac{\chi_{\alpha}-q^{-1}}{1-\chi_{\alpha}}=-\frac{1+q^{-1}}{2}

associated with the trivial
W
-orbit
\left\{0\right\}
; similarly, associated to
\left\{ke/2\right\}
one has another size-one block

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{R}}(w_{\alpha},\chi)_{ke/2}=\tau(w_{\alpha},\chi,\mathbf{s}_{ke/2},\mathbf{s}_{ke/2})=\frac{(1-q^{-1})\chi_{\alpha}^{2}}{1-\chi_{\alpha}}-q^{-1}\chi_{\alpha}=\frac{1+q^{-1}}{2}.

–

For every
1\leqslant i\leqslant k/2-1
, one has a size-two block

(5.19)

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{R}}(w_{\alpha},\chi)_{ie}=\left(\begin{matrix}-(1-q^{-1})/2&{\bf g}_{{}^{t}\psi^{-1}}(i))\\
{\bf g}_{{}^{t}\psi^{-1}}(-i)&(1-q^{-1})/2\end{matrix}\right),

where the left-upper entry and right-lower entry are equal to
\tau(w_{\alpha},\chi,\mathbf{s}_{ie},\mathbf{s}_{ie})
and
\tau(w_{\alpha},\chi,\mathbf{s}_{-ie},\mathbf{s}_{-ie})
respectively.

From the above, we immediately get (writing
{}^{e}\psi
for
{}^{e(\varpi)}\psi
)

\sigma^{{\rm Wh}_{{}^{e}\psi}}=(k/2)\cdot\mathbbm{1}\oplus(k/2)\cdot\varepsilon_{W}=\sigma^{{\rm Wh}_{\psi}},

where the second equality follows from
[
Ga7
, Proposition 8.3]
. Hence,

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho))=\dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{e}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho))=n/4

for any
\rho\in\left\{\mathbbm{1},\varepsilon_{W}\right\}={\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})
, as expected.

5.4.3.
Covers of
{\rm GL}_{r}

It seems to be a folkloric result that a Zelevinsky-type classification in terms of segements of reducibility points of parabolic induction from supercuspidal representations holds for covers of
{\rm GL}_{r}
. However, the classical proof of Zelevinsky relies crucially on the fact that the Whittaker model of the linear
{\rm GL}_{2}
is unique, and thus the approach is not directly adaptable in the covering settings. If one restricts to the representations with unique Whittaker model, then one can obtain similar results by using the same method as in
[
BZ1
,
BZ2
,
Zel
]
.

On the other hand, it is expected that every unitary unramified genuine principal series of
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r}
is irreducible. Recall that every Brylinski–Deligne cover is associated with
B_{Q}
such that

B(e_{i},e_{i})=2\mathbf{p}\text{ and }B(e_{i},e_{j})=\mathbf{q}\text{ for }i\neq j.

One has
Q(\alpha_{i})=2\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}
.

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r}
be an arbitrary Brylinski–Deligne cover of
{\rm GL}_{r}
such that
n_{\alpha}\cdot Y\subset Y_{Q,n}
. Then one has

\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}=\left\{1\right\}.

Hence, every unitary unramified genuine principal series of such cover
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r}
is irreducible.

Proof.

In view of the embedding
\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})\hookrightarrow\Gamma_{G,{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}
for every unitary unramified
\chi
, it suffices to prove the triviality of the latter group.
Recall that

\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}=(X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc})/X_{Q,n}^{sc}.

Assume

x\in\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}c_{i}\cdot\alpha_{i}\in X_{Q,n}\cap X_{G,\mathbf{Q}}^{sc}

with
c_{i}\in\mathbf{Q}
and
\alpha_{i}=e_{i}^{*}-e_{i+1}^{*}
for each
i
. Since
n_{\alpha}Y\subset Y_{Q,n}
by assumption, we have
n_{\alpha}e_{k}\in Y_{Q,n}
for every
1\leqslant k\leqslant r-1
. Then we have
{\left\langle x,n_{\alpha}e_{k}\right\rangle}\in\mathbf{Z}
, that is,

n_{\alpha}c_{1}\in\mathbf{Z}\text{ and }n_{\alpha}(c_{i+1}-c_{i})\in\mathbf{Z}

for every
1\leqslant i\leqslant r-1
. This gives that
c_{i}\in\mathbf{Z}/n_{\alpha}
and thus
x\in X_{Q,n}^{sc}
.
∎

It is clear that Proposition
5.14
applies to the cases when
n_{\alpha}=n
or
\mathbf{q}=0
, in particular to the Kazhdan–Patterson covers and Savin covers discussed in §
2.4.1
.

Remark 5.15
.

It is expected that the last assertion in Proposition
5.14
holds for arbitrary Brylinski–Deligne cover of
{\rm GL}_{r}
, without the assumption
n_{\alpha}Y\subset Y_{Q,n}
, i.e., we expect the equality
\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi})=\left\{1\right\}
for every unitary unramified
\chi
. However, the group
\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}
may not be trivial in general. For example, consider the Brylinski–Deligne cover
\overline{{\rm GL}}_{2}^{(4)}
associated with

\mathbf{p}=1,\ \mathbf{q}=2\text{ and }n=4.

Then it is easy to check that
n_{\alpha}=1
and
\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}=(\mathbf{Z}\alpha/2)/\mathbf{Z}\alpha\simeq\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}
in this case.

6.
Analysis for covers of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}

In this section, we analyze the unitary unramified principal series
I(\chi)
of
\overline{G}={\rm GSp}_{2r}
of type I (see §
2.4.2
) for the following three aspects:

(i)

the determination of the irreducible components
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho),\rho\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi}))
of an
(K,s_{K})
-unramified
I(\chi)
, determine the pair
(K^{\prime},s_{K^{\prime}})
with respect to which
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)
is unramified,

(ii)

determining the
\psi
-Whittaker dimension of each constituent
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)
,

(iii)

investigation of the restriction of each
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho)
to
\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}
and the pair
(K_{0},s_{K_{0}})
with respect to which a constituent is unramified, and also how the Whittaker dimension of each constituent varies with respect to different
\psi
-Whittaker datum.

Recall that the
n
-fold cover
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
of type I is associated with the quadratic form
Q:Y\to\mathbf{Z}
such that

Q(\alpha_{r}^{\vee})=-1\text{ and }Q(e_{0})=0,

where we follow the notations in §
2.4.2
closely.

We fix the hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup
K={\rm GSp}_{2r}(O)
and a splitting
s_{K}:K\hookrightarrow\overline{G}
. Then for every

f:K\longrightarrow\mu_{n},

one obtains a splitting
f\otimes s_{K}
.
The homomorphism
f
factors through the similitude map

{\rm sim}:{\rm GSp}_{2r}\longrightarrow F^{\times}

and thus corresponds to a homomorphism

\xi:O^{\times}/O^{\times n}\longrightarrow\mu_{n}

such that
f=\xi\circ{\rm sim}
.
We are interested in the subgroup
{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})^{\natural}\subset{\rm Hom}(K,\mu_{n})
, that is, those
f_{z}
with
z\in(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}
. For every
z\in(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}
, as a function on
{\bf T}(O)\subset K
, one has

f_{z}(y\otimes u)=[y\otimes u,z(\varpi)],

and in particular

f_{z}(e_{0}(u))=[e_{0}(u),z(\varpi)]=(u,\varpi)_{n}^{B_{Q}(e_{0},z)}

and

(6.1)

1=f_{z}(\alpha_{i}^{\vee}(u))=[\alpha_{i}^{\vee}(u),t]=(u,\varpi)_{n}^{B_{Q}(\alpha_{i}^{\vee},z)}

for every
u\in O^{\times}
and
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
. The equalities in (
6.1
) are equivalent to

B_{Q}(\alpha_{i}^{\vee},z)\in n\mathbf{Z}.

for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
, or equivalently,

B_{Q}(e_{i},z)\in n\mathbf{Z}

for every
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
. We have in this case
(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}=Y_{K}^{\natural}/Y_{Q,n}
, where

Y_{K}^{\natural}:=\left\{z\in Y:B_{Q}(z,e_{i})\in n\mathbf{Z}\text{ for all }i\right\}

and
Y_{Q,n}
is given in (
3.12
). Setting
z=\sum_{i=1}^{r}y_{i}e_{i}
, it is easy to see that

(6.2)

Y_{K}^{\natural}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\sum_{i=0}^{r}y_{i}e_{i}\in Y:\\
\bullet\quad n|(-2y_{i}+y_{0})\text{ for every }i\end{array}\right\}.

Let
I(\chi)
be a
(K,s_{K})
-unramified principal series. It is also
(K,f_{z}\otimes s_{K})
-unramified for every
z\in(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}
. On the other hand, for any
y\in Y
, consider

K_{y}:=y(\varpi)\cdot K\cdot y(\varpi)^{-1}.

The representation
I(\chi)
is unramified with respect to

(K_{y},y\cdot s_{K})

as well. Indeed, since
[y(\varpi),{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O)]=1
, we see that the splittings
y\cdot s_{K}:K_{y}\hookrightarrow\overline{G}
and
s_{K}:K\hookrightarrow\overline{G}
agree on
{\bf T}_{Q,n}(O)
. We also have

(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}\simeq(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K_{y}}^{\natural}

for every
y\in Y
. Combining the above, we see
I(\chi)
is
(K_{y},f_{z}\otimes y\cdot s_{K})
-unramified for every
z\in(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}
and
y\in Y
.

We set

K_{0}={\rm Sp}_{2r}\cap K={\rm Sp}_{2r}(O),

and for any
y\in Y
denote

K_{y,0}=K_{z}\cap{\rm Sp}_{2r}=y(\varpi)\cdot K_{0}\cdot y(\varpi)^{-1}.

In particular, we write

K^{\prime}=K_{e_{0}}\text{ and thus }K_{0}^{\prime}=K_{e_{0},0}.

It follows that

\mathcal{K}_{0}=\left\{K_{0},K_{0}^{\prime}\right\}

is the set of conjugacy classes of hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup of
{\rm Sp}_{2r}
.

For the questions in (i)–(iii), we will give a seperate discussion according to the parity of
n
.

6.1.
Odd fold cover of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}

If
n
is odd, then it is given in §
3.4.1
that
Y_{Q,n}
has a
\mathbf{Z}
-basis

\left\{ne_{i}:1\leqslant i\leqslant r\right\}\cup\left\{n_{(r)}\cdot e_{c}\right\}

and also that

(6.3)

\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{\vee}=\left\{(g,a)\in{\rm GSpin}_{2r+1}\times{\rm GL}_{1}:\lambda(g)=a^{\gcd(n,r)}\right\}.

One has

\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}\right|=2n^{r}\cdot n_{(r)}.

Since
n
is odd, we have
Y_{0,Q,n}=Y_{Q,n}^{sc}
, which is then equal to
Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}
. In any case, we have for odd
n
the identity

\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}=\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}.

Moreover, it is easy to see that

\left\{e_{i}:1\leqslant i\leqslant r\right\}\cup\left\{n_{(r)}e_{c}\right\}

constitutes a basis for
Y_{0}+Y_{Q,n}
, and therefore
\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|=2\cdot n_{(r)}
. One has in this case

(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}=\left\{i\cdot e_{c}:\ 0\leqslant i\leqslant n_{(r)}-1\right\}.

The diagram (
5.4
) becomes

(6.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\Gamma}}

One can check that the image of
(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}
in
\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}
via
p_{\Gamma}
is of index 2.

Every unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified genuine principal series
I(\chi)
is irreducible, in view of (
6.3
). As remarked, for every
y\in Y
and
z\in(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}
, the representation
I(\chi)
is also unramified with respect to

(K_{y},f_{z}\otimes y(\varpi)\cdot s_{K}).

The pair
(\overline{G},\overline{G}_{0})
is an isotypic pair, and one has the natural map
f_{G,G_{0}}
between the
L
-groups arising from the composites as in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{G}^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm GSpin}_{2r+1}\times{\rm GL}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{G}_{0}^{\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm SO}_{2r+1}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{G,G_{0}}}

By the choice of a distinguished genuine character
\chi_{\psi}:Z(\overline{T})\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
, which by restriction gives a distinguished genuine character
\chi_{\psi}^{0}:Z(\overline{T})\to\mathbf{C}^{\times}
, one can extend
f_{G,G_{0}}
to be a homomorphism of
L
-groups

f_{G,G_{0}}:{}^{L}\overline{G}\longrightarrow{}^{L}\overline{G}_{0}.

We have

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot I(\omega),

where
\omega=\chi|_{Z(\overline{T}_{0})}
. For any non-degenerate
\psi
, one has

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\chi))=2\cdot n^{r}\cdot n_{(r)}\text{ and }\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\omega))=n^{r}.

Note that
I(\omega)
might be reducible. Indeed, if
\chi_{\alpha_{r}}=-1
, then

I(\omega)=\pi(\phi_{\omega},\mathbbm{1})\oplus\pi(\phi_{\omega},\varepsilon)

where
\varepsilon
is the nontrivial character of
\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\omega})=\left\{1,w_{\alpha_{r}}\right\}
.
Let
\mathcal{K}_{0}=\left\{K_{0},K_{0}^{\prime}\right\}
be the above set of conjugacy classes of hyperspecial maximal compact subgroups of
{\rm Sp}_{2r}
, as above. Let

s_{0}:K_{0}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}\text{ and }s_{0}^{\prime}:K_{0}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}

be the two unique splittings of
K_{0}
and
K_{0}^{\prime}
respectively.

Theorem 6.1
.

For
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{(n)}
with odd
n
and a unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified
I(\chi)
, one has

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|\cdot I(\omega)

where
\omega=\chi|_{Z(\overline{T}_{0})}
. If
I(\omega)=\pi(\phi_{\omega},\mathbbm{1})\oplus\pi(\phi_{\omega},\varepsilon)
is reducible, and we assume that
\pi(\phi_{\omega},\mathbbm{1})
is
(K_{0},s_{0})
-unramified, then
\pi(\phi_{\omega},\varepsilon)
is
(K_{0}^{\prime},s_{0}^{\prime})
-unramified. Regarding the Whittaker dimension, for an additive character
\psi
with
\mathfrak{f}(\psi)=O_{F}
, one has

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\omega},\mathbbm{1}))=\frac{n^{r}+n^{r-1}}{2},\ \dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\omega},\varepsilon))=\frac{n^{r}-n^{r-1}}{2}

and also

\dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{t}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\omega},\mathbbm{1}))=\frac{n^{r}-n^{r-1}}{2},\ \dim{\rm Wh}_{{}^{t}\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\omega},\varepsilon))=\frac{n^{r}+n^{r-1}}{2},

where
t:=e_{0}(\varpi)
.

Proof.

The result here is essentially the content of Remark
5.12
. Indeed, the assertion that
\pi(\phi_{\omega},\varepsilon)
is
(K_{0}^{\prime},s_{0}^{\prime})
-unramified follows from the same argument as in Proposition
5.10
. The equalities regarding the
\psi
-Whittaker dimensions follow from
[
Ga7
]
, while those for the
{}^{t}\psi
-Whittaker dimension follows from the same argument in Proposition
5.11
, by noting
\mathfrak{f}({}^{e_{0}}\psi)=\mathfrak{p}_{F}
.

Alternatively, to prove the equalities on Whittaker dimensions, as noted in Remark
5.12
, one may reduce (essentially) to the
\overline{{\rm SL}}_{2}^{(n)}
by parabolic induction. Indeed, let
\overline{M}\subset\overline{P}\subset\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}
be the maximal Levi subgroup associated with
\Delta-\left\{\alpha_{r-1}\right\}
. Then one has

\overline{M}\simeq\overline{{\rm GL}}_{r-1}\times_{\mu_{n}}\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2}

and also

\omega=\chi_{1}\boxtimes\mu.

If
\chi_{\alpha_{r}}=\omega_{\alpha_{r}}=-1
, equivalently
\mu_{\alpha_{r}}=-1
, then one has a decomposition of the genuine principal series

I(\mu)=\pi_{0}(\phi_{\mu},\mathbbm{1})\oplus\pi_{0}(\phi_{\mu},\varepsilon)

of
{\rm Sp}_{2}
, such that

\pi(\phi_{\omega},\rho)={\rm Ind}_{\overline{P}}^{\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}}I(\chi_{1})\boxtimes\pi_{0}(\phi_{\mu},\rho)

for
\rho\in\left\{\mathbbm{1},\varepsilon\right\}={\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi}))
.
Since
\dim_{\psi}(I(\chi_{1}))=n^{r}=\dim_{{}^{t}\psi}(I(\chi_{1}))
, we see that the desired equalities follow directly from Rodier’s heredity and the results in Proposition
5.11
. This also concludes the proof.
∎

Regarding Conjecture
5.4
and
5.7
, we see that the three groups

{\rm Hom}(K,\mu)^{\natural},\ \widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}\text{ and }\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}

are all trivial. Also, every unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified
I(\chi)
is irreducible. Hence, we see that Conjecture
5.4
and Conjecture
5.7
hold trivially.

6.2.
Even fold cover of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}

In this subsection, we assume
n=2m
is even. We fix a unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified
I(\chi)
of
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
, it follows from Theorem
2.24
that

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}\right|\cdot\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}\bigoplus_{\omega_{\gamma,j}\in\mathscr{E}(\chi,{}^{\gamma}\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))}I(\omega_{\gamma,j}),

where

\left|\mathscr{E}(\chi,{}^{\gamma}\tilde{\chi}_{O};Z(\overline{T}_{0}))\right|=\left|Y_{0,Q,n}/Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}\right|=2.

We also have

\left|\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}\right|=m^{r}.

There are two situations according to the parity of
r
.

6.2.1.
When
r
is odd

In this case, as discussed in §
3.4.2
, the set

\left\{v_{0}:=n_{(r)}\cdot e_{c}\right\}\cup\left\{me_{i}+v_{0}/2:1\leqslant i\leqslant r\right\}

constitutes a basis for
Y_{Q,n}
and

\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{\vee}={\rm GSp}_{2r}.

Thus, every unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified
I(\chi)
is irreducible. Moreover,

\left|Y/Y_{Q,n}\right|=2\cdot m^{r}\cdot n_{(r)}.

It is also easy to see that
Y_{0}+Y_{Q,n}\subset Y
is the
\mathbf{Z}
-span of

\left\{e_{i}:1\leqslant i\leqslant r\right\}\cup\left\{v_{0}/2\right\}

and thus

\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}=Y/(Y_{0}+Y_{Q,n})=\left\{ie_{0}:\ 0\leqslant i\leqslant n_{(r)}-1\right\},

which has size
n_{(r)}
. It is straightforward to compute from its definition that

\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}=\left\{ie_{0}:\ 2|i\text{ and }0\leqslant i\leqslant n_{(r)}-1\right\}

and thus

\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}=\left\{0,e_{0}\right\}.

We have

(6.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0,Q,n}/Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0}/Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0}/Y_{0,Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{r-1}\cdot n_{(r)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n_{(r)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2m^{r}}

where the numbers around an arrow indicate the ratio of the orders of the two groups as the domain and codomain of the pertaining map; for example
2m^{r}=\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}\right|/\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|
. Since
\mathbf{Z}e_{c}/Y_{Q,n}\subset(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}
, we see that
p_{\Gamma}
is surjective.

From the above, we obtain more explicitly

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\frac{n_{(r)}}{2}\cdot(I(\omega_{0,0})\oplus I(\omega_{0,1}))\bigoplus\frac{n_{(r)}}{2}\cdot(I(\omega_{e_{0},0})\oplus I(\omega_{e_{0},1})).

Note that
I(\omega_{0,i}),i=0,1
are both
(K_{0},s_{0})
-unramified.
We show that
I(\omega_{e_{0},i}),i=0,1
are both
(K_{0}^{\prime},s_{0}^{\prime})
-unramified. Indeed, we have

i(\omega_{e_{0},i})={\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}_{0}}^{\overline{T}_{0}}(\omega_{e_{0},i}\boxtimes{}^{e_{0}(\varpi)}\tilde{\chi}_{O})\in{\rm Irr}(\overline{T}),

where

{}^{e_{0}(\varpi)}\tilde{\chi}_{O}:{\bf T}_{0}(O)\longrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}

is the twisted character. Since the character
\tilde{\chi}_{O}
restricted to
s_{0}({\bf T}_{0}(O))
is trivial and by definition

s_{0}^{\prime}(k^{\prime})=e_{0}(\varpi)\cdot s_{0}\big(e_{0}(\varpi)^{-1}\cdot k^{\prime}\cdot e_{0}(\varpi)\big)\cdot e_{0}(\varpi)^{-1}

for every
k^{\prime}\in K_{0}^{\prime}
, it gives that

{}^{e_{0}(\varpi)}\tilde{\chi}_{O}(s_{0}^{\prime}(k))=\tilde{\chi}_{O}\circ s_{0}(k)=1

for every
k\in{\bf T}_{0}(O)
. Thus, by the Satake isomorphism, every
I(\omega_{e_{0},i})
for
j=0,1
is
(K_{0}^{\prime},s_{0}^{\prime})
-unramified.

Since the dual group
\overline{{\rm Sp}}_{2r}^{\vee}={\rm Sp}_{2r}
, we have
\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\omega_{\gamma,j}})=\left\{1\right\}
and thus every
I(\omega_{\gamma,i})
is irreducible. We give a summary of the above discussion.

Theorem 6.2
.

Assume
n=2m
and
r
is odd. Then every
(K,s_{K})
-unramified unitary principal series
I(\chi)
of
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}
is irreducible, and one has

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=\frac{n_{(r)}}{2}\cdot(I(\omega_{0,0})\oplus I(\omega_{0,1}))\bigoplus\frac{n_{(r)}}{2}\cdot(I(\omega_{e_{0},0})\oplus I(\omega_{e_{0},1}))

where
I(\omega_{0,j}),j=0,1
is
(K_{0},s_{0})
-unramified and
I(\omega_{e_{0},j}),j=0,1
is
(K_{0}^{\prime},s_{0}^{\prime})
-unramified. Moreover, for every
\gamma\in\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}/\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}
and
j\in\left\{0,1\right\}
one has

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\omega_{\gamma,j}))=m^{r}

for any non-degenerate character
\psi
.

Similar to the odd-fold cover of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
, we see that if
n
is even with
r
odd, the two groups
\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}
and
\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}
are trivial. This shows that Conjecture
5.4
and Conjecture
5.7
hold trivially as well.

6.2.2.
When
r
is even

In this case, we have
Y_{Q,n}=Y_{Q,n}^{sc}\oplus\mathbf{Z}v_{0}
with
v_{0}=n_{(r)}\cdot e_{c}
as above. Thus,

\left\{m(e_{i}-e_{i+1}):1\leqslant i\leqslant r-1\right\}\cup\left\{ne_{r}\right\}\cup\left\{v_{0}\right\}

constitutes a basis for
Y_{Q,n}
, and also

\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{\vee}={\rm PGSp}_{2r}\times{\rm GL}_{1},

see §
3.4.2
. One has

\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}\right|=4m^{r}\cdot n_{(r)}.

It is easy to see that

Y_{0}+Y_{Q,n}
is the
\mathbf{Z}
-span of

\left\{e_{i}:1\leqslant i\leqslant r\right\}\cup\left\{v_{0}=n_{(r)}\cdot e_{c}\right\}.

Thus

\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}=\left\{ie_{c}+ce_{0}:\ 0\leqslant i\leqslant n_{(r)}-1\text{ and }0\leqslant c\leqslant 1\right\}

with
\left|\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\right|=2\cdot n_{(r)}
. In view of the map

\mathfrak{c}:\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}({\bf T}_{0}(O)\cap Z(\overline{T}_{0}),\mu_{n}),

we see that

\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}:={\rm Ker}(\mathfrak{c})=\left\{ie_{c}:0\leqslant i\leqslant n_{(r)}-1\right\}\subset\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}.

We have

(6.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0,Q,n}/Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0}/Y_{0}\cap Y_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{X}_{0,Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0}/Y_{0,Q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{r-1}\cdot n_{(r)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2n_{(r)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2m^{r}}

where the map
p_{\Gamma}
is surjective. In particular,

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=n_{(r)}\cdot(I(\omega_{0,0})\oplus I(\omega_{0,1}))\bigoplus n_{(r)}\cdot(I(\omega_{e_{0},0})\oplus I(\omega_{e_{0},1})).

Note that
I(\chi)
itself might be reducible, exactly when
\chi
satisfies that

\chi_{\alpha_{i}}=-1\text{ for all }i=2k-1,1\leqslant k\leqslant r/2.

In this case
\mathcal{S}(\chi_{\chi})=\left\{1,w\right\}
with
w=w_{\alpha_{1}}w_{\alpha_{3}}...w_{\alpha_{r-1}}
and we write

I(\chi)=\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})\oplus\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon),

where
\varepsilon\in{\rm Irr}(\mathcal{S}(\phi_{\chi}))
is the nontrivial character.

Proposition 6.3
.

Assume
I(\chi)
is unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified and reducible, and assume
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})\subset I(\chi)
is the unique
(K,s_{K})
-unramified constituent. Then
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon)
is the unique
(K,f_{me_{r}}\otimes s_{K})
-unramified constituent.

Proof.

First note that
me_{r}\in(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}
, in view of (
6.2
). The idea of the proof is the same as Proposition
5.13
, though here we are dealing with an intertwining operator associated with

w=w_{\alpha_{1}}w_{\alpha_{3}}...w_{\alpha_{r-1}}.

Let
\tilde{\chi}
be a genuine character of
\overline{A}
extending
\chi
and trivial on
s_{K}|_{{\bf T}(O)}
. For the proof, we set

t=me_{r}(\varpi)\text{ and }s_{K}^{\prime}=f_{me_{r}}\otimes s_{K}.

Then
{}^{t}\tilde{\chi}
also extends
\chi
and is trivial on
s_{K}^{\prime}
restricted to
{\bf T}(O)
.

Let
\overline{t}
be any lifting of
t
. One has the isomorphism

\Phi_{\overline{t}}:{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(\tilde{\chi})\longrightarrow{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}({}^{t}\tilde{\chi})

between the two models given by

\Phi_{\overline{t}}(f)(g)=f(\overline{t}^{-1}g).

Let
f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}\in{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}({}^{t}\tilde{\chi})
be the
(K,s_{K}^{\prime})
-unramified vector, which gives
\Phi_{\overline{t}}^{-1}(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}})\in{\rm Ind}_{\overline{A}U}^{\overline{G}}(\tilde{\chi})
. We want to compute the number
c\in\mathbf{C}
such that

T(w,\tilde{\chi};r_{w_{\alpha}}^{\rm un})\big(\Phi_{\overline{t}}^{-1}(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}})\big)=c\cdot\Phi_{\overline{t}}^{-1}(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}).

We have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I(\tilde{\chi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I({}^{w}\tilde{\chi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I(^{t}\tilde{\chi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I(^{w}(^{t}\tilde{\chi}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I(^{t}(^{w}\tilde{\chi})),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(w,\tilde{\chi})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{\overline{t}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{wtw^{-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{\overline{t}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(w,{}^{t}\tilde{\chi})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{twt^{-1}w^{-1}}}

where

{}^{w}\tilde{\chi}=\tilde{\chi}.

Let
c(w,{}^{t}\tilde{\chi})\in\mathbf{C}
be the number such that

\Phi_{\overline{t}}\circ T(w,\tilde{\chi})\circ\Phi_{\overline{t}}^{-1}(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}})=c(w,{}^{t}\tilde{\chi})\cdot f_{s_{K}^{\prime}}.

Thus, we get

c(w,{}^{t}\tilde{\chi})=T(w,{}^{t}\tilde{\chi})(f_{s_{K}^{\prime}})(wtw^{-1}t^{-1}).

Now we see that

wtw^{-1}t^{-1}=w_{r-1}\cdot\overline{me_{r}(\varpi)}\cdot w_{r-1}^{-1}\cdot\overline{me_{r}(\varpi)}^{-1}=\overline{h}_{\alpha_{r-1}}(\varpi^{m})\in Z(\overline{T}).

It then follows that

c(w,{}^{t}\tilde{\chi})={}^{w}\chi(\overline{h}_{\alpha_{r-1}}(\varpi^{m}))\cdot c_{\sf gk}(w,\chi)=(-1)\cdot c_{\sf gk}(w,\chi).

This shows that the eigenvalue of

\mathcal{A}(w,\chi):I(\tilde{\chi})\longrightarrow I(\tilde{\chi})

is equal to
-1
on the
(K,s_{K}^{\prime})
-normalized unramified vector
\Phi_{t}^{-1}(f_{s_{k}^{\prime}})\in I(\tilde{\chi})
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Regarding Conjecture
5.4
, we have
\widehat{\Gamma_{G}^{\rm tor}}=\left\{1\right\}
and
\widehat{\Gamma_{G_{Q,n}}^{\rm tor}}=\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}
, where the nontrivial element is equal to
\varphi\circ h(me_{r})
with the function
\varphi\circ h
given as in (
5.7
). Thus, we see that Proposition
6.3
implies Conjecture
5.4
for
z=me_{r}
(while
y=1
). It is not hard to verify Conjecture
5.4
for any other
z^{\prime}\in{\rm Hom}(K,\mu)^{\natural}
. Thus, Conjecture
5.4
holds for even fold cover of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
, where
r
is also even.

On the other hand, the hypothesis of Conjecture
5.7
is not satisfied for such cover of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
. We analyze the Whittaker dimension
\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\rho))
as follows. It follows from the analysis in §
4.4
that

I(\omega_{0,0})\simeq I(\omega_{0,1})

since
\omega_{0,1}={}^{w}\omega_{0,0}
, and that these two principal series are both
(K_{0},s_{0})
-unramified. On the other hand, we have

I(\omega_{e_{0},0})\simeq I(\omega_{e_{0},1}),

which are both
(K_{0}^{\prime},s_{0}^{\prime})
-unramified. Note that Theorem
4.12
implies that
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})|_{\overline{G}_{0}}
and
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}
both contain
n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{0,0})
. Since
I(\chi)
is also
(K_{e_{0}},e_{0}\cdot s_{K})
-unramified, it is easy to see that
I(\omega_{e_{0},i})
is
(K_{0}^{\prime},s_{0}^{\prime})
-unramified. Theorem
4.12
then also implies
n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{e_{0},0})
is contained in both
\pi(\mathbbm{1})
and
\pi(\varepsilon)
. Thus, as representations of
\overline{G}_{0}
, we have

\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})|_{\overline{G}_{0}}\simeq\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}\simeq n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{0,0})\bigoplus n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{e_{0},0})\simeq n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{0,1})\bigoplus n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{e_{0},1}).

For every nondegenerate
\psi
, we have

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1}))=\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon))=2m^{r}\cdot n_{(r)}.

We give a summary of this as follows.

Theorem 6.4
.

For
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{(n)}
with
n
and
r
both even, a unitary
(K,s_{K})
-unramified principal series
I(\chi)
has the decomposition

I(\chi)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}=n_{(r)}\cdot(I(\omega_{0,0})\oplus I(\omega_{0,1}))\bigoplus n_{(r)}\cdot(I(\omega_{e_{0},0})\oplus I(\omega_{e_{0},1})),

where
I(\omega_{0,j})
is
(K_{0},s_{0})
-unramified and
I(\omega_{e_{0},j})
is
(K_{0}^{\prime},s_{0}^{\prime})
-unramified. Here every
I(\omega_{\gamma,j})
is irreducible. If
I(\chi)=\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})\oplus\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon)
is reducible, then

\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})|_{\overline{G}_{0}}\simeq\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}\simeq n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{0,0})\bigoplus n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{e_{0},0})\simeq n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{0,1})\bigoplus n_{(r)}\cdot I(\omega_{e_{0},1}).

Regarding the Whittaker dimension, one has

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(I(\omega_{\gamma,j}))=m^{r}

for every non-degenerate
\psi
.

Example 6.5
.

Consider
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{4}^{(4)}
, i.e.,
n=4
and
r=2
. In this case,
n_{(r)}=2
. We have

\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\Gamma}=\left\{0,e_{c},e_{0},e_{c}+e_{0}\right\}\simeq\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}\times\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}.

and

\mathscr{X}_{Q,n}^{\mathfrak{c}}=\left\{0,e_{c}\right\}.

On the other hand, we see

(Y/Y_{Q,n})_{K}^{\natural}=\left\{0,2e_{2},e_{c},e_{c}-2e_{2}\right\}\simeq\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}\times\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}

with

p_{\Gamma}^{-1}(0)=\left\{0,2e_{2}\right\}\text{ and }p_{\Gamma}^{-1}(e_{c})=\left\{e_{c},e_{c}-2e_{2}\right\}.

When the
(K,s_{K}
)-unramified
I(\chi)
is reducible, we have

\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})|_{\overline{G}_{0}}\simeq\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon)|_{\overline{G}_{0}}\simeq 2\cdot I(\omega_{0,0})\bigoplus 2\cdot I(\omega_{e_{0},0})\simeq 2\cdot I(\omega_{0,1})\bigoplus 2\cdot I(\omega_{e_{0},1})

with

\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1}))=\dim{\rm Wh}_{\psi}(\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon))=16.

Assume
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\mathbbm{1})
is
(K,s_{K})
-unramified, then it is also
(K,f_{e_{c}}\otimes s_{K})
-unramified, and that
\pi(\phi_{\chi},\varepsilon)
is both
(K,f_{2e_{2}}\otimes s_{K})
and
(K,f_{e_{c}-2e_{2}}\otimes s_{K})
-unramified.

Remark 6.6
.

Here we observe again that covers satisfying

\overline{G}^{\vee}\simeq G_{ad}

seem to exhibit quite special properties. For instance, they do not satisfy the hypothesis of Conjecture
5.7
. One such example here is the even fold cover of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
with
r
being even. A previous example, as noted in Remark
5.8
, is the
n
-fold cover of
{\rm Spin}_{2r+1}
with
r
odd and
n_{\alpha}\equiv 2\mod 4
for every short coroot
\alpha^{\vee}
. The
n
-fold cover of
{\rm SO}_{3}
studied in §
5.4.2
, if
4|n
, is also of such type.

Remark 6.7
.

For the double cover
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{(2)}
of
{\rm GSp}_{2r}
, many results in this section are already proved in
[
Szp5
]
, even without assuming the tame condition
p\nmid n
. Indeed, the restriction problem for general genuine principal series of
\overline{{\rm GSp}}_{2r}^{(2)}
is analyzed systematically in loc. cit. by utilizing a natural maximal abelian subgroup
\overline{A}\subset\overline{T}
chosen independent of the residual characteristic
p
of
F
, see
[
Szp5
, Lemma 2.1]
.
